# Theme — Optical / CPO (Co-Packaged Optics & AI Interconnect)

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-01 — TWO THINGS THIS PAGE DID NOT HAVE: **A QUANTIFIED TEST-CAPACITY BOTTLENECK (FiconTEC: 1,000 SYSTEMS IN 1-1.5 YEARS vs 2,000 IN THE LAST 25 YEARS)** AND A **NAMED DISSENT ON THE CPO ADOPTION DATE FROM A PARTICIPANT** (Lightmatter: NPO through 2027, CPO only later 2028)

**(MS, relayed by MS desk · Tom Wigg, 2026-09-01, on [[LITE]] / [[COHR]] / [[CIEN]] / [[GLW]] / GFS / SOI)**

- 🔴🔴 **THE BOTTLENECK IS TESTING, AND IT IS NOW A NUMBER RATHER THAN AN ADJECTIVE: *"**TESTING IS THE MAIN CHALLENGE for SiPho rollout**, per test system vendors ([[ADVANTEST]]), but also **foundries and OSATs. FiconTEC (supplies SiPho testing systems) will ship 1,000 SYSTEMS IN THE NEXT 1-1.5 YEARS (vs 2,000 IN THE LAST 25 YEARS).**"*
- ➤ 🔴🔴 **THAT RATIO IS THE FINDING: a merchant test-equipment supplier is about to ship ~half its cumulative 25-year installed base in 12-18 months. It is the most concrete capacity-ramp datapoint on this page, and it independently confirms what [[TSM]]'s own packaging head said at SEMICON Taiwan the same week — *"**foundry CAN scale up optical engines, but THE REAL BOTTLENECK for large-scale deployment lies in OTHER PARTS OF THE SUPPLY CHAIN, including LASERS, OPTICAL FIBERS, FIBER CONNECTORS, AND PRODUCT TESTING**"* (K.C. Hsu). **Two independent sources, one conclusion: the constraint is downstream of the foundry, and TESTING is on both lists.** ⚠️ **What the datapoint does NOT tell you is whether FiconTEC is capacity-constrained itself, or what share of SiPh test it holds — so it sizes the RAMP, not the shortage.**
- ⚠️🔴 **AND THE ADOPTION-CURVE DISSENT, WHICH IS THE MOST USEFUL DISAGREEMENT ON THIS PAGE RIGHT NOW: *"**Lightmatter suggested NPO IN USE THROUGH 2027 and CPO MIGRATION LATER 2028.**"* **Against TSMC: *"some producers will **MASS PRODUCE CPO in 2H26. TSMC is on track to enter production of CPO in 2H26**"*, and **SiPh *"will become MAINSTREAM IN 2027, with market share topping 50%"***. **And against Imec, which sides with TSMC: *"**Co-packaged optics is being adopted FASTER than originally expected.** I've seen market reports that were showing very moderate and gradual adoption, moving into 2030 when it could mean something in terms of markets"* (Imec VP Philippe Absil).** ➤ 🔴 **HOLD THE SPREAD RATHER THAN RESOLVING IT, AND NOTE WHO IS SAYING WHAT: the SUPPLIERS of the technology (TSMC, Imec) say faster; a PARTICIPANT building the systems (Lightmatter) says NPO holds through 2027 and CPO volume is a 2028+ event. **A vendor's production date and a customer's deployment date are different objects, and the gap between them is roughly ONE GENERATION.** For the equities that gap is the whole timing question — it decides whether CPO revenue lands in FY27 or FY28 estimates. ✅ **A useful third mark from the same window: Jefferies' Taiwan strategy note has *"[[NVDA]] having HALVED the HBM capacity for its next-generation GPUs, [yet] **overall RACK VALUE is expected to RISE through NPO and CPO scale-up**"* — i.e. the NPO rung is already monetising.**
- **THE COUPE LADDER, RESTATED WITH THE FULL BANDWIDTH TARGETS BECAUSE THEY BOUND THE TAM (TSMC at SEMICON Taiwan, via Jefferies Asia · Conor O'Mara and @QQ_Timmy):** *"'**Fast and Narrow**' [takes] per-channel speeds from **200 Gbps to more than 400 Gbps** while expanding channel counts **from 16 to over 128**, lifting total bandwidth **from 3.2 Tbps to more than 12.8 Tbps**; '**Slow and Wide**' [expands] WDM from a single wavelength to **4, 8, 16, and beyond**."* **The loss physics: *"its **2–4-micron bonding pitch limits transmission loss at 112 Gbps to just 0.06 dB, compared with 1.38 dB for MICRO-BUMPS**, helping reduce power consumption and latency to **10–20 nanoseconds**"*, and *"**more than 10 TIMES the power efficiency** compared with traditional copper-wire-based applications."* **The two architectures: CPO (engine on substrate) vs **OOI (optical interposer, engine adjacent to the XPU)**, with *"power efficiency ~**4-10x** better and latency ~**10-20x** lower."* **Manufacturability, which is the underrated leg: *"TSMC has enabled **WAFER-LEVEL OPTICAL TESTING for edge-coupled devices, allowing DEFECTIVE DIES TO BE SCREENED OUT BEFORE COSTLY CPO ASSEMBLY**"*, plus an **optical PDK** for electronic-photonic co-simulation that *"**supports [[CDNS]] and [[SNPS]] tools**"* — a named, dated EDA hook. **Forward: modulators, SOA and light sources may mount directly on the BACK of COUPE; COUPE usable as an optical interposer up to **~3.3x reticle**, later combined with CoWoS, supporting CWDM/DWDM.**
- **AND THE MARKET-SIZE MARKS FROM THE SAME SESSION: *"Sales of optical transceivers in 2025 will grow by **25%** and **50% in 2026**. The **100G-and-above speed market will grow 60% in 2025.**"* ⚠️ **Note the 2025 figures are stated in the future tense in the relay — a translation artifact. Treat the 2026 +50% as the usable forward mark.**
- 🔴 **THE LASER LAYER IS CONTRACTING LONG AND TIGHT, AND THE CORROBORATION COMES FROM THE BUYERS' OWN ANALYST: JPM · Joe Cardoso, previewing [[CIEN]], cites *"**recent MULTI-YEAR LTAs FOR PUMP LASERS signed by [[COHR]] and [[LITE]] [as] suggesting VOLUME GUARANTEES at BETTER-THAN-FEARED PRICING**, which should ease supply/cost concerns for system vendors like CIEN"* (2026-09-01). ➤ **Two independent sources this week say lasers are the scarce layer (TSMC naming them FIRST among the bottlenecks) and that they are being contracted multi-year. That is a pricing-power argument for LITE/COHR and a cost-certainty argument for CIEN — the same fact, read from both sides of the invoice. ⚠️ But note the tension with this page's standing framing: an LTA that gives the SELLER volume also gives the BUYER a price CEILING. Which side wins depends on where spot goes relative to the contract, and no source in this window gives the LTA price.**
- **NEW COVERAGE ON THE WHOLE COMPLEX: **DB (Gianmarco Conti) initiated the hardware sector 2026-09-01 with BUY on [[ANET]], [[COHR]], [[CSCO]], [[HPE]] and [[LITE]]**, and Hold on [[DELL]] (PT $480), ATEN and INGM. ⚠️ **PTs for the Buy-rated names were NOT carried in any of the three relays as delivered (Barclays desk, BofA competitor screens, UBS) — OPEN ITEM.** ➤ **The SHAPE is the read: Buy the components and networking, Hold the system integrators — the same "value accrues to the constrained layer, not the assembler" thesis this wiki has been building. Per the DELL page, DB *"initiates BULLISH ON THE DEMAND AND BEARISH ON THE MARGIN, and prefers HPE."***
- ⚠️🔴 **AND THE TAPE DID THE OPPOSITE OF ALL OF IT: on 2026-09-01, *"**Optical −3.9%** ([[CRDO]] −8.6%, [[CIEN]] −6%, [[LITE]] −5%, [[ALAB]] −6% all underperformed)"* — the second-worst factor in tech behind Bitcoin-related, on a *"broad low liquidity DE-GROSSING day"* with QQQ −1.3%, Semis/Software −2%, Security SW −5.3% and the EDA pair [[CDNS]] −7.6% / [[SNPS]] −5.6% (TMTB EOD Wrap). **Macro: the 10Y hit 4.792%, its highest since January 2025; 30Y Treasuries on their worst run since 2006; 10Y JGB through 3% (a 30-year high); the market pricing 40bps of HIKES vs 25bps pre-Jackson Hole.** ➤ **CRDO's failure to raise its $600m FY27 optical guide was the idiosyncratic trigger — see [[CRDO]] — but the complex fell together on rates. Recorded explicitly: the strongest dated CPO/SiPh disclosures of the year, a quantified test-capacity ramp, and a fresh DB Buy initiation all landed on the day the group was sold hardest.**

## 🆕🔭 Recent signals (Sep 1, /newsletters-to-vault — **DAMNANG PUBLISHES THE CALL AND THEN ARGUES AGAINST THE NAIVE VERSION OF IT: "BANDWIDTH GROWTH IS NOT THE SAME THESIS AS OPTICAL REVENUE GROWTH"**)

**DAMNANG (Substack)** — *"The AI Investment Map: From Tokens to Megawatts"*, **2026-09-01**. ⚠️ **INDEPENDENT SUBSTACK AUTHOR (damnang2.substack.com), NOT A BROKER — no rating, no price target, no house model.** Korean ex-Qualcomm engineer. **His own disclaimer, verbatim: *"This note is for information only and is not a recommendation to buy or sell any security… Third-party estimates vary by source, so they are better read for direction and structure than for absolute levels."*** He frames both calls as a **base case that changes if the conditions change**, and publishes an explicit falsification test for each. Same publisher as the March "War of Light" and the 08-20 HBM-density pieces already on this page — see the 📌 STANDING SOURCE NOTE below. [Source](../../Damnang/2026-09-01%20-%20The%20AI%20Investment%20Map-%20From%20Tokens%20to%20Megawatts.md)

🔴🔴 **HIS CALL #2, STATED AS A BASE CASE: *"Data movement takes a larger share of AI system economics through 2028."*** Mechanism is the one this page already runs — cluster size growing, **copper reach shortening as electrical lane speed rises**, larger scale-up domains — with the OIF timeline as the anchor: *"OIF released the **CEI-448G framework document in November 2025** and launched the **CEI-448G-VSR and LR projects in the first quarter of 2026**. The open question is not whether 448G electrical is feasible. It is **how far electrical reach can hold in the generation after that**."*

⚠️⚠️ **AND THEN THE PART THAT MAKES THIS WORTH LOGGING RATHER THAN JUST ANOTHER OPTICS BULL NOTE — HE REFUSES THE READ-ACROSS: *"**This should not be read as a simple optics bull case.** If CPO is adopted quickly, the same bandwidth can be delivered with **lower optical dollar content**."* And, twice more: *"**Bandwidth growth is not the same as optical revenue growth**"* · *"**Being long optics does not mean being long every optics company**… Within the same optical upcycle, growth and margin trajectories differ sharply by supplier."* ➤ **This is the CPO debate re-expressed as a BOM-content question rather than a timing question, and it is the cleanest statement on this page of why the CPO date and the optics P&L are two different bets.**

### 🔴 THE STRUCTURAL CLAIM: THE BOTTLENECK SITS **ABOVE** THE MODULE, AND THE SUPPLIER COUNT COLLAPSES AS YOU GO UP

*"Part of today’s optical scarcity shows up in **upstream components rather than the complete transceiver**… Moving up from the transceiver, the chain runs **module assembly → laser chip → epitaxy → InP substrate**. Effective suppliers thin out and substitution gets harder with each step up… **The supply numbers get worse the further up you go**."* The counts he gives:

| Layer | Effective supplier set (his figures) |
|---|---|
| Module assembly | **dozens** of competitors; **7 of the top 10 are Chinese** |
| Laser chip | **fewer than five firms at volume**; **200G/lane EML** (what 1.6T needs) narrower still |
| Epitaxy | in-house at the laser makers or a **small set of specialist foundries**; **MOCVD tools export-controlled**, so expansion is gated by tool lead times |
| InP substrate | **three firms hold 80-90%** |

➤ **His conclusion, which maps directly onto this page’s "who captures the optical dollar" debate: the qualified-supplier test *"actually binds… **at the substrate and the light source, not at the module**."* ✅ **Corroborating marks he cites:** [[LITE]] **pump laser shipments +80% y/y** and **200G EML pricing above the prior generation**; **EML lead times beyond 2027** (TrendForce); **800G transceiver production 40-60% below demand through 2027**, **1.6T 30-40% short through 2029** (consulting estimates cited in public reporting); and the contract proof — the [[AXTI]]–[[LITE]] **InP supply-and-capacity-reservation agreement through end-2031, $43.5m deposit plus a second $43.5m settled in 2028** (AXT 8-K, 2026-07-29) — *"Buying several years of capacity in advance is what happens when the part cannot be sourced on demand."*

⚠️ **ONE TECHNICAL DISTINCTION HE INSISTS ON, RELEVANT TO THE SiPh LEG OF THIS PAGE: *"The chain above also splits by light source. **EML and CW lasers sit on InP** and ride this chain directly. **VCSEL sits on GaAs** and follows a different one. **Moving to silicon photonics does not remove the need for a light source, so InP demand does not disappear**."* ➤ **i.e. a CPO/SiPh transition is a threat to MODULE and transceiver dollars, not to the InP/laser layer — which is exactly the split this page has been trying to price.**

⚠️ **SCALE-UP vs SCALE-OUT, and he warns against the rack shorthand: *"Scale-up and scale-out should not be divided by the rack boundary. **Scale-up is the fabric inside a tightly coupled accelerator domain, and that domain can extend across multiple racks**. Scale-out connects multiple scale-up domains or clusters."* Economics differ: *"**Scale-out is Ethernet-centric with a broad supplier set. Scale-up ties protocol and architecture more deeply to a compute platform.** Switching cost and supplier concentration are generally higher on the scale-up side."*

⚠️ **WHAT WOULD BREAK HIS OWN CALL (he publishes it): *"**scale-up domain size flattening for two consecutive generations**, **448G-class electrical connectivity achieving longer reach than expected** and delaying optical penetration, or **CPO cost reduction moving so fast that bandwidth rises while optical dollars per system fall**."* **What he tracks: interconnect+optics BOM / total system BOM · transceiver count per rack · scale-up domain size · optical component capacity converting into shipments.** ➤ **Nothing here dates the CPO transition — it does not supersede the three-rung ladder or the NPO/CPO timing rows above; it prices the CONSEQUENCE of either branch.**

### 🆕🔭 THE SECOND PUBLICATION IN THE SAME 09-01 /newsletters-to-vault LANE — **FUNDAAI PUTS A FOURTH TIER *INSIDE* THE TRAY AND SIZES IT AT ~10x SCALE-UP / ~100x SCALE-OUT, THEN REFUSES TO PUT A DOLLAR ON IT**

🔭 **FundaAI (Substack)** — *"Deep|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray, and the Bandwidth Opportunity Could 10x Scale-Up"*, **2026-09-01** ⚠️ **INDEPENDENT SUBSTACK PUBLISHER, NOT A BROKER — no rating, no price target, no house model**. **This page already runs scale-out → scale-up → scale-across. The note adds the layer INWARD of all three and defines it narrowly: *"scale-in refers to **optical links inside the tray** that serve high-bandwidth connectivity between memory and between GPUs... **die-to-die** connections between chiplets and compute die, and **chip-to-memory** connections such as **XPU to HBM**, over reaches from **millimeters to about one meter**."* Links from the XPU to a pooled memory pool are explicitly held OUT of the definition and treated as an adjacent pooled-memory fabric.** ⚠️ **This page already carried the TERM from [[LITE]] via Barclays (08-25, rendered as *"memory disaggregation within systems"*) and one @jukan05 corpus line (*"at least for scale-in, it seems fair to assume that CPO will eventually be replaced"*, 08-31). What it did not have is the definition, the reach, the ratios or the taxonomy conflict.** (FundaAI "Deep|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray...", 2026-09-01)

🔴🔴 **THE BANDWIDTH LADDER, WHICH IS THE NET-NEW CONTENT AND THE FIRST TIME THIS PAGE HAS ALL FOUR RUNGS ON ONE SCALE — all quoted ONE WAY, per the note’s own stated convention (a byte is eight bits, so 1 TB/s = 8 Tb/s, and a link can be quoted one way or both ways):**

| Tier (on [[NVDA]] Rubin public specs) | Bandwidth per GPU, one way | Step |
|---|---|---|
| Scale-out (Ethernet via ConnectX-9) | **~1.6 Tb/s** | — |
| Scale-up (NVLink 6, 1.8 TB/s per direction) | **14.4 Tb/s** | **~9x scale-out** |
| Pooled-memory fabric (the adjacent tier) | **35-70 Tb/s** ⚠️ *publisher ASSUMPTION: an external pool taking 20-40% of local HBM traffic* | several times NVLink per direction |
| Local HBM4 (22 TB/s) | **176 Tb/s** | **~another 10x** |

➤ **The note’s conclusion: *"Each step inward is about 10x, and end-to-end the span is about 100x. Scale-in, which targets memory-class bandwidth, runs roughly an order of magnitude above scale-up per GPU, about 100x scale-out."*** ⚠️⚠️ **TWO GUARDRAILS THE PUBLISHER SETS HIMSELF AND THIS PAGE MUST KEEP: (1) *"The thesis does not require optics to displace the HBM PHY, and **local HBM stays electrical for as far as we can see**. We use the 176 Tb/s figure ONLY AS A COMPARISON POINT, and the opportunity for optics is the memory traffic that no longer fits inside the package."* **The 176 Tb/s is NOT an addressable optical number — do not chain it into any TAM bridge on this page.** (2) **NO DOLLAR TAM, BY DESIGN: *"This note sizes bandwidth only since per-lane and per-port pricing for in-tray optics is not yet set, and a dollar TAM would imply precision that does not exist."*** The only dollar figures he will attach come from the adjacent tiers — [[MRVL]]’s FY28 scale-up-optics guidance and the **$120bn** qualifying-revenue ceiling in the [[GOOG]] warrant, **both of which this page already carries**. ✅ **Against the 08-25 [[LITE]]/Barclays TAM of ~$100bn at 45-50% CAGR that this page holds, the note adds NO competing TAM and therefore supersedes nothing.** (FundaAI "Deep|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray...", 2026-09-01)

🔴 **THE TAXONOMY CONFLICT, AND IT IS A READING HAZARD FOR EVERY FUTURE SOURCE ON THIS PAGE: *"Scale-in now has at least three different technical meanings."* [[NVDA]] at Hot Chips 2026 uses **Scale-In** for a **BlueField-4 DPU per-node infrastructure network** (agentic CPU capacity, storage, security, orchestration) quantified at **800 Gb/s per Vera Rubin compute tray plus 4x1.6 Tb/s of scale-out** — and calls its pooled-memory tier **Context Memory (CMX)**, NOT scale-in. [[META]]’s MTIA 400 deck uses *"Scale-in DMA & streaming reductions"* for **electrical** chiplet-to-chiplet movement inside the package. Azure has long used scale-in for **removing VM instances in autoscaling**.** ➤ **Practical rule for this page: a source using the word "scale-in" proves nothing about which tier it means — check the reach before routing it. The optics-and-memory camp (Marvell "scale-inside", Avicena, Keysight, Eliyan, LightXcelerate) means the tray; NVIDIA does not.** (FundaAI "Deep|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray...", 2026-09-01)

🔴 **THE TECHNOLOGY FORK AT IN-TRAY REACH — AND IT CUTS AGAINST THE InP-SCARCITY TRADE THIS PAGE RUNS ON. Entry bar, attributed to [[LITE]]’s CTO at ECOC 2025: *"parallel signaling, very high volumes, about **$0.10 per Gbps**, under **4 pJ/bit**, under **1 FIT**, and over **2 Tbps/mm** of density. **Silicon photonics and VCSELs are the leading candidates at this tier, while InP and TFLN fall short on density and power.**"*** ⚠️ **That is the opposite routing from the substrate/light-source bottleneck argument DAMNANG makes in the block above (module → laser chip → epitaxy → InP substrate, *"three firms hold 80-90%"*). **Both are logged, neither is resolved: DAMNANG is describing TODAY’S pluggable/CPO chain; FundaAI is describing a 2029-30+ in-tray tier. They are not the same chain and must not be netted.** **The three named paths, in the note’s own split: (1) **SiPh / DWDM** — highest aggregate bandwidth and longest reach; Ayar Labs 8 Tbps optical-I/O chiplet; **Lightmatter Passage M1000 at 114 Tbps through a 3D photonic interposer** (new figure for this page, which carries Passage as the named CPO threat via Jefferies 08-31); [[MRVL]]’s Photonic Fabric plus **Polariton** plasmonic modulators at *"near 10 µm against 3,000 to 5,000 µm for conventional silicon photonics and bandwidth demonstrated past 1 THz."* (2) **VCSEL / micro-VCSEL** — array parallelism, low cost, mature base; [[COHR]] ships 2D VCSEL and photodiode arrays and has published a **wide-and-slow CPO architecture**; [[LITE]] demonstrated a **1060 nm monolithic 2D VCSEL-array platform** and is pushing 1060 nm through the IEEE 802.3 200G-per-lane multimode study group; **LightXcelerate** (Palo Alto, seed, founded 2025) shows a **19-core multi-core-fibre chiplet with back-emitting micro-VCSEL arrays at 10 Tbps/mm of edge density and under 2 pJ/bit end-to-end** (OFC 2026). (3) **MicroLED** — highest density for the shortest links, no laser needed; **Avicena** LightBundle targets XPU-to-HBM directly, has demonstrated **200 fJ/bit** transmitters and moved evaluation kits from **~0.5-0.9 Tbps in March 2026 to 1 Tbps in August**, with **[[SAMSUNG]], [[MU]] and [[SKHYNIX]] all holding stakes**; **[[CRDO]] acquired Hyperlume for about $92 million** (new to this page); **[[MSFT]]’s MOSAIC programme targets commercial microLED cables by late 2027.** (FundaAI "Deep|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray...", 2026-09-01)

⚠️ **THE TIMING, WHICH IS THE BEAR HALF AND SHOULD BE READ BEFORE THE RATIOS: *"Scale-up optics ships starting in 2027 while **scale-in follows two to three years later, if not more**, because those same parts still have to clear tighter thermal, reliability, test, and other requirements within the tray."* And the harder line, from the note’s own risk section: *"**no Hot Chips 2026 deck used scale-in for die-to-die optics**"* — today’s die-to-die and die-to-memory links are ELECTRICAL and standardising that way ([[SAMSUNG]] custom HBM replacing the standard HBM PHY with a die-to-die interface on an advanced logic base die; [[MU]]’s tutorial listing a memory-optimised SerDes die-to-die PHY and CPO side by side; **Eliyan**’s UMI becoming an open OCP standard). [[NVDA]] *"argues an all-copper scale-up rack improves throughput per watt"* and [[AMD]]’s MI400 materials read *"nearing optical transition."*** ➤ **Net for this page: scale-in is a 2029-30+ architecture call with NO dollar attached, from a publisher with no model. It corroborates the DIRECTION of the electrical-to-optical boundary moving inward that this page already runs, and it changes no CPO/NPO date, no TAM, no content estimate and no rating.** (FundaAI "Deep|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray...", 2026-09-01)

✅ **CROSS-CHECKED AGAINST WHAT THIS PAGE ALREADY HOLDS, SO NOTHING IS DOUBLE-COUNTED: the **$2bn each from [[NVDA]] into [[LITE]] and [[COHR]]** (March 2026, multi-year purchase commitments), the **$3.25bn [[MRVL]] / Celestial AI** price, and the **$120bn** [[GOOG]]-warrant ceiling are ALL already on this page from primaries or broker notes — FundaAI’s figures MATCH and are logged as independent restatement, not as new capital. ⚠️ **ONE DATED DISCREPANCY WORTH FLAGGING RATHER THAN FIXING: the note describes *"NVIDIA’s announced Rubin Ultra specification puts **1 TB of HBM4E** in each package for the **Kyber rack generation planned for 2027**"*, with August-2026 reports of testing **192-256 GB** configurations. **This page carries Kyber as CANCELLED (08-18) or PUSHED OUT (08-20) — an unresolved conflict already logged — and `themes/hbm-memory` holds the Rubin Ultra de-spec as a live multi-mark debate (192GB / 256GB / 288GB / 384GB against the originally previewed 1TB). FundaAI’s 192-256GB band CORROBORATES the low end of that debate; his *"Kyber planned for 2027"* framing does NOT reflect the cancellation this page carries. Flagged, not resolved.** (FundaAI "Deep|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray...", 2026-09-01)

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-02 — THE LARGEST OPTICAL-DSP VENDOR SAYS ITS OWN LASER CAPACITY IS THE CONSTRAINT AND IS DOUBLING IT WITH CAPEX; AND [[CRDO]] DISCLOSES **INITIAL NPO DESIGN WINS** PLUS ITS FIRST SILICON-PHOTONICS IC REVENUE

- 🔴🔴 **[[AVGO]] — THE CAPEX LINE WENT UP AND THE REASON IS LASERS.** Asked directly on the Q3 FY26 call whether rising capex was for EML, CW and indium phosphide: *"**We are investing in factories.** We talked about substrates — that's in production. **We are investing in EML, CW and VCSEL in the US AND Singapore. We have ALREADY DOUBLED and will increase even more — that's what you are seeing in capex. Demand for lasers is HUGE; CW and EML is FAR SURPASSING SUPPLY. We are doubling down on capacity in this area. We have a LARGE SHARE of this market.**"* In prepared remarks: *"**leader in optical DSP, rapidly expanding capacity in EML and CW lasers and VCSELs**"* (Jefferies · Conor O'Mara, verbatim call notes, 2026-09-02).
  - ➤ ✅ **THIS IS DIRECT CORROBORATION OF THIS PAGE'S CENTRAL SUPPLY CALL, FROM THE BIGGEST INCUMBENT. It also confirms the [[LITE]] characterisation logged 08-27 — that 200G EML is effectively a Lumentum/Broadcom duopoly with a ~2x ASP uplift — because Broadcom is now saying it holds *"a large share"* and that demand *"far surpasses supply."*** ➤ 🔴 **READ-THROUGH IN BOTH DIRECTIONS: near term it is bullish for laser pricing across [[LITE]], [[COHR]] and the CW/EML chain (a duopolist adding capacity because it cannot serve demand); medium term it is a CAPACITY ADD by the largest player into a tight market, which is how tight markets end. Date the capacity: US + Singapore, "already doubled," increasing further.**
  - **And the switching roadmap it feeds:** **Tomahawk 6 (100T) sold out**, **Tomahawk 7 (200T) just taped out**, **Tomahawk Ultra** shipping this quarter for **low-latency Ethernet scale-up** — *"enabling scaling up clusters of XPU **and GPU** on Ethernet for the first time… it will perform just as well as any other solution on loss and latency."* **AI networking +2.5x in Q3, tripling in Q4, and *"expected to grow just as fast as XPU"*** — Broadcom has retired its own "~40% of AI revenue is the ceiling" framing.
- 🔴 **[[CRDO]] — NPO DESIGN WINS ARE NET-NEW TO THIS WIKI, AND THEY COME FROM THE COVERING ANALYST'S LOOK-THROUGH LIST.** JPM's F1Q27 review (Joseph Cardoso, Overweight, PT $335 → $310, 2026-09-02) names the FY28 optionality management highlighted: *"increasing and strong engagements across **ALC and Omniconnect**, as well as **a NEW OPPORTUNITY in the form of NPO, where the company noted INITIAL DESIGN WINS** — all of which we expect is **yet to be fully underwritten in sell-side and buy-side forecasts alike**."* ➤ **Add Credo to the NPO participant list this page tracks. It is consistent with the three-rung CPO/NPO ladder logged 08-31 (NPO fills the gap before CPO lands 2H C28+) and with [[ALAB]]'s independent vote on NPO-before-CPO timing.**
- **[[CRDO]] — AND THE SiPh MILESTONE:** *"optical DSPs reached a **record** and CRDO recognised its **FIRST SILICON PHOTONICS IC REVENUE following the DustPhotonics acquisition**"*; F27 optical is guided to **$600m+ with ZF Optics, SiPho PICs and optical DSPs EACH contributing $100m+**, and *"**qualification at a SECOND HYPERSCALER remains another meaningful source of potential upside**"* (Jefferies · **Blayne Curtis**, BUY, PT $270, 2026-09-02). ⚠️ **The $600m+ guide did NOT move despite JPM having flagged $700m+ as its estimate driver #1 — the single line that cost CRDO ~10% on the print. Optical is *"more back-half loaded in F27"* and *"should exit the FY at a higher level, providing a strong base for F28 growth."***
- **Also on the copper side, because it bounds the optical TAM:** CRDO reported **record retimer revenue driven by copper-based SCALE-UP deployments** using **Screaming Eagle at 100G/lane** with **initial Blue Heron at 200G/lane**, and **ALCs extending cable reach to 30m** (Jefferies, 2026-09-02). ➤ **Copper is still winning inside the rack at 100-200G/lane; that is the boundary condition the optical scale-up thesis has to push against.**



## 🆕🔴🔴 Recent signals (Aug 31, /run-inbox — **A NEW HOUSE INITIATES THE WHOLE SECTOR AND MAKES THE ARCHITECTURE-AGNOSTIC ARGUMENT THE CENTRAL CALL: *"WHICHEVER ARCHITECTURE WINS… THE LASER IS STILL NEEDED. SILICON CANNOT LASE."* AND TSMC PUTS ENGINEERING NUMBERS ON THE I/O WALL AT SEMICON.**)

**Sources: (1) Deutsche Bank · Gianmarco Conti — "Necessity is the mother of invention — Initiating on the Hardware sector", 174pp INITIATION of coverage on 8 names, 2026-08-31** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/883478bc_729b_4d0d_a75e_f4962a9e3e10_604.html) · **(2) Wells Fargo · Aaron Rakers — "MRVL Purchase Commits Expansion, DRAM Contract Pricing, CXMT 2Q26 Results, & More" (headlines flash), 2026-08-31** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/MRVL_Purchase_Commits_Expansion_DRAM_Contract_Pricing_CXMT.html)

🔴 **THE INITIATION AND ITS RATINGS — the first time this dossier can carry a full sector-wide rating set from one house on one day:** DB initiates **5 Buys — [[LITE]] PT $1,200, [[COHR]] PT $400, [[ANET]] PT $220, [[CSCO]] PT $135, [[HPE]] PT $62 — and 3 Holds — [[DELL]] PT $480, A10 Networks PT $28, Ingram Micro PT $30.** **LITE and COHR are named the two *"highest conviction Buys."***

🔴 **THE CENTRAL ARGUMENT, AND IT IS THE STRONGEST STATEMENT OF THE LASER THESIS THIS PAGE CARRIES:** *"The critical conclusion is that **whichever architecture wins (pluggables, LPO, NPO or CPO) the laser is still needed. SILICON CANNOT LASE.** That is why Lumentum and Coherent are our highest conviction Buys."* On LITE specifically: *"it wins under every architecture. If pluggables persist, it sells EMLs, if CPO takes off, it sells CW lasers and the ELS, if NPO wins it sells the same laser families into a different package."*

⚠️ **THIS IS A DIRECT, DATED COLLISION WITH THE JEFFERIES OBJECTION ALREADY ON THIS PAGE (Blayne Curtis, 08-07), AND THE PAGE SHOULD HOLD BOTH RATHER THAN PICK:** Curtis argued that as MPO fills the gap left by CPO's pushout, *"these are SHORTER SCALE-UP CONNECTIONS — YOU MIGHT NOT NEED A HIGH-POWERED LASER,"* and that management's claim that laser content is unchanged is *"a lot of debate as to whether that's actually true."* DB asserts content survives the architecture switch; Jefferies says the physics may not carry it. **This is a specific, testable engineering dispute about whether laser content is invariant to link length — it is the single most load-bearing unresolved question on this dossier, and two houses now sit on opposite sides of it in print.**

🔴 **DB'S PHYSICS CASE FOR THE OPTICAL MIGRATION, with the marks:**
- *"Copper survives INTRA RACK THROUGH 2028, but **every doubling of data rate roughly HALVES its usable reach**, there is **NO VISIBLE SerDes ROADMAP BEYOND 448G**, and **model sparsity is growing the required coherent domain FASTER THAN COPPER CAN FOLLOW**."* SerDes marks: **[[AVGO]] 224G today → 448G next.**
- Power per port: **Meta/Broadcom 5.4W per 800G**, **[[NVDA]] ~4-5W**, versus ~15-17W for older generations.
- *"The thermal wall does not destroy demand, it **RELOCATES VALUE** to whoever solves heat at each layer."*
- 🔴 **CPO TIMING, and it lands on the LATE side of this page's range: *"CPO lands in SCALE OUT NOW as a supply chain pipe cleaner and in SCALE UP AROUND 2028-2029 (our channel checks say CLOSER TO 2029)."*** ⚠️ **Consistent with the Jefferies-conference read (2H C28 at the earliest, possibly C29/C30) and against the earlier DAMNANG "CPO is imminent" framing. The ladder holds.**

**DB'S TAM MARKS (third-party, cited not derived):** **Dell'Oro — total AI back-end networking approaching $259bn by 2030, with SCALE-UP ALONE EXCEEDING $144bn.** **Yole — optical transceivers from c.$23bn in 2025 to MORE THAN $112bn by 2031, with AI OVER 90% OF THE MIX by then.** ⚠️ **Cross-check against the ~$100bn TAM mark this page took on Aug 25 from a different chain: Yole's $112bn/2031 is in the same neighbourhood on a longer horizon, so the two are broadly consistent rather than a raise.**

**SUPPLY IS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT, and DB quantifies it per name:** [[LITE]] — **200G EML *"sold out for the foreseeable future"***, the 200G part at *"roughly 2x the ASP of 100G at a much smaller cost uplift,"* **InP output rising c.50% Q4/25→Q4/26**, **Greensboro fab built for c.$5bn of annual revenue capacity** adding from 2028. [[COHR]] — **datacenter book-to-bill above 4x for two consecutive quarters**, order duration into 2028, SKU-level forecasts to 2030, and the **6-inch InP transition doubling device output by September.** ⚠️ **The 6-inch transition is the same discriminator Jefferies named as the swing factor in the LITE-vs-COHR relative trade; DB now underwrites the COHR side of it.**

**CPO SIZING FROM THE COHR SIDE:** DB characterises CPO as *"a c.**10x UNIT-VOLUME EXPANSION** with recent earnings confirming **NO PUSH-OUT ON DEMAND**"*; liquid-crystal OCS already in early revenue; multi-rail scale-across impacting from **H1/27**; advanced thermal materials — in aggregate **$20bn+ of incremental SAM on top of a $50bn+ existing one.**

🔴 **THE FACEPLATE THERMAL WALL HAS AN ANSWER, AND IT EXTENDS THE PLUGGABLE ERA:** [[ANET]]'s **XPO (extra-dense pluggable optics)** — a **liquid-cooled 12.8T module reaching 204.8T per rack unit.** DB: this *"effectively extends the pluggable era that Arista is built on."* ⚠️ **Directly relevant to the CPO-timing debate: if faceplate density can be liquid-cooled upward, one of the strongest physical arguments for co-packaging weakens. DB simultaneously names *"CPO eventually bypassing the board level moats"* as the reason its ANET PT is *"disciplined"* — i.e. the same house that supplies the reprieve prices the eventual loss.**

🔴 **AND THE FOUNDRY QUANTIFIES THE I/O WALL — [[TSM]] at SEMICON Taiwan (via Wells Fargo):**
- **Compute performance is increasing 3x every 2 years against conventional I/O bandwidth growing only ~1.4x every 2 years.** ⚠️ **This is the same 3x-vs-1.4x ratio the page took on Aug 31 from the /wiki-ingest pass — now confirmed from a second independent write-up of the same TSMC presentation. Not a new datapoint; a corroborated one.**
- **TSMC quantified CPO benefits at 4-10x energy efficiency and 10-20x lower latency vs copper.**
- **COUPE** (Compact Universal Photonic Engine) uses **SoIC 3D bonding** for photonic integration, with **COUPE-GC (grating coupling)** or **COUPE-EC (edge coupling)**.
- **The bandwidth-scaling path: (1) per-channel rates rising from the current 200 Gbps while LANE COUNTS GO FROM 16 TO 128+, and (2) WDM wavelengths going from 1 to 4, 8, or 16+.**
- ⚠️ **The constraint this creates and the page should track: COUPE routes CPO volume through SoIC — the same 3D-stacking capacity that HBM base dies and advanced logic already compete for. Cross-read the Fubon SoIC cut already on this page (70K WPM by Q4-28, down from 90K). A CPO ramp and an HBM4E base-die migration are bidding for the same tool.**

**Vendor colour also in the WF flash:** *"[[NVDA]] supplier Unimicron probed over allegedly relabeling China-made parts"* (NikkeiAsia).
## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-08-31 — THE CPO DATE IS NOT ONE DATE, IT IS A THREE-RUNG LADDER, AND THREE SOURCES SUPPLIED ALL THREE RUNGS IN 48 HOURS

**The page has been carrying "CPO timing" as a single contested number. It is not. TSMC, Coherent and Jefferies each named a different date on 2026-08-30/31 and they are describing DIFFERENT EVENTS. Written as a ladder so the page stops treating them as a disagreement:**

| Rung | Date | Who says it | What it actually is |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Foundry production readiness** | **2H26** | **[[TSM]] · K.C. Hsu, SEMICON Taiwan** — *"some producers will mass produce CPO in 2H26. TSMC is on track to enter production of CPO in 2H26"* | TSMC's own optical-engine line running |
| **First revenue at a component vendor** | **December quarter 2026** | **[[COHR]] management** — *"CPO (scale out) demand has been **PULLED FORWARD**, with **initial revenue expected during the December quarter**"* (via @crux_capital_, corpus, 2026-08-30) | First dollars, small base |
| **Volume / large-scale deployment** | **2H28 at the earliest** | **Jefferies · Lucy Roe, Chicago Semis Conference feedback** — *"the near-term opportunity being **NPO**, as **CPO timing is pushed more towards 2H28 at the earliest**"* | The number that matters for the P&L |

- 🔴🔴 **AND THE MECHANISM THAT MAKES THE TWO-YEAR GAP COHERENT IS TSMC'S OWN, STATED IN THE SAME BREATH AS ITS 2H26 CLAIM: *"**Foundry can scale up optical engines, but the real bottleneck for large-scale deployment lies in OTHER PARTS of the supply chain, including LASERS, OPTICAL FIBERS, FIBER CONNECTORS, and PRODUCT TESTING**"* (K.C. Hsu, TSMC head of advanced packaging, relayed by Jefferies Asia TMT and independently by @aleabitoreddit citing UDN, corpus, 2026-08-31).** ➜ **The foundry is not the constraint and has not been for a while. The constraint is everything around the engine — which is exactly where [[COHR]], [[LITE]], [[AAOI]], Fabrinet, connector and test vendors live. **This reallocates the CPO trade away from the silicon and toward the optical path and the test layer, and it is the most actionable conclusion on this page this month.**
- 🔴 **THE TEST LEG, NAMED AND SIZED IN TWO PLACES. TSMC has enabled **wafer-level optical testing for edge-coupled devices**, *"allowing defective dies to be **screened out BEFORE costly CPO assembly**"* (Mirror Daily, via Jefferies, 08-31). And from the corpus: *"**CPO becomes revenue for $VIAV BEFORE it reaches volume for the rest of the industry**… a problem with **one optical channel can affect the yield and economics of the ENTIRE PACKAGE**, making earlier and more precise testing far more valuable"* (@crux_capital_, 2026-08-31). ➜ **Known-good-die economics: in a co-packaged part, one bad channel condemns the whole assembly, so test value scales with package value rather than with channel count. Test is the earliest-revenue, highest-leverage rung on the ladder above.**
- 🔴 **SILICON PHOTONICS CROSSES 50% IN 2027, PER THE FOUNDRY: *"**silicon photonics could account for more than 50% of the optical transceiver market in 2027**"*, becoming *"mainstream"* (K.C. Hsu; corroborated @ParadisLabs, corpus). **Market growth marks from the same source: optical transceiver sales **+25% in 2025 and +50% in 2026**; the **100G-and-above segment +60% in 2025**.** **imec VP Philippe Absil at the same event: *"**Co-packaged optics is being adopted FASTER than originally expected.** I've seen market reports that were showing very moderate and gradual adoption, moving into 2030."***
- 🔴 **COUPE NOW HAS PUBLISHED PHYSICS, AND IT IS THE STRONGEST TECHNICAL CASE ON THIS PAGE: **2–4 micron bonding pitch limits transmission loss at 112 Gbps to 0.06 dB vs 1.38 dB for micro-bumps** (~23x), with **latency 10–20 ns**; SoIC bonding of EIC to PIC; supports both grating and edge couplers. **Scaling paths: per-channel 200 → >400 Gbps with channels 16 → >128 (total bandwidth 3.2 → >12.8 Tbps), and WDM 1 → 4 → 8 → 16+ wavelengths.** Compact universal photonic engine claimed at *">10x power efficiency vs traditional copper wire-based applications."* **An optical PDK for electronic-photonic co-simulation *"supports [[CDNS]] and [[SNPS]] tools"* — the first EDA read-through from CPO on this wiki.**
- 🔴🔴 **[[COHR]] IS CONTENT-NEUTRAL BETWEEN NPO AND CPO, WHICH DE-RISKS IT AGAINST EVERY DATE ON THE LADDER ABOVE: *"**$COHR says NPO and CPO carry ROUGHLY THE SAME CONTENT for it.** NPO moves the optical engine close to the chip. CPO places it directly beside the chip inside the same package. COHR says we're good either way. **Because it can supply so much of the FULL OPTICAL PATH — from generating the light to moving it and converting it back into an electrical signal — the ATTACH POINT changes while its TOTAL CONTENT stays similar.** Management says **almost every major strategic customer has an active NPO or CPO engagement**… **PhotonLink, launching September 21**, packages those capabilities into one integrated platform"* (@crux_capital_ relaying COHR management, corpus, 2026-08-30). ➜ **This is the single most important competitive fact on the page for position sizing: the NPO-vs-CPO timing fight — which Jefferies calls the near-term trade — is NOT a Coherent risk. It is a risk for anyone whose content is specific to one attach point. **PhotonLink on Sept 21 is a dated catalyst.**
- ⚠️🔴 **A GENUINE TECHNOLOGY THREAT TO THE WHOLE CPO PREMISE, FLAGGED BY JEFFERIES AND WORTH TRACKING: *"Keep an eye on **Lightmatter** as it relates to CPO… developing an interposer technology called **Passage**… simplistically it could **BYPASS THE NEED FOR CPO as it stacks the CPU/GPU ON TOP OF the optical network rather than placing it BESIDE it**. At our conf last week it was clear that the development of Passage is progressing **faster vs when I saw them in SF last summer, and as a result could come AT THE SAME TIME as CPO**"* (Jefferies · Lucy Roe, 2026-08-31). ➜ **If an optical interposer arrives concurrently with CPO rather than after it, the industry may skip a generation of co-packaged parts. Jefferies flags it as *"potential −VE for SOI, potential +VE for STM"*. **Logged as the first credible architectural alternative to CPO on this page — early, private, single-source, but the timing claim is what makes it matter.** ⚠️ **A second, cruder version of the same idea in the corpus: *"At least for scale-in, it seems fair to assume that **CPO will eventually be replaced** as well"* (@jukan05, 08-31).**
- 🔴 **THE DEMAND SIDE FINALLY HAS THIRD-PARTY NUMBERS — DELL'ORO Q2, AND THE MIX SHIFT IS THE STORY: **optical transport equipment +15% y/y; DATA-CENTER INTERCONNECT revenue +45%; DISAGGREGATED OPTICAL LINE SYSTEMS +80%; cloud providers now 34% of the ENTIRE optical transport market**"* (Dell'Oro via @crux_capital_, corpus, 2026-08-30). **The framing, which is the thesis: *"AI clusters are increasingly going to spread across separate buildings, campuses and regions… If an AI cluster gets too large to fit inside one building, or **power availability forces the GPUs to be installed across several locations**, those sites still need to exchange enormous amounts of data and operate as one larger system (**scale across**). That extends the optical opportunity beyond the short links inside the building and into the **coherent equipment carrying data across KILOMETRES**."* **[[CIEN]] and Nokia are named as the leading vendors in disaggregated OLS — the +80% line.** ➜ **"Scale-across" is a power-constraint consequence, not an optics fashion. It is the cleanest link on this wiki between the [themes/ai-datacenter-power](ai-datacenter-power.md) bottleneck and optical revenue, and it accrues to the coherent/DCI vendors rather than to the intra-rack players.**
- **ECOC SEASONALITY, DATED AND QUANTIFIED — a tradeable pattern rather than a view: *"we've got **ECOC coming up in Sept** which should be a +ve catalyst for the optical cohort. Optical pure plays **[[CIEN]]/[[GLW]]/[[COHR]]/[[LITE]] all saw strong pre-ECOC momentum last year, trading up 21%/11%/9%/9% in the 3 weeks before**"* (Jefferies · Lucy Roe, 2026-08-31). **Jefferies is hosting ECOC meetings with Nokia, CIEN, MXL and *"OSRAM's new hire from [[NVDA]], Ashkan Seyedi"*.** **Barclays is separately running a *"Field Trip: Barclays Hosted Meetings at ECOC Exhibition"* (Thomas O'Malley, 08-31).**
- **SUPPLY-SIDE TIGHTNESS, TWO MARKS: *"French chip materials maker **Soitec is locking customers into MULTI-YEAR SUPPLY AGREEMENTS with DEPOSITS AND FIXED PRICING** as demand surges for the wafers used in AI data center optics, its chief executive told Reuters"* (@ReutersBiz, corpus, 2026-08-31) — deposits and fixed pricing is LTA behaviour arriving in optical substrates, the same structure memory adopted a year earlier. **And the merchant epi layer is earning like a bottleneck: *"A merchant InP foundry just reached a **47% OPERATING MARGIN**"* — LandMark — *"That's got to be a read through for $IQE"* (@crux_capital_, corpus, 2026-08-30), which closes the IQE read-through this wiki left open on 08-31 and is a read-across to [[AAOI]].**
- ⚠️ **COMPETITIVE PRESSURE FROM CHINA, and the numbers are large: *"Chinese optical firms absolutely huge earnings w/ **Innolight H1 rev +183%** and **Yangtze OF net profits +889%**. **Eptolink H1 net profits +91%** too with **heavy component stockpiling**"* (@ParadisLabs, corpus, 2026-08-30). ⚠️⚠️ **NAME GUARD (a documented failure mode on this wiki): INNOLIGHT is the Chinese optical-module maker — it is NOT [[LITE]] / Lumentum. Do not let a transcript or relay collapse the two.** **The stockpiling detail is the interesting one: component pre-buying by Chinese module makers is demand that is being pulled forward, not created.** **Jefferies' own EU pick for optical exposure remains Nokia, partly on an SX5E inclusion flow argument (*">$1.5B passive flow"*, cut-off 08-31).**

## 🆕🔴🔴 Recent signals (Aug 31, /wiki-ingest) — **[[TSM]] QUANTIFIES THE I/O GAP THAT MAKES OPTICAL COMPULSORY — 3x vs 1.4x EVERY TWO YEARS — AND DATES SiPh TO MAINSTREAM IN 2027. SEPARATELY, THE PUMP-LASER LTAs GET READ AS A PRICE CEILING BY THE BUYERS' OWN ANALYST.**

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE RATIO IS THE BEST SINGLE ARGUMENT THIS PAGE HAS EVER CARRIED, AND IT COMES FROM TSMC ITSELF AT SEMICON TAIWAN: *"TSMC believes the **optical I/O is becoming PREREQUISITE for AI/HPC system as the I/O GAP IS WIDENING between the COMPUTE SCALING (3x EVERY 2 YEARS) and I/O SCALING (1.4x EVERY TWO YEARS)**"* (BofA · Haas Liu, Buy, PO NT$3,100, 2026-08-31).** ➜ **A compounding 3x-vs-1.4x divergence is the physics case for CPO/NPO stated as a ratio rather than as a roadmap date — it says the transition is forced, not chosen, and it gets worse every generation. **Every 2027-28 content-step argument on [[LITE]], [[COHR]], [[CRDO]], [[AAOI]] and [[ALAB]] can now be anchored to a foundry-sourced number instead of to a vendor claim.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE COUPE ROADMAP, WITH BOTH SCALING AXES NAMED: TSMC's platform and *"broad photonics device/PDK library"* advance through *"**two complementary paths - 'FASTER AND NARROWING' with higher per-lane speed (200Gbps to 400Gbps) and lane count (128+ LANES), and 'SLOWER AND WIDER' by increasing wavelengths from 1 to 16+ THROUGH WDM**."* And the manufacturing progress: *"TSMC has **LOWERED COUPE GRATING COUPLER INSERTION LOSS while improving EDGE COUPLER PERFORMANCE** and sees **WAFER LEVEL TESTING IS CRITICAL to reduce the wasted packaging cost**"* (BofA, 2026-08-31). Dated by the trade press the same day: *"**TSMC sees SiPh going mainstream in 2027**"* (Digitimes, 2026-08-31).** ➜ **THE UNDERRATED ITEM IS WAFER-LEVEL TEST. Known-good-die screening BEFORE packaging is where the CPO cost curve actually breaks — packaging a bad photonic die scraps the whole assembly. Independent corroboration from the equipment side at the same show: Taiwan's **Kao Ming Tieh (4573)** demonstrated a production-grade **120-channel FAU-PIC automated active-alignment and test platform** with **Known-Good-Die screening before packaging** to *"reduce the risk of scrapping an entire batch from a single-channel failure"*; its **1.6T coupling equipment is *"validated by multiple silicon-photonics module makers and formally delivered"***, positioned as a standard **CPO alignment (AA Cell) station**, with the bottleneck named as *"ultra-high-channel-count FAU-PIC precision alignment as PIC channel counts rise past 100 and optical interfaces move to 2D arrays"* (@QQ_Timmy, corpus, 2026-08-30).**
- 🔴🔴 **THE PUMP-LASER LTAs, AND THE PERSPECTIVE MATTERS BECAUSE IT IS THE BUYER'S ANALYST TALKING: *"component shortages and the associated cost inflation have been a key concern for the margin trajectory; however, we note that **vendors such as [[COHR]] and [[LITE]] have recently highlighted MULTI-YEAR LTAs SIGNED FOR PUMP LASERS**, which, in our view, indicate **VOLUME GUARANTEES AT BETTER-THAN-FEARED PRICING** and should help **unlock supply at lower risk than perceived earlier to margins for system suppliers like [[CIEN]]**"* (JPM · Joseph Cardoso, CIEN preview, OW PT $635, 2026-08-28).** ➜ ⚠️⚠️ **READ THIS BOTH WAYS AND CARRY BOTH: (a) for the SYSTEM vendors ([[CIEN]], [[ANET]], [[CSCO]], NOK) it is margin protection and de-risks the 2027 gross-margin path; (b) for the COMPONENT vendors it is a **CEILING on participation in spot tightness** — a multi-year contract at a price the buyer's analyst calls favourable is not the same thing as scarcity pricing. **The optical-component bull case this quarter has partly rested on scarcity; LTAs convert scarcity into contracted volume.** ⚠️ **Neither COHR nor LITE has disclosed the LTA terms or duration in any source this wiki holds. OPEN ITEM.** **Demand-side corroboration in the same note, and it is unambiguous: *"strong DCI/scale-across demand signals from **both system vendors (ANET, CSCO, NOK) AND component suppliers (COHR, FN, LITE) through earnings to-date**."***
- 🔴 **THE 1.6T ASP STEP IS NOW QUANTIFIED ON THE COPPER SIDE TOO: *"AEC remains strong, with the **move to 1.6T expected to provide a roughly 60% ASP UPLIFT**"* ([[CRDO]]; Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, BUY PT $270, 2026-08-28).** ➜ **A ~60% generational ASP step means interconnect revenue can grow strongly on flat-ish units — which is the mechanism that makes GW-linked scenarios ([[SPCX]] at ~6GW in CY27) produce large revenue numbers. ⚠️ **STANDING UNIT GUARD: the per-GW figures in the CRDO work are **Credo REVENUE per GW of customer capacity**, NOT build cost and NOT capex/GW. They must never be netted against a datacentre construction anchor.**
- **AND THE FORWARD-SCHEDULE ITEM: **ECOC 2026, 2026-09-21/22 (Spain)** — Morgan Stanley hosts **[[COHR]], [[CIEN]], [[CSCO]], KEYS and NOK** (MS TMT desk, 2026-08-31). First optical corporate-access window of the autumn. ⚠️ **Peer-valuation framing from the corpus, logged as an independent view and NOT as research: LITE/COHR/AAOI at *"5-14x 2027 sales"* with *"revenue that's sold out, LTAs to 2028-30, shipping today, on 30-50% GMs"* (@ParadisLabs, corpus, 2026-08-28) — the band is a range across three companies, not a mark on any one of them.** **Also logged: an independent piece flags a *"major read through"* from **IQE** (compound-semi epiwafers) to [[AAOI]], SMTC, Sivers and MACOM — **body did not survive, no thesis captured, OPEN ITEM** (Substack, 2026-08-28). IQE sits upstream of InP/GaAs laser production and this wiki has no IQE coverage.**

## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 29, /run-inbox — **THE CPO ROADMAP THIS PAGE CARRIED FROM THE COMPANY RELEASE NOW HAS ITS ENGINEERING TARGETS, AND A NAMED BANDWIDTH-WALL RATIO**)

**Wells Fargo's Aaron Rakers works through [[SKHYNIX]]'s co-packaged-optics roadmap (published in *Nature Electronics*), and supplies the three technical targets the company newsroom item on this page did not quantify:**

- 🔴 **>100 Tb/s of bandwidth per node · energy consumption <1 pJ/bit · chip-to-chip latency <10 nanoseconds.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE BANDWIDTH-WALL RATIO, which is the cleanest justification for CPO on this page: *average interconnect bandwidth is expanding at ~1.4x per 2 years against compute throughput expanding at ~3.0x per 2 years*.** ➤ **A ~2.1x-per-2-years divergence is the whole thesis in one number — it is why copper fails, and it compounds. Rakers: this *"is putting increased strain on the use of copper-based interconnects, which Hynix notes as suffering from RAPIDLY INCREASING SIGNAL LOSS AND POWER CONSUMPTION as distances across chip packages and AI rack infrastructure expand,"* requiring more re-timers, which themselves add power and latency.**
- **TIMING, and it is the discipline item: Rakers sees CPO-attached memory as **a 2028+ story**. Not a 2026-27 revenue event.**

🔴 **THE CONCEPTUAL SHIFT WORTH RECORDING: Rakers explicitly moves CPO out of the box this page has kept it in. *"As we have focused on CPO as predominately in the NETWORKING LAYER of AI infrastructure, we believe the evolution of OPTICS-CONNECTED MEMORY could become an increasingly visible dynamic to monitor."* SK hynix's own long-term view is that CPO *"is expected to extend optical interconnects ALL THE WAY TO THE MEMORY INTERFACE"* — Rakers flags as open whether that means DDR, CXL or another interconnect. Optical fibre connectivity is also shown enabling POOLED MEMORY.** ➤ **If CPO addresses the memory interface rather than only the switch port, the addressable content per rack is materially larger than the optical-transceiver TAMs on this page assume. Logged as an open sizing question, not a re-sized TAM.**

**The named derivative read-through inside Rakers' coverage, logged to [[MRVL]]: *"Marvell — positioning of Celestial AI capabilities; … Photonic Fabric memory modules, Photonic Fabric NICs, and Photonic Fabric chiplets, noted as FOUNDATIONAL ELEMENTS OF A MULTI-RACK OPTICAL SHARED MEMORY ARCHITECTURE FOR AI INFERENCE."* Company line from SK hynix: *"optical interconnects are likely to become the FOUNDATIONAL CONNECTIVITY TECHNOLOGY for future AI infrastructure,"* with ecosystem collaboration named as the key driver of broad-based volume deployment.** (Wells Fargo Securities · Aaron Rakers, Technology Weekly, 2026-08-21) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Wells_Fargo_Hynix_-_Whos_Customer_B_CPO_Roadmap_S_Korea_Memo.html)

Flagged for verification. In-rack fault-routing makes it a RELIABILITY and RESOURCE-MANAGEMENT product, which attaches per rack rather than per topology and is not in any ratio model on this page.** **Also: LITE claims a *"BROAD BASE of customer engagement, with some early customers being even MORE SUBSTANTIAL than [[GOOG]]"* — ⚠️ which is hard to square with the same management telling BTG on 08-13 that *"our shipments appear to be only to Google."* Engagement vs shipments; tracked as an open item on the LITE page.**

It is the invariant across the form-factor question this page has spent months on.**

Both statements are on the record from 2026-08-27 and they cannot both be comfortable.**
- **PHYSICS, QUANTIFIED, FOR THE COPPER-WALL ARGUMENT THIS PAGE RUNS QUALITATIVELY: copper's reliable reach goes from **~10 metres at 800G to ~2 metres at 1.6T**, against many in-rack and in-cluster links longer than 2-3m. And the forward monitorable LITE names is **"SCALE IN"** — *"does this thing really take hold? Are you going to see optics not only penetrate the BACK PLANE, which I think is largely a foregone conclusion, are you going to see it actually **go IN-TRAY and serve this high-bandwidth connectivity between MEMORY and between GPUs**"* — over the next **12-18 months**. 🔴 **This is the same architectural claim DAMNANG makes from the MEMORY side in "HBM Density Peak" (08-20): as HBM density per GPU stops rising, system budget migrates from capacity to data movement and the optical conversion point moves toward the package. A laser supplier and a memory analyst, independently, same transition. See [hbm-memory](hbm-memory.md) and [cxl-memory-fabric](cxl-memory-fabric.md).**
- **Two supporting marks from the same Jefferies note: **1.6T transceiver shipments tracking toward 100M UNITS in C27 while 800G keeps GROWING** — *"overlapping ramps rather than a simple replacement cycle"*; and **STMicro** engaged with **80% of the optical market** (AWS <30% penetrated, China <50%), **SiPho mix 5% → 30% by 2028**, **NPO first shipments 2H27 ramping 2028 at 2x transceiver content** on added advanced packaging.** **On EMLs: LITE says 100G→200G is a **~2x ASP uplift** with **[[AVGO]] the only other main supplier**, EML transceiver share falling **70-80% (800G) → 40-50% (1.6T)** on SiPh but unit counts still rising, and a share REBOUND expected at **3.2T** as *"silicon photonics runs out of steam."* ⚠️ MACOM separately claims **200G photodiode share gains vs COHR/AVGO**, mostly into 1.6T.**

## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 27, /wiki-ingest — **~100 COMPANIES AT ONE CONFERENCE SAY CPO HAS SLIPPED TO 2H C28 AT THE EARLIEST AND POSSIBLY C29/C30 — AND [[COHR]] SAYS THE NPO CONTENT OPPORTUNITY IS "BROADLY COMPARABLE," WHICH TURNS THE DELAY FROM A THESIS RISK INTO A FORM-FACTOR SUBSTITUTION**) — 🔴🔴 **THIS PAGE'S OPEN ITEM HAS BEEN "CPO IS INEVITABLE (DAMNANG, MARCH)" vs "CPO IS ~2-3 YEARS+ OUT (MIZUHO/SEMIANALYSIS, AUGUST)". THE JEFFERIES CONFERENCE RESOLVES THE TIMING SIDE DECISIVELY TOWARD *LATER*, AND THREE INDEPENDENT SOURCES IN THE SAME 48 HOURS SAY THE CONTENT DOLLARS DO NOT WAIT FOR IT.**

### 🔴🔴 THE CONFERENCE READ — "NPO STEALS THE SHOW"

**Jefferies annual Semiconductor Conference, ~100 companies across the semiconductor and hardware ecosystem (Blayne Curtis / Janardan Menon / Kevin Garrigan et al., recap 2026-08-27). Verbatim:**
- **THE VOLUME NUMBER: *"**1.6T transceiver shipments now tracking toward 100M UNITS in C27** while demand for 800G remains robust, creating **OVERLAPPING RAMPS rather than a simple replacement cycle**."*** ➤ **The "overlapping ramps" framing is the important part: 800G is not being cannibalised on the timetable the bears assumed.**
- **[[COHR]] SPECIFICALLY: *"COHR described demand as **EXTRAORDINARY** and expects **demand to exceed supply for at least another 12-18 MONTHS**, potentially requiring **capacity expansion BEYOND its existing 4x plan**."***
- 🔴 **THE TIMING VERDICT: *"several companies indicated that **meaningful CPO adoption has shifted to 2H C28 AT THE EARLIEST and could SLIP INTO C29/C30** as **testing, reliability, supply-chain, and standardization challenges persist**."***
- 🔴 **AND THE CONSEQUENCE, WHICH IS THE ACTIONABLE PART: *"The delay is creating a **significant OPENING FOR NPO**, with **multiple hyperscalers, switch vendors, and XPU providers developing PROPRIETARY architectures rather than waiting for standardized CPO solutions.** Importantly, **[[COHR]] sees the optical-content opportunity in NPO as BROADLY COMPARABLE TO CPO**, suggesting the **timing shift changes the near-term FORM FACTOR more than it diminishes the longer-term opportunity for lasers, optical engines, fiber attachment, and other optical components.**"***
➤ **This is the cleanest statement of the thesis this page needs: the CPO date slipping is NOT a content-dollar downgrade, it is a redistribution from a standardised platform transition to a set of proprietary NPO designs. What changes is WHO integrates (hyperscalers and switch vendors, in-house, sooner) and WHEN standards arrive (later) — not how many lasers, engines and fibre attachments get sold.**

### ✅ THIS CORROBORATES THE 08-25 [[LITE]] DATAPOINT RATHER THAN CONTRADICTING IT

**The 08-25 entry on this wiki logged LITE up-sizing CPO with its largest customer and **pulling NPO FORWARD from CY29 to CY28**. Read against Jefferies: CPO slips to 2H C28+, NPO pulls forward to CY28. **Those are the same statement from two ends of the chain — NPO arrives first and CPO arrives late — and the LITE optical TAM raise ($90bn → ~$100bn by CY30 at a 45-50% CAGR) is what that substitution looks like in a supplier's model.** ➤ **The apparent contradiction between "CPO delayed" and "LITE raising optical TAM" dissolves once form factor is separated from content.**

### 🔴 A THIRD INDEPENDENT VOICE, AND IT IS THE SWITCH VENDOR: [[ANET]] MAKES NON-INTEGRATION THE STRATEGY

**From an [[ANET]] investor call (2026-08-26; internal relay via Daniel Grozdea — ⚠️ *this is an LLM-structured SUMMARY of the call, not a verbatim transcript; treat the quotes as paraphrase-grade*):**
- **ANET's *"LACK of in-house optical integration is presented as a VIRTUE, allowing it to work with a broad MERCHANT ecosystem — Cisco/Acacia, [[CIEN]], Nokia/Infinera, [[MRVL]] — to offer optimal solutions (lowest cost, power, highest transmission distance) to customers WITHOUT VENDOR LOCK-IN."***
- **Its **XPO (External Pluggable Optics)** approach: *"liquid-cooled, high-reliability, short/long-reach laser options, **including LPO (Linear Pluggable Optics) for very low power consumption, analogous to CPO BUT WITH SERVICEABILITY**."* ANET *"also supports **'Open CPO'** to ensure multi-vendor choice and serviceability."*
- **The summary's own read: *"the strategic advantage of ARST's non-integrated optical approach and the practical benefits of **XPO over early CPO implementations (serviceability, choice, power efficiency)** are often overlooked in the CPO hype cycle."***
➤ **Three different layers of the stack — the component vendor ([[COHR]]), the module/laser vendor ([[LITE]]) and the switch vendor ([[ANET]]) — independently arrive at the same 2026 conclusion: serviceable pluggable/near-package optics win the next two years, and standardised CPO is a late-decade event. That is now the page's best-supported position, and it is a reversal of the March "CPO is inevitable [soon]" framing.**

### 🔴 AND [[NVDA]] HEDGED IT IN SILICON, IN THE SAME WEEK

**Announced with NVDA's Q2 FY27 print: *"NVIDIA **Spectrum-6** switch systems — **supporting BOTH pluggable AND co-packaged optics** as part of the NVIDIA Vera Rubin platform — are arriving across the world's gigascale AI factories"* (NVIDIA product announcements, relayed by Jefferies · Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-26).
➤ **The largest buyer of optics in the world is shipping a switch that takes either form factor. That is a supplier-friendly outcome (both roadmaps get volume) and it removes the binary risk this page has been carrying on CPO timing. It also means NVDA is no longer a forcing function for a CPO date.**
- ⚠️ **Carry the standing engineering caveat from this page's 08-18 entry alongside it: the joint NVIDIA-Research/[[LITE]] monolithic DFB laser array for NPO was judged NOT shippable by an independent reviewer on RIN (~−137 dBc/Hz), and **~10% laser wall-plug efficiency is the critical floor below which CPO/NPO energy savings degrade exponentially.** An NPO-first world still has to clear that floor.**

### THE ADJACENT SUPPLY CONSTRAINTS — AND THEY ARE NOW THE BINDING ONES

- 🔴 **SUBSTRATES AND GLASS CLOTH, NOT OPTICS, MAY BE THE 2027 CEILING: *"**ABF substrates & PCB/CCL are the biggest constraints for 2027.** Nvidia or Google can reduce HBM per GPU and DRAM per server to increase shipments, but **they can't work around this**"* (@zephyr_z9, corpus, 2026-08-27). Concretely, **Kinsus says T-glass shortage means *"approximately 10% to 15% of potential MONTHLY REVENUE cannot currently be realized"* — *"the key operational bottleneck"* — with ABF utilisation at 85% heading to ~95% by year-end and **BT utilisation ~75% → ~85%, *"supported by memory demand and GROWING OPPORTUNITIES IN HIGH-SPEED OPTICAL COMMUNICATIONS"***, and a possible further capacity round **as early as mid-2027 if optical demand accelerates** (Jefferies · O'Mara, 2026-08-26).**
- **Taiwan's tape agreed: *"TAIEX +1.5% with **CPO/ABF/CCL/Cooling leading the rally**"* (Jefferies · O'Mara, 2026-08-26).
- **A new gold-bumping datapoint in the optical chain: Jefferies/Fubon initiated on **Chipbond (6147 TT, BUY)** on *"gold bumping in high-speed optics"* (2026-08-26) — and **Jefferies Silk Road initiated BUY on LUXSHARE and CHIPBOND TECH, with BUYs on CRYSTAL OPTECH and LANTE OPTICS** (2026-08-27). *All off-coverage here; logged as chain-level confirmation that the optical build is broadening into packaging and component names.*
- ⚠️ **The bear vector on laser pricing this page already tracks got no new support: no update this week on JX Advanced Metals' InP substrate capacity plan (+7-10x). The InP bottleneck framing from JPM (EML supply ~30% BELOW demand; AXT and Sumitomo as the consolidated substrate base) stands unchallenged. See [[AXTI]], [[COHR]], [[AAOI]].**

### 🔴 THE OTHER STRUCTURAL SHIFT: "SCALE-ACROSS" IS ALREADY ONE-THIRD OF [[ANET]]'S AI BUSINESS

**From the same ANET call (2026-08-26): AI networking is *"transitioning from **scale-out** (single data center) to **scale-across** (multi-site clusters), which **ALREADY ACCOUNTS FOR ONE-THIRD of its AI business**"*, driven by *"the physical limitations (space, power) of single data centers for ever-growing AI clusters."* **Scale-up** (within-rack high-bandwidth) is *"expected to be material **no earlier than next year**, representing a multi-year journey"*, with ANET *"actively influencing the development of Ethernet for scale-up in OCP."* ANET reaffirmed its **minimum $3.5bn AI guide** and now has **over 100 AI customers**, with **two 10%+ customers and one-to-two more expected to reach that threshold, potentially including neoclouds.**
➤ **Scale-across is a long-reach, DWDM/coherent problem, not a short-reach transceiver problem. At one-third of ANET's AI revenue already, it is the fastest-growing optical demand pool on this page and it favours [[CIEN]], Nokia/Infinera, [[MRVL]] coherent DSP and long-reach laser content over intra-rack CPO. It is also the clearest example of demand migrating to a form factor the CPO debate does not cover at all.**
- **Supply-chain visibility from the same call, useful as a lead-time anchor for the whole theme: *"Demand visibility for projects extends **1-2 years**, with customers planning **18-24 months out due to 52-WEEK LEAD TIMES for TSMC wafer starts**."***

### ⚠️ AND THE PERENNIAL COUNTERWEIGHT, RESTATED
