Is It Going To Happen Again?"* (@Gaetano substack, corpus, 2026-08-26 — *video, not read here; logged as a sentiment marker only, no analytic content extracted*).** ⚠️ *Recorded so the page does not read as one-sided; it carries no data.*

## Recent signals (Aug 25, /run-inbox 23h — **THE JPM OPTICAL FEEDBACK CALL, THE MIZUHO/SEMIANALYSIS DINNER, AND A SAMSUNG SILICON-PHOTONICS DATAPOINT FROM THE COMPANY**) — 🔴🔴 **TWO HOUSES PUT OPPOSITE-SOUNDING CPO TIMELINES ON THE TAPE WITHIN TEN DAYS AND THEY ARE *NOT* IN CONFLICT — BOTH LAND ON 2028. WHAT THEY GENUINELY DISAGREE ABOUT IS WHAT YOU OWN IN THE MEANTIME.**

### THE APPARENT CONTRADICTION, RESOLVED — AND THE REAL DISAGREEMENT UNDERNEATH IT

- **JPM (Joe Cardoso, 2026-08-14), from a conference at which it hosted the optical complex plus 650 Group:** *"big debates in June, CPO, NPO. **Everyone's pretty much in consensus is that CPO is NOT DELAYED. The material year is expected to be calendar 2028**, and that **NPO is ADDITIVE to the CPO TAM**… from a content perspective, while there's some nuances, it seems like it's **consensus at this point across at least the supplier ecosystem that CONTENT IS LARGELY THE SAME** as you think about CPO and NPO."*
- **Mizuho relaying SemiAnalysis (2026-08-24):** *"Strong copper, NPO SiPho/InP, UPSIDE to [[CRDO]] and [[LITE]], while **CPO is ~2-3 years away**."*
- ➤ ✅ **From 2026-08-24, "~2-3 years away" IS 2028-2029. The two are the same call. Do not log this as a disagreement.**
- ➤ 🔴 **THE REAL DISAGREEMENT IS ABOUT THE BRIDGE.** Mizuho/SA use the CPO delay to argue for **copper and AEC** in the interim (*"Upside 2027E [[CRDO]] AEC copper for agentic AI CPU racks and key customer xAI/[[SPCX]] potentially ramping 5-10GW"*) plus **NPO/SiPho/InP for [[LITE]]**. JPM uses the same 2028 date to argue that **NPO broadens the CUSTOMER set** away from a CPO ecosystem that *"has largely been [[NVDA]]"*. **Same date, two different intermediate trades — copper vs merchant optical engine. That is the live question, and this page should carry it as such.**

### 🔴🔴 OPEN ITEM — **"CPO IS INEVITABLE" (DAMNANG, MARCH) vs "CPO IS ~2-3 YEARS+ OUT" (MIZUHO/SEMIANALYSIS, AUGUST).** RECORDED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, DATED, AND **NOT RESOLVED.**

| Side | Source, dated | Verbatim |
|---|---|---|
| **INEVITABLE** | **DAMNANG (Substack), "Why CPO Is Becoming Inevitable? (CPO Special Part 1)" and "CPO, Fully Dissected (Part 2)", both 2026-03-18** | The whole argument is that copper's reach, power and shoreline-bandwidth-density limits converge, so *"In the 1.6T world and beyond, **optical interconnects aren't a nice-to-have. They're the only option**"* — and that the place CPO is *"most urgently needed"* is the scale-out switch, *"when you have tens or hundreds of optical channels feeding into a single chip."* |
| **~2-3 YEARS+ OUT** | **Mizuho · Vijay Rakesh, relaying SemiAnalysis' Doug O'Laughlin at a Mizuho-hosted dinner, 2026-08-24** | *"Less-constrained GPU/ASIC shipments imply **upside AEC/InP/NPO with CPO ~2-3yr+ out**"* — and the trade that follows is **copper/AEC ([[CRDO]]) and NPO ([[LITE]]) in the interim**, not CPO. |

- ⚠️ **BE PRECISE ABOUT WHERE THEY ACTUALLY DIFFER, BECAUSE IT IS NARROWER THAN THE HEADLINES SUGGEST — AND THAT IS ITSELF THE FINDING.** DAMNANG's own timing, in the SAME March cluster: *"All three of these constraints converge around **2027 to 2028**. That is also when the industry is targeting CPO volume production"*, and his reading of Hock Tan — *"His 'not yet' horizon runs to roughly **2028**"* — with the standing warning that *"'Copper versus optical' is not a binary choice. It is a **SEQUENCE**."* **So the DATES are compatible: inevitable-by-2027/28 vs 2-3yr+-out-from-August-2026.** 🔴 **THE REAL DISPUTE IS DIRECTION-OF-CERTAINTY AND WHAT YOU OWN IN THE MEANTIME.** DAMNANG treats the destination as physically forced and the interim as a sequencing question; Mizuho/SA treat the delay as the tradeable fact and route the money to **copper AEC and NPO**, i.e. to the very technologies that exist BECAUSE CPO is not here yet.
- ➤ **AND THE "+" IN "2-3yr+" IS DOING REAL WORK.** It is open-ended in a way "2027-28" is not. A CPO material year of 2029-30 rather than 2028 would leave the AEC and NPO trades running roughly twice as long as this page currently assumes — which is the single most valuable thing to falsify here.
- ➤ **WHAT WOULD SETTLE IT, and it is checkable:** the **NPO ELS ramp in 2H27** and the **embedded-NPO ramp in 2028** that JPM reports (above) are the intermediate milestones. **If ELS NPO ships at volume in 2H27, DAMNANG's "sequence" reading is right and Mizuho's "+" is wrong. If the ELS date slips into 2028, CPO's material year moves with it and the copper/AEC window extends.** ⚠️ **DO NOT COLLAPSE THIS INTO ONE VIEW. This page carries all three timings — DAMNANG 2027-28, JPM calendar-2028, Mizuho/SA 2028-29+ — as an open range, and every CPO TAM on this page should be stated against a named date.**



### 🔴 THE JPM CALL, WHICH IS THE MOST DETAILED SUPPLIER-LEVEL OPTICAL SOURCE IN THIS RUN

**Source: J.P. Morgan · Joe Cardoso (hosted by Josh) — client call following [[LITE]] and [[COHR]] earnings and the JPM technology conference, 2026-08-14. ⚠️ Machine transcript with name garbling ("Crito"=[[CRDO]], "Coop"=CoWoS/CPO, "E-cock"=ECOC, "Fabernet"=Fabrinet, "light"=[[LITE]]).** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_14_jpm_on_lite_and_cohr_feedback.html)

- 🔴 **NPO COMES IN TWO FLAVOURS AND THEY RAMP A YEAR APART.** *"MPO [NPO], basically **two flavors** they're highlighting, there's an **ELS version**, very similar to the CPO rendition. And the other… is an **EMBEDDED version, where basically the laser is packaged near the optical engine. Both of them are expected to have similar content to CPO.** The nuance is that the **embedded laser opportunity has the LOWER COST LASER**… instead of a high[ly] ultra high-powered laser, they're using a **MEDIUM POWERED laser**… so it has a little bit lower ASPs relative to the ultra high power that they're using for [[NVDA]], **but higher ASP relative to 200 gig EML**. So sitting in between those two, but they make up the content difference in terms of the complexity around the packaging."* 🔴 **Timing: "the EMBEDDED solution looks likely to ramp in 2028 versus the ELS version of [N]PO looking more closely aligned to the CPO version of ELS in that BACK HALF OF 2027."** And the competitive point: *"the interesting dynamic with the embedded solution is **they view it as a HIGHER BARRIER TO ENTRY versus the ELS**."* ➤ **This is the first time this page has had NPO split into two products with separate ramp dates, separate ASP bands and a stated barrier-to-entry difference. It also gives the 2H27 ELS date a companion 2028 embedded date, which is what makes JPM's "CPO not delayed, material year 2028" internally consistent.**
- 🔴 **THE MERCHANT CW LASER OPPORTUNITY, SIZED AS A SHARE OF CHIP OUTPUT.** *"what's really driving them to pursue this first and foremost is that they have **efficiencies running through the plant well ahead of what they expected**, and so that's opening up capacity to go after the CW opportunity. They basically expect it to be a **pretty meaningful contribution in 2027**. The way they framed it is, **you take your EML capacity additions in 2027 and you layer on the CW opportunity in such a way that equates to 10 to 20% of the chip output in 2027.**"* The driver: *"**silicon photonics is moving from 20 to 30% of the mix, [to] something closer to 50, 60% of the mix**."* Margin: *"the lower cost design… helping the margin profile look much better. **Not as high as EML, but definitely above the corporate average margins.** And so accretive at the end of the day."* 🔴 **And the pricing consequence, which is the genuinely new item: *"because of the supply imbalance that's happening at the CW level. This is obviously driving negotiations and negotiation leverage for them and CONCESSIONS ACROSS THE PORTFOLIO… across the portfolio including OCS, transceivers and EML specifically pricing on the EML side."*** ➤ **A shortage in ONE component (merchant CW) is being converted into price across the WHOLE portfolio. That is the mechanism by which an optical supply constraint shows up as margin rather than as revenue, and it is worth watching for wherever else a single chokepoint exists.**
- **THE [[NVDA]] ELS WIN, DELIBERATELY NOT OVERSOLD BY THE ANALYST.** *"Relative to the ELS win or order that they talked about, that's specifically with [[NVDA]] and their scale up CPO solution. They didn't necessarily size it. However, I would say **anecdotally, it didn't seem like it's a huge volume opportunity**, or at least they don't have visibility right now in terms of it being a huge volume opportunity. The way that they wanted to frame it was more around, hey, **this is a big BEACHHEAD win for us**, and essentially this will help us win other customers, whether that's [N]PO or CPO, **particularly those customers that maybe have less engineering resources.**"* ➤ **Read against the 08-24/08-25 blocks on this page where [[LITE]] said an NPO deal could be signed "in coming months": the ELS win is strategic, not yet material, and the company itself is positioning it as a reference sale.**
- **LTAs HAVE REACHED THE TRANSCEIVER LINE, WHICH IS NEW.** *"everyone's talking about now signing **multi-year long-term agreements ACROSS THE BOARD**, across their portfolio… **including even some of them suggesting it's on the TRANSCEIVER side as well**"*, and separately that **optical backlog now stretches into 2028**. For [[LITE]]: a **three-year pump-laser LTA** where *"they gave up some incremental pricing in exchange of the long-term commitment, but **there's still price upside**"*, and *"now they have **LTAs across most of their portfolio EXCLUDING transceivers**."* For [[COHR]]: *"a **large percentage of the biz is now covered by long-term agreements**… they actually suggested that they're even **seeing that on the transceiver side** of the business as well"*, plus its own multi-year pump-laser LTA — but *"here it's a bit nuanced in that they're focused more on **CONSUMING A LOT OF THE CAPACITY INTERNALLY** as they push their own amps and line cards and **compete with the networking OEMs more directly**."* ➤ 🔴 **Two suppliers, same LTA wave, opposite intent: [[LITE]] is selling the pump lasers, [[COHR]] is keeping them to go up the stack against the networking OEMs. That is a vertical-integration divergence, not a pricing one.**
- **GUIDANCE MECHANICS, for the models (name-level detail belongs on the company pages):** **[[LITE]] — margin progression slower through calendar 1H27 on transceiver mix, "flattish or inching up through the low 50% margin range", improving in 2H CY27 as higher-margin lines inflect; OCS revenue in the September quarter "low triple digits… and below 150 million a quarter."** **[[COHR]] — revenue +10% q/q through the year until a ~8% sequential in Q4, reaching a ~$3bn exit run rate; gross margin trending ~+20bps per quarter; opex $440mn / $460mn / $490mn; share count +3mn per quarter.**
- **[[COHR]] PRODUCT AND PROCESS MARKS:** **800G transceivers still growing and expected to grow in 2027; 1.6T "early but shipping" with the crossover question sidestepped; 400G and below now starting to DECLINE. Indium phosphide mix today roughly 50% 3-inch / 50% 6-inch, expected to reach 75% 6-inch in calendar 2027. Capex continues to rise quarter over quarter — "they're just midway through their major capacity ramp."** ➤ **The InP 3-inch→6-inch migration is the single largest identified gross-margin lever at [[COHR]] and it is only half done. Worth tracking as a quarterly datapoint.**
- **THE TRANSCEIVER-MARGIN GAP, AND A CEO CHANGING HIS OWN BOGEY.** *"in terms of transceiver margins, [[COHR]] should be much closer to that like **35%** territory… [[LITE]] historically they've talked to us about tracking **sub 30%**… I would say they might even be a little bit even more aggressively lower than that… something in like the **mid 20s**."* 🔴 **"Interesting comment though from them more recently is that Michael [Hurlston] definitely seems anecdotally MORE CONFIDENT around tracking closer to that 30%. I think if you talk to him six months ago, he would have outlined a bogey that's like low 30 at best and suggesting that he'll never be able to reach [[COHR]] at like mid 30 plus… Now… he definitely suggested that they can be in that MID 30s OR HIGH 30s."** The levers he cites: **1.6T ASPs on the technology side ("a lever that they feel very comfortable in"), with MANUFACTURING the constraint — "it seems like their bigger hit point here is manufacturing."** ➤ **A ~10pp transceiver-margin convergence, if it happens, is the largest single earnings swing factor between the two names. It is currently an anecdote about management confidence, not a guide.**
- **AND A HARD MANUFACTURING CONSTRAINT WORTH RECORDING:** asked about Fabrinet doing 1.6T silicon-photonics transceivers, *"I'd push back… because **Michael has told us pretty clearly that they're NOT ALLOWED TO USE CONTRACT MANUFACTURERS FOR TRANSCEIVERS, because [[GOOG]] and [[MSFT]] DON'T WANT THEM TO.**"* ⚠️ **Second-hand from an analyst relaying management. If accurate it is a customer-imposed vertical-integration requirement, which is a materially different thing from a capacity choice — and it constrains how fast transceiver supply can scale.**
- **A NEW [[COHR]] PRODUCT, described but not launched:** "photo link" — *"it's gearing towards customers that… have less engineering resources… going after customers that don't have the same resources that [[NVDA]] has or the same resources at [[TSM]] or the same connections at TSMC for Co[WoS]… **they're creating an integrated package of the OPTICAL ASSEMBLY, LASER, FIBER, DETECTION, PIC as essentially a KIT** that they can sell into new customers"*, with several SKUs and *"some type of new element that they've had in the portfolio that they haven't really discussed as much."* More expected at/around **ECOC**. ➤ **A packaged NPO kit for customers without NVIDIA-scale engineering is the commercial expression of the "merchant optical engine" opportunity — and it explains the M&A pattern: "it's probably some of the reasons why you saw last quarter talk about interest in looking at PICs and why we saw [[CRDO]] acquire [Dust]photonic[s]."**
- **MICRO-LED, WITH TWO NAMED PURSUERS:** *"**Micro LEDs seems like it's becoming more of a reality**… They talked about the similar customers that we've been hearing in terms of both **[[MSFT]] and [[GOOG]] looking to pursue micro LEDs**, and so it does seem like there's some more legs to the micro LED story despite… the debate around the technology and the scalability there."* ✅ **Corroborates the DAMNANG MicroLED/Avicena backfill below from an entirely independent direction — see [outros-asia.md](outros-asia.md).**
- **AND THE DEMAND BACKDROP, which is not an AI-only story:** *"traditional enterprise infrastructure demand is definitely **ramping throughout the entire summer**… there's been a lot of scrutiny… is this really just memory pull through? It seems like every month now we're starting to see more and more data points where **it's not just the memory dynamic that's pulling in some of these orders**… what you're seeing is actually more **secular tailwinds around folks looking to upgrade because of Mythos, because of inferencing and agentic workflows now… hitting their traditional install base. ### THE JALAPEÑO OPTICAL BILL OF MATERIALS — A LAB ASIC PUTS AN OCS IN EVERY RACK

**Source: SemiAnalysis, "OpenAI Jalapeño: Better Than Nvidia Blackwell", 2026-08-25 (complete forwarded-email version).** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_25_ENC_OpenAI_Jalape%C3%B1o_Better_Than_Nvidia_Blackwell_email.html)

- **The topology: a 128-XPU LOCAL domain on passive copper backplane (6,144 differential pairs per rack), and a 2,048-XPU / 16-rack GLOBAL domain at 1.6 Tb/s per XPU using "a combination of copper backplane, electrical 204.8T TH6 switch, 1.6T transceivers, and OPTICAL CIRCUIT SWITCH."** 🔴 *"The global domain adopts a **RAIL-ONLY architecture consisting of 8-rails**… **we think OpenAI routes optical links in the global domain via Optical Circuit Switches (OCS) INSTALLED IN EVERY RACK.** For every XPU, 1.6Tb/s of global bandwidth will travel to the global switch tray over the copper backplane. This then **exits the switch through the front panel via 1.6T transceivers, which go to the passive optical switch before exiting the rack**."*
- ➤ 🔴 **Copper INSIDE the rack, optical BETWEEN racks, with an OCS per rack — and it is a first-generation lab ASIC, not a merchant roadmap. This is an independent instance of the copper-then-optics sequencing this page has run since March, arriving from a new architecture. ⚠️ SemiAnalysis flags the OCS routing as inference ("we think"), not disclosure.**
- **The economic frame, which cuts against reading interconnect as a large cost line:** *"because **scale-up networking is only about 10% of total system cost**, that flexibility buys valuable optionality for future 10-20 trillion parameter models or 2-4 million token context windows."* ⚠️ **~10% is SemiAnalysis's figure for THIS system; do not generalise to NVL72-class racks.**

### AND A COMPANY-PRIMARY DATAPOINT: [[SAMSUNG]] PUTS SILICON PHOTONICS ON ITS *MATURE* FOUNDRY NODES

**Source: [[SAMSUNG]] Electronics IR — group call hosted by BTG, raw notes, 2026-08-25 (⚠️ company management primary; machine transcript).** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Samsung_IR_call_BTG_raw_notes_25_ago_26.html)

Asked whether 2027 foundry growth is mix-driven or capacity-driven: *"in the near term, we believe that other[wise] advanced node momentum will drive overall foundry growth, especially on two nanometer… **However, on the MATURE NODE, we are also quite working on there. One of the examples is SILICON PHOTONICS. And we believe that going forward this photonics, the order could be another momentum for us to drive. We can also UTILIZE OUR LEGACY NODE to address this silicon photonics market.**"*

➤ 🔴 **A third volume silicon-photonics foundry option is being stood up on depreciated capacity, by a company with its own memory, packaging and 2nm logic.** ⚠️ **No customer, no volume, no node, no date — a strategic statement of intent only.** ➤ **Its significance is competitive: this page's SiPh-foundry scarcity argument (which the DAMNANG [[TSEM]] backfill below states in its strongest form — "the de facto sole volume supplier of 1.6T PICs") is a thesis about the number of qualified suppliers. Samsung saying it will use legacy nodes for SiPh is the first sighting of that number potentially going up. Logged as a WATCH ITEM, not a change to the scarcity call.**

In one line: independent Korean semiconductor Substack, NOT a broker, no rating, no PT, no formal estimate.** ⚠️ **His 2026-08-04 "The Trigger for Phase Two of the Optical Trade Was Geopolitics, Not Technology" was already ingested on the [[AAOI]], [[COHR]] and [[MRVL]] company pages and is deliberately NOT re-logged here.**

### 🔴🔴 THE FOUNDING EVENT OF THIS THEME'S CURRENT PHASE: **THREE OPTICAL MSAs ON ONE DAY, 2026-03-12**

**DAMNANG (Substack) — "The War of Light Has Begun: Why Three Optical MSAs Were Formed at the Same Time", 2026-03-16, and "The War of Light, Part 2: Who's at the Table, Who's Not, and Who's Hiding", 2026-03-17.** *"Three separate optical-related MSAs dropped on the same day. That does not happen by coincidence."*

| MSA | Founding members | What it standardises |
|---|---|---|
| **OCI** (Optical Compute Interconnect) | **[[AMD]], [[AVGO]], [[META]], [[MSFT]], [[NVDA]], [[OPENAI]]** | The **common optical PHY** for GPU↔GPU and GPU↔scale-up-switch inside the scale-up domain. **NRZ + WDM; GEN1 200 Gbps/direction, GEN2 400 Gbps/direction (800 Gbps per fiber), roadmap beyond 3.2 Tbps.** Protocol-agnostic — works under NVLink or UALink. Supports pluggable, on-board optics and CPO. |
| **Open CPX** (Open Co-packaging) | **Ciena, [[COHR]], [[MRVL]], Molex, Samtec, TeraHop** | The **physical form factor of the optical engine**: socket, connector, thermal management, electrical pinout, optical interface between ASIC and CPO/NPO engine. *"OCI says WHAT optical signal to use; Open CPX says HOW to physically plug that optical engine next to the ASIC."* |
| **XPO** (eXtra-dense Pluggable Optics) | Led by **[[ANET]]**, founding members incl. Lightmatter, Eoptolink, TeraHop; **>40 optical module vendors have joined** | A next-generation pluggable beyond OSFP. **Target 12.8 Tbps per module and 204.8 Tbps per OCP rack unit — 4x the front-panel density of OSFP. Integrated liquid-cooled cold plate handling >400W per module. One XPO replaces eight OSFP. Arista claims XPO cuts switch rack count by 75% in a 400MW AI data center. Volume production targeted for 2027.** |

- 🔴 **THE MEMBERSHIP READ, WHICH IS THE ANALYTICALLY USEFUL PART.** **NVIDIA and AMD sit at the same OCI table because they compete at the PROTOCOL layer (NVLink vs UALink) and cooperate at the PHY** — *"If you keep your protocol proprietary while opening up the PHY, you get two things at once: a **multi-vendor optical supply chain that drives down component costs**, and you **keep the NVLink lock-in intact**."* **Three hyperscalers/labs at the table is the departure from precedent: "the buyers writing the spec themselves."** And the absences carry information: **[[AVGO]] and [[NVDA]] are NOT in Open CPX** — Broadcom's CPO is *"three generations deep in the Tomahawk series, vertically integrated with their own switch ASICs. They don't need to agree with anyone else on what the optical engine socket looks like. Worse, **if a standard socket gets defined, it undercuts the integration advantage that Broadcom has spent years building**"* — while **[[MRVL]] is in, having closed the $3.25bn Celestial AI acquisition in February 2026**, giving it reason to shape how a standard optical-engine interface gets defined around technology it now owns.
- 🔴 **AND THE HOCK TAN EPISODE, WHICH IS THE BEST-DOCUMENTED CPO-TIMING MISREAD ON RECORD.** In early March 2026 Broadcom's CEO said on the earnings call: *"**We will push copper in scale-up as long as we can. CPO is coming. Not this year, probably not next year either. Silicon photonics becoming meaningful is not far off, but not yet.**"* **Optical stocks dropped more than 10% across the board.** *"Then, one week later, Broadcom CO-FOUNDED the OCI MSA. At OFC 2026, the company unveiled a **CPO-integrated Tomahawk 6 switch, the industry's first 400G/lane optical DSP, its own 400G EML laser, and a VCSEL-based NPO.**"* His reading: *"The market misread what Tan actually said… **He was talking about TIMING, not direction.** His 'not yet' horizon runs to roughly 2028… **'Copper versus optical' is not a binary choice. It is a SEQUENCE.**"* ✅✅ **Five and a half months later JPM reports the supplier ecosystem in "consensus that CPO is not delayed, material year calendar 2028" — the same date Broadcom implied and the market mispriced. Filed as the standing warning against reading a timing statement as a directional one; the same error is available to make again on every "800V is delayed" and "de-spec" headline this wiki carries.**
- **THE PHYSICS BEHIND THE 2027-28 DATE, in three constraints that converge together:** **reach** (*"Above 800 Gbps, copper cable maxes out at a few meters… This is why NVL72's copper connections are confined to a single rack. Multi-rack scale-up requires optical"*); **power** (*"copper networking already consumes more than half of data center network power"*); and **bandwidth density** (*"a hard physical ceiling on how much I/O bandwidth you can pull off an ASIC shoreline using copper"*). Quantified elsewhere in the corpus: **at 800G, DAC reaches several metres; at 1.6T (200G × 8) usable distance shrinks to under one metre and needs a retimer; at 3.2T (400G × 8) copper struggles at distances measured in centimetres** ("The War of Light, A Laser Shortage", 2026-03-31).
- **And a TAM marker from the same piece:** *"The **LightCounting CEO has projected that annual CPO/NPO port shipments will exceed 100 MILLION UNITS within five years.**"* ⚠️ **Third-party projection quoted by an independent author — treat as a directional marker, not an estimate this wiki holds.**

### THE LASER SHORTAGE, AND WHAT NVIDIA PAID TO LOCK IT UP

**DAMNANG (Substack) — "The War of Light, A Laser Shortage: A Complete Breakdown of the AI Data Center Laser Market", 2026-03-31.**

- 🔴 **THE CAPITAL EVENT, dated:** *"On **March 2, 2026, [[NVDA]] invested $2B in [[COHR]] and another $2B in [[LITE]]**. Both companies make lasers. The two announcements came on the same day and included **'multi-billion dollar purchase commitments' and 'FUTURE CAPACITY ACCESS RIGHTS.' Jensen Huang wasn't just buying lasers. He was locking up the ability to make them.**"* With the Ayar Labs Series E participation, *"more than **$4B committed to the optical supply chain**."* His framing of the strategy as three reinforcing layers: **lock in supply through investment, drive down component cost through standardisation (OCI), and keep the protocol lock-in (NVLink) on top.**
- **And the thesis in one line:** *"**If HBM was the bottleneck of 2023 to 2024, LASERS are the bottleneck of 2025 to 2027.** And this optical market is more complicated than HBM. There are four types of lasers, various types of modulators, and three form factors."* ✅ **Five months later the JPM call above reports exactly that: a merchant-CW supply imbalance being converted into price concessions across OCS, transceivers and EML. The bottleneck moved to where he said it would.**

### THE INVESTMENT MAP, AND THE ONE RESULT THAT SHOULD SURVIVE IT

**DAMNANG (Substack) — "Optical Investment Map Stock Review: Who Moved and Who's Next?", 2026-05-01** (reviewing his own v1.0 map of 2026-04-19).

- **The v1.0 thesis: "vertical integration depth and stock returns move in an INVERSE relationship."** On one-year returns, **4-position-deep names (Sumitomo, Nokia, [[CSCO]]) averaged ~+78% while some 2-position pure-plays ran +900% to +1,700%** — *"a paradox where the deepest integration produced the lowest returns."*
- **On a ONE-MONTH window to 2026-04-30 the category ordering held but flipped internally: 4-pos +23%, 3-pos Upstream +46%, 3-pos Downstream +73%, 2-pos Materials-Device +96%** — with Downstream IC-to-Photonics overtaking Upstream Natives for the first time.
- 🔴 **THE FINDING WORTH KEEPING, which is his own falsification of his own framework:** *"Where it broke is INSIDE the categories. Within 4-pos, only Nokia moved. **Within Materials-Device, Sivers and IPG diverged by roughly 100x** [IPG +3% vs Sivers +327%]. Category averages remain meaningful, but **WHICH CHOKEPOINT A COMPANY CONTROLS turned out to be the bigger variable at the stock level.** For stock-level decisions, **chokepoint ownership, end-market exposure, and narrative fit rank HIGHER than category classification.**"* ➤ **That is the correct way to read every "optical basket" pitch, including the ones on this page. ⚠️ All returns are self-reported, measured in one of the strongest tapes on record, and are ~4 months stale.**

### THE OFF-COVERAGE OPTICAL NAMES, EACH WITH A DATED SANITY CHECK

- **[[TSEM]] — "Tower Semiconductor: Everything You Need to Know", 2026-03-20.** *"It is the **de facto sole volume supplier of 1.6T PICs**, and it serves as the **primary foundry for both pluggable transceivers and CPO**."* Business: specialty analog — SiGe, RF CMOS, BCD, CMOS image sensors — across 200mm fabs in Migdal Haemek and Newport Beach, TPSCo 200/300mm in Japan and a shared 300mm with STMicroelectronics in Italy. **FY2025 revenue ~$1.44bn; Q4-2025 $440mn, a record quarter.** The Intel history: *"In February 2022, [[INTC]] announced it would acquire Tower for **$5.4B**. The deal collapsed in August 2023 after failing to clear Chinese regulatory approval… **The fact that Intel was willing to spend $5.4B to acquire Tower is itself proof of how irreplaceable its specialty analog technology and fab infrastructure are.**"* After the break-up the two kept a *"capacity corridor"* partnership but *"the practical integration of their different process systems has been difficult to execute"*, and Tower *"appears to have redirected its strategy toward expanding its Japanese TPSCo fabs."* ⚠️ **All financials are ~5 months stale. ➤ Hold the "sole volume supplier" claim against the [[SAMSUNG]] mature-node silicon-photonics statement above.**
- **[[POET]] — "Is POET a brilliant opportunity or pure wishful thinking?", 2026-03-16.** ⚠️⚠️ **The valuation fact he opens with: "Market cap around $1 billion. Last quarter's revenue: $298,434."** His structural read is not a technology verdict: *"POET carries nearly every attribute the current market rewards… **the company generates INTERPRETABLE NEWS rather than confirmed results.** Names like Foxconn, Luxshare, Mitsubishi, Semtech, Sivers, NTT, and Quantum Computing Inc. appear repeatedly across partnership and development announcements. **Because actual revenue is minimal, the market tends to read these names as HARBINGERS OF FUTURE CONTRACTS rather than evidence of current ones.**"* And the specific open question: whether [[MRVL]] *"will continue using POET at volume production or replace it with in-house capability through the Inphi acquisition remains an open question."* ⚠️ **He also notes POET is NOT at any of the three MSA tables — "the company is not writing the standards; it is one of the players that will need to conform to them."** ➤ **His 05-22 scorecard marks POET +111% and attributes several of the biggest swings to CFO-related events rather than technology — "a reminder of how much NON-TECHNICAL risk can hit a stock."**
- **LWLG (Lightwave Logic — no wiki page, plain text) — "LWLG Structural Technology Risk Analysis: How Far Can Polymer Modulators Really Go?", 2026-04-12.** ⚠️⚠️ **The gap he documents: "the stock surged more than 40% intraday the day after the announcement, pushing the market cap past $1.5B. But **FY2025 revenue was $236,855. The company has 34 employees. A working transceiver has never been publicly demonstrated.**"** The announcements in question were **a development agreement with [[TSEM]] and the design becoming available in the GlobalFoundries PDK** — *"a foundry announcement does not equal product adoption, and **PDK availability does not equal production qualification**."* Competitive context from the same OFC: **HyperLight demonstrated a TFLN-based 1.6T transceiver; OpenLight unveiled a III-V heterogeneously integrated SiPh PIC with lasers and modulators on one chip; China's Liobate exhibited 1.6T and 3.2T DR8 TFLN modulator lines.** ➤ **The only NEGATIVE call in his 05-22 scorecard (LWLG −13%) and the one he uses to illustrate that "the market is always an unpredictable creature" — the stock spiked to $18 on a Marvell/Polariton acquisition narrative before returning to ~$12.**
- **Sivers Semiconductors (no wiki page, plain text) — "Sivers Semiconductors: Verifying the Thesis After a 2,100% Run in Six Months", 2026-05-27** (co-authored with NUTTY and PhotonCap). **Up 2,104.31% in six months; SIVE.ST closed SEK 86.85 on 2026-05-26; SEK 306.6mn of revenue (~$33mn) against a market cap above $2.5bn — ~80x trailing P/S, loss-making.** The thesis: **CPO cannot put the laser inside the GPU package, so External Light Sources are required, and one ELS approach is a multi-wavelength array delivering several wavelengths from a single source.** *"What the market has started paying attention to is not 'optical growth' in general, but **LASER SOURCE SCARCITY IN THE CPO ERA**."* The operating-leverage argument: *"[[LITE]] and [[COHR]] can build array light sources, but for those companies, an ELS array is ONE LINE inside a massive portfolio. For Sivers at a $2.5B market cap, the same ELS ramp is an inflection point that transforms the entire revenue structure."* Peer marks: **QD Laser (TYO: 6613) outputs 5-16mW per channel, "not been enough to secure a CPO ELS production customer"; Aeluma (ALMU) grows III-V directly on Si but FY26 Q3 revenue of $1.2mn came mainly from R&D contracts.** ⚠️⚠️ **His own disclaimers are severe and should travel with the entry: financial restatements during PCAOB alignment, allegations of information leakage ahead of a potential Nasdaq dual listing, "a Swedish-listed small-cap with limited liquidity, a pre-production money-losing company with extreme price volatility." All marks are three months stale.** Off-coverage treatment on [outros-asia.md](outros-asia.md).
- **[[AAOI]] — "Citron Sees the Valuation, but Misses the Technology: A Technical Rebuttal to the AAOI Short Report", 2026-04-10.** Written the morning Andrew Left posted a short position calling the stock *"delusional."* His concession: *"Looking at a stock that ripped 73% in two weeks and flagging valuation pressure is exactly what a short seller is supposed to do."* His objection is narrow and technical: *"This report didn't stop at a valuation call. **It effectively labeled AAOI as a 'commodity economics' business.** And that's where it gets thin. **AAOI is not a company that buys off-the-shelf lasers and bolts together modules… It DESIGNS AND FABRICATES ITS OWN LASERS AND OPTICAL SUBASSEMBLIES IN HOUSE.** In LPO, the removal of the DSP raises the quality bar across the entire analog link. In CPO, the importance of external light sources and package-level optical integration only grows."* Marks: **~$456mn of 2025 revenue with the company indicating it expects to exceed $1bn in 2026.** ⚠️ **He is explicit about what the piece is not: "It is not an argument that AAOI is cheap. It is not a claim that the current stock price is automatically justified."** ⚠️ **Four months stale.**
- **[[CRDO]] and [[ALAB]] — "Credo Technology Deep Dive" (2026-04-14) and "Catching Both Copper and Optics: ALAB and CRDO" (2026-06-09).** The technical moat: **SerDes IP designed in-house across every generation from 28Gbps to 224Gbps, with ~500+ of 622 employees being engineers**, and the AEC as the product it built on that — *"a small chip inside the connector at each end… Because the chip regenerates the signal mid-path, you can use **thin copper wire (32AWG) and still transmit up to 7 meters, while cutting cable bulk by up to 75% compared to DAC**… In 120kW and 200kW racks where liquid cooling pipes and cables compete for the same physical space, this is the difference between being able to build the system or not."* Plus the reliability argument Credo calls **"ZeroFlap."** Marks: **FY26 Q3 revenue $407mn, +201% y/y.** The pair framing: *"**[[ALAB]] binds tightly clustered GPUs into a single block. [[CRDO]] links servers and racks that sit far apart.** They do different jobs, they win customers in different ways, and the structure of how they make money is different"* — with the market's habit of assigning them similar multiples *"right up to a point."* ⚠️ **Both are ~2.5-4.5 months stale; the [[CRDO]] and [[ALAB]] marks on this wiki's company pages supersede.** ➤ **The durable content is the ALAB-vs-CRDO position map and the AEC physical-constraint argument, which is the counterweight to the "copper is finished" framing — and which Mizuho/SA revived on 08-24 with the CRDO AEC upside call above.**



## Recent signals (Aug 25, /wiki-ingest — THE TAM GOES TO ~$100bn, NPO IS PULLED FORWARD A YEAR, AND AMD SAYS THE NEXT GENERATION GOES OPTICAL) — 🔴🔴 **FOUR INDEPENDENT SOURCES IN ONE DAY MOVE THE SAME THREE VARIABLES: TAM UP, TIMELINE FORWARD, AND THE UPSTREAM SUBSTRATE BOTTLENECK QUANTIFIED FOR THE FIRST TIME.**

- 🔴 **TAM AND TIMING — [[LITE]] management to Barclays** (Tom O'Malley, Kyle Bleustein, Matthew Pan, Trip Smith, *"Quick Takes from Calls with LITE & SNDK Mgmt."*, 2026-08-25): *"**Total TAM closer to $100B (vs. $90B prior) with a CAGR between 45-50%** as **scale-up may have been undersized in prior estimates**, with increasing likelihood that **scale-up could be nearly HALF THE SIZE OF SCALE-OUT by CY30**."* And the two timeline items: **"CPO demand has been UP-SIZED from the prior agreement with its largest customer"** and **"NPO has been PULLED FORWARD from CY29 to CY28 with customers looking to deploy for scale-up immediately."** A new term is added to the taxonomy: **"scale-IN" = memory disaggregation within systems**, which LITE says *"enhances the use of more optical links within memory architectures."* ⚠️ **The note's gross-margin paragraph is truncated in the distributed summary — *"Similar to [[COHR]], the GM profile of 800G…"* — and was NOT read here.**
- 🔴 **THE ARCHITECTURAL CONFIRMATION, FROM A COMPUTE VENDOR RATHER THAN AN OPTICS VENDOR.** At Hot Chips 2026, **[[AMD]] said Helios "is near the practical limit for an ALL-ELECTRICAL scale-up fabric. Electrical connectivity forces GPUs, switches and cabling into a very dense physical configuration because reach is limited. AMD expects to begin introducing OPTICAL SCALE-UP in the following generation, which should loosen the coupling between the compute topology and rack layout."** It adds a second-order consequence worth carrying: *"Asked how rack power evolves, AMD said… **it does not expect a substantial increase in power density from current levels**. The move to optical scale-up should **reduce the need to keep pushing physical density simply to preserve signal reach**"* (TMT Breakout Hot Chips recap, 2026-08-25). ➤ **A named vendor putting a generation stamp on the copper→optical scale-up transition — the single most useful timing datapoint on this page since the CPO mass-production announcement.**
- 🔴 **THE UPSTREAM BOTTLENECK, QUANTIFIED.** *"The industry is passing through an **acute chokepoint for indium phosphide (InP), the substrate on which the lasers are built**. AI datacenter demand has pushed **EML supply ~30% BELOW NEED**, per Customer L, with **InP substrate highly consolidated ([[AXTI]], Sumitomo)** and tightened further by **China's Feb-25 export controls**. Vendors like Customer L are therefore **channeling capital into UPSTREAM WAFER FABS**, where the bottleneck sits, and are likely to **outsource back-end assembly**"* — to Malaysian assembler Nationgate, which has *"existing optical experience with [[COHR]]"*, has committed **~500k sq ft (Plants 11A/11B) to this customer alone**, ramping from a **4Q26 pilot line** toward **~US$1bn at peak** (JPM · Samuel Tan, Gokul Hariharan, Yen Voo, Khai Seang Lee, upgrade to OW, PT RM0.90 → RM2.40, 2026-08-25). ⚠️ **"Customer L" is JPM's redaction; the detail points to [[LITE]] but the identification is inferred, not stated.**
- 🔴 **THE POLICY LEG, WHICH IS NEW TO THIS PAGE.** *"A **draft FCC rule to ban imports of new Chinese optical TRx destined for US data centers** further fuels the trend of US suppliers shifting manufacturing out of China toward **Malaysia, Vietnam and Thailand**. With **Chinese suppliers holding c.35% of a $50bn+ optical datacom market (of which c.50%+ is US revenue, per JPMe), the reshoring opportunity for non-China players approaches ~$10bn**"* (same note). ⚠️ **A draft rule, not enacted.**
- **MERCHANT LASER SUPPLY IS ALREADY RATIONING.** *"$AAOI stated **multiple customers approaching them for CPO lasers (but had to turn them away)**; $MTSI stated many customers approaching them for **CW capacity (but doesn't come online until H2 2027)**"* (@aleabitoreddit, corpus, 2026-08-25) ⚠️ *corpus relay of prior company statements.* From the system side, **[[LITE]] pump-laser shipments are up >80% y/y with a target of ~4x over the next several quarters, supported by additional LTAs; "optical components remain constrained, leaving suppliers such as LITE and COHR with PRICING POWER"** (MS · Meta Marshall, [[CIEN]] preview, 2026-08-25).

## Recent signals (Aug 25, /ingest — CORPORATE ACCESS, [[AIXA]] IR) — 🔴🔴 **THE UPSTREAM TOOL LAYER JUST TOLD THE MARKET THAT TOOL INTENSITY PER UNIT OF OPTICAL DEMAND CAN *FALL* — WHICH PUTS A VARIABLE MULTIPLIER INSIDE EVERY "TRANSCEIVER UNITS × SHARE" BRIDGE ON THIS PAGE**

- **Source: AIXTRON IR · Christian Ludwig (VP Investor Relations) at the Jefferies Semis Conference, 2026-08-25** (corporate-access note). [[AIXA]] is the MOCVD/epitaxy tool layer of the InP laser chain this page has tracked all month — near-100% InP MOCVD share.
- 🔴🔴 **THE NEW MECHANIC, AND IT CUTS AGAINST THE PAGE'S DIRECTION OF TRAVEL: management says C28 opto is "harder to frame" because LARGER WAFER SIZES AND IMPROVING 6-INCH YIELDS COULD *REDUCE* TOOL INTENSITY — with the offset being ELS/UHP scale-up consuming MATERIALLY LARGER InP WAFER AREA. Management would not say which side wins.** ➜ **This page has spent two months establishing that the binding constraints in the optical chain sit upstream — InP substrate (Jul 31), then the MOCVD tool itself (Aug 1: "customers are SHORT MOCVD MACHINES", slots reserved into 2027). The tool vendor now says its own unit demand is NOT a fixed function of transceiver units: wafer-size migration and yield are DEFLATIONARY to tool count per unit of end demand, while ELS/UHP area is INFLATIONARY.** **Practical consequence for this page: a units forecast (e.g. Jefferies' "transceiver units ~5x 2025→2028") no longer maps linearly to epi-tool demand, and the InP-area intensity of the ELS/UHP architectures becomes a variable worth tracking on its own — it is the leg that decides whether the upstream squeeze persists into C28 or self-relieves.**
- 🔴 **THE C27 NUMBERS, WHICH ARE A CLEAN UNIT READ ON THE OPTICAL CHAIN: opto revenue ~€480-500m on ~120 TOOLS in C27, versus 60-80 TOOLS in C26 — a ~1.5-2.0x step in tool count** — inside a total C27 revenue pathway of ~€800m. **Implied ASP ~€4.0-4.2m/tool** (our arithmetic: €480-500m ÷ 120). ➜ **This is the cleanest published proxy on this page for how fast InP epi CAPACITY is actually being added: ~120 MOCVD tools of C27 shipments against 60-80 in C26.** Pair it with the substrate leg — InP substrate/epi prices taking a **3rd hike this year, another 10%+ expected in 4Q26** (UDN via Jefferies · O'Mara, 2026-08-17) — and the read is that **capacity is being added at roughly 1.5-2x the prior-year rate and prices are still rising**, i.e. the squeeze is not yet relieved by the C27 tool wave.
- ⚠️ **AND THE PRICING SIGNAL FROM THE CHOKEPOINT IS *DOWN*, NOT UP, WHICH IS COUNTER-INTUITIVE FOR A SOLD-OUT MONOPOLY: management says moving a customer from 1-2 to 10-20 tools "requires discounts", and that a prior attempted price push sent a customer to VEECO — so it now prioritises retaining ~90% share over price.** ➜ **Read against the Aug 1 item on this page ("demand outstrips supply, customers reserving slots into next year"): the tool vendor is NOT monetising the shortage through price. That matters for the chain — it means the InP cost curve for [[LITE]]/[[COHR]]/[[AAOI]] is NOT being inflated at the tool layer, and the substrate/epi price hikes documented above are a substrate-layer phenomenon, not a pass-through from equipment.**
- **Order-book context, for dating the capacity wave: management is "very confident" in ~€200m/quarter of intake "for the next few quarters", and ~€190m of ADVANCE PAYMENTS already represents "almost half of backlog"** ➜ backlog backs out to **~€380-420m** (our arithmetic) vs €287m at Q3/25 — **but 2H27 and C28 orders are NOT YET BOOKED**, and ~€200m/Q annualises to a **~1.0x book-to-bill** against the €800m C27 target. **Customer forecasts "have strengthened materially since May."**
- ⚠️ **CURRENCY BASIS: the source note writes "$" throughout for a EUR reporter; carried here as EUR. The decisive, FX-rate-robust check is the intake figure — €200m/Q is a step UP from Q1'26's €171m, while $200m is €167-190m across a EURUSD 1.05-1.20 band, a 12-22% DECLINE from the €215m Q2'26 actual at every rate. The revenue check (€800m ≈ the €794m CY27 consensus) only corroborates — as USD it is 4-16% below consensus, inconclusive at the weak end of the band. Unconfirmed with IR — do not model off it yet.** Full detail on [../AIXA.md](../AIXA.md).

## Recent signals (Aug 19, /run-inbox) — ✅🔴🔴 **THE "SOURCE UNCONFIRMED" VIRGO EXCERPT ON THIS PAGE IS RESOLVED: IT IS *FUNDA*. AND THE 6D-TORUS CALL GETS ITS MISSING ARITHMETIC — WRAP-AROUND OPTICAL PORTS PER TPU GO **1.5 → 6 (4x)**, WHICH IS *NOT* THE SAME NUMBER AS SemiAnalysis's "ICI LINKS 6 → 12" (2x). PLUS THE FIRST *RATED* SELL-SIDE VOTE ON THE THESIS: GF SECURITIES, **[[LITE]] BUY / [[COHR]] HOLD**.**

**Sources: **FUNDA**, "Deep|Optics: 6D Torus Points to a 4x Uplift in Optical Content per TPU", **2026-08-19** (independent paid Substack — **no rating, PT, estimates or model**; premise explicitly *"supply-chain rumors"*). **GF Securities (HK) · Alicia Xia / Jeff Pu / Evan Lee**, "AI Networking: Google's 6D Torus & Beneficiaries", **2026-08-19** (sector report, Networking / Overseas Technology, benchmark Hang Seng; **ratings on suppliers only — LITE Buy, COHR Hold — no price targets and no estimates**).**

- ✅🔴 **THE ATTRIBUTION FIX, AND IT DIRECTLY DISCHARGES THIS PAGE'S OWN STANDING INSTRUCTION.** The **"Recent signals (Aug 18, companion excerpt)"** block below carries the caveat *"⚠️ SOURCE NOT STATED IN THE PASTE — attribution UNCONFIRMED… Do not cite the analysis to a house until the source is verified."* **It is FUNDA.** Today's note reproduces that passage essentially verbatim — the *"precisely the third use case in Lumentum's OFC framework (Figure 2 below)"* phrasing, the **0.2:1 → 1:1 "fivefold increase"**, the **1:1–1:2 → 1:3–1:4** module ratio, **in-rack switches one → four-six per rack**, **800G → 1.6T**, and the **130K+ TPUs at ~47 Pb/s** anchor — and cross-references FUNDA's own earlier report *"Deep|AI Infra 2026: Shifting from 'Brain Power' Competition to 'Whole-Body' Evolution"* (FUNDA, **2026-04-14**). ➤ **Cite that block to FUNDA. It is an independent paid Substack with no rating/PT/estimates — NOT a bank, and NOT SemiAnalysis. The ratio FRAMEWORK stays Lumentum's own (OFC, Figure 2); the Virgo application is FUNDA's.** ⚠️ **The uncomfortable consequence, which should be said because this page treated them as mutually reinforcing: the two 08-18 blocks were **SemiAnalysis + FUNDA**, not two independent confirmations of each other's numbers. The scale-up half (SemiAnalysis) and the scale-out half (FUNDA) are separate sources on separate axes. Today's GF note is the first genuinely independent third vote — and the first from a licensed house with ratings attached.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE NET-NEW ARITHMETIC, AND IT IS THE PART THAT CHANGES THE INVESTABLE NUMBER. Hold one thing fixed: the building block is still a 64-chip rack; what changes is how those 64 chips are organised.** In **3D (4×4×4)** each torus dimension is a closed ring of **edge length k=4**; adjacent chips inside a ring connect over short copper/PCB and **only the wrap-around link that closes the ring must leave the rack optically**, so optical ports per chip per dimension = **2/k = 0.5**, i.e. **1.5 across three dimensions** — *"the 64 chips form a solid cube: most chips are interior nodes connected entirely over copper."* In **6D (2⁶)** the **edge length falls to k=2**; in a ring of two **both chips are boundary chips and there are no interior nodes**, so **2/2 = 1 optical port per chip per dimension = 6 in total**, and *"100% of chips need optical connectivity."* ➤ **"Why 4x rather than the intuitive 2x?… The key is not the number of dimensions but the EDGE LENGTH of each dimension." The 4x is the product of two 2x factors: dimensions ×2 (3→6) AND ports-per-dimension ×2 (0.5→1).** GF derives the identical result from the other end: **external optical links per 64-chip block rise 96 → 384**, per-chip ports **1.5 → 6**; with scale-out at ~**1:2.5** it puts the **aggregate TPU-to-port ratio at 1:8.5**.
- ⚠️🔴 **UNIT GUARD — THIS PAGE NOW CARRIES A "2x" AND A "4x" FOR THE SAME ARCHITECTURE AND THEY ARE NOT IN CONFLICT. SemiAnalysis (08-18): the 6D move "doubles each chip's ICI links from 6 to 12" — that is **total ICI links, copper + optical**, so 2x. FUNDA/GF (08-19): **wrap-around OPTICAL ports** go 1.5 → 6 — 4x. FUNDA states both in the same note (*"ICI links per chip doubling from 6 to 12"* alongside *"each TPU requires six optical ports"*). The optical share rises faster than the link count precisely because edge length collapses and interior nodes disappear. ➤ **Never present 2x and 4x as competing estimates of one quantity, and never average them. The optical-supply-chain number is the 4x on PORTS.**
- 🔴 **THE RATIO-LADDER MAPPING, WHICH IS THE SINGLE MOST USEFUL THING IN TODAY'S NOTE FOR THIS PAGE, BECAUSE IT CONVERTS A MARKETING RANGE INTO A TESTABLE MAP.** Lumentum's OFC slide gives **Scale-Up today 1.5:1** (driven by *"topology enablement and cluster resiliency"*), **future Scale-Up 2:1 to 10:1** (*"increased bandwidth; multiple customers and use cases"*), and **Spine/Super-Spine 0.2:1 → 1:1** — but, in FUNDA's words, *"Lumentum gave the ratio range without spelling out the architectural assumption behind it; higher dimensionality is what fills that range in: **4D maps to 3:1, 6D to 6:1, and 10D to 10:1**."* ➤ **Two consequences. (1) The 3D→6D step corresponds to a 4x rise in OCS ports and module content per TPU (~6.7x if the path extends to 10D). (2) The TOP of Lumentum's own published range implies a TEN-DIMENSIONAL torus, which nobody is forecasting — so treat 6:1 as the aggressive-but-derivable case and 10:1 as an architectural ceiling, not a base case. That is a materially more disciplined way to use the slide than quoting "up to 10:1."**
- ⚠️🔴 **AND A BASELINE TRAP FUNDA CALLS OUT THAT THIS PAGE SHOULD ADOPT AS A STANDING GUARD, BECAUSE IT WOULD OTHERWISE PRODUCE A FALSE BEAR ON 8i.** *"The right baseline for 8i is Google's long-standing small serving architecture rather than the 8,000–9,000-chip training superpod."* The prior small pod was **256 chips (four 64-chip racks) connected ENTIRELY over copper — a scale-up optical-module and OCS-port ratio of ZERO**; 8i takes **all inter-rack connectivity optical, to roughly 1.25:1.** *"Anyone benchmarking 8i against the large superpod and concluding that optical intensity is down has simply picked the wrong baseline."* ➤ **8i is a copper-to-optics transition off a zero base, NOT a step-down from the 1.5:1 training figure. Different denominators — do not net them.** Related, and consistent with the OCS-as-structural-dependency framing already on this page: **8i's Boardfly interconnects 36 groups through OCS with direct optical links, cutting network diameter from ~16 to ~7 hops** — *"turning OCS from an optional component into a structural dependency."*
- 💰 **THE COST FRAME — carried with FUNDA's own label attached, because the label is load-bearing: "a very optimistic, upper-bound estimate."** On a **fully configured 6D torus**, **~$13,700 of optical + OCS content per TPU**, covering **optical modules, OCS ports and cabling ONLY and excluding switching equipment**, at assumed ASPs of **~$700 for 1.6T DR8 (IMDD)** and **~$1,500 for 2.4T Coherent Lite**. Mapping caveat verbatim: the four scale-out modules sit at both ends of in-rack-switch→leaf→spine links while **OCS is deployed only at the spine, so ~4 modules map to ~1 OCS port, not one-to-one.** ⚠️ **The ~2.1x IMDD→Coherent-Lite ASP step is the single most important assumption behind "the value uplift would exceed 4x" — it is a FUNDA estimate, not a quoted price. Add switching back and FUNDA says the order of magnitude resembles per-accelerator networking cost in [[NVDA]]'s NVL576 — but at very different scale-up domain sizes, so it is not like-for-like.**
- **THE COHERENT-LITE LEG GETS A PHYSICAL MECHANISM AND A DATE, and it is self-reinforcing in a way worth noting:** once OCS ratios rise sharply, **insertion loss (Palomar ~2 dB; Lumentum R300 1.5–3 dB) consumes IMDD's already-limited link budget (~4 dB for FR4, zero margin in the worst case)** — so **Coherent Lite begins adoption with 2.4T systems in 2027 and fully transitions at 3.2T (400G/lane) in 2028**, reinforced as scale-up superpods grow from **9,600 (8t) toward 10,000+ and 20,000+** where reach matters. ➤ **The loop: more OCS attach consumes more link budget, which pulls the coherent transition EARLIER. OCS growth and the coherent transition are not independent forecasts — they are coupled, and this page has been treating them as separate legs.**
- **SUPPLIER MAP AS PUBLISHED (product positions, not sizing):** **OCS systems** — [[LITE]] (*"R300 deployed at Google; next-generation product in customer sampling"*), [[COHR]] (*"300×300 DLX"*), InnoLight; **modules & lasers** — InnoLight (IMDD + Coherent Lite), LITE (*"EMLs and transceivers to Google; 200G EML capacity … through the end of 2026"*), COHR (*"EMLs plus transceivers; first to introduce a 400G D-EML"*); **DSP / Coherent-Lite ecosystem** — [[MRVL]] (*"a long-standing optical DSP supplier to Google"*), Nokia/Infinera (*"InP PIC in volume production; has secured Google WAN orders"*), Celero, Alphawave; **upstream materials** — [[AXTI]] (InP substrates), Soitec (engineered substrates), Semtech (TIA/driver AFE); **ODMs** — Celestica, Jabil, [[FLEX]]. GF's recommendation list: *"OCS supply chain: **Lumentum (LITE Buy) & Coherent (COHR Hold)**"* plus an unnamed *"2.4T coherent lite transceiver supplier."* ⚠️ **A beneficiary list is not a share estimate. Nothing here is sized per name, and the ODM/materials names carry no claim beyond category membership.**
- **The strategic frame, which survives even if 6D never ships and is the best one-line answer to "why does Google keep buying optics":** *"A single TPU is unlikely to outperform NVIDIA's flagship GPU on raw silicon, and Google has never competed chip-for-chip"* — it uses **proprietary OCS plus bidirectional (bidi) modules** (transmit and receive on one fibre, holding cost and fibre count down) to build a scale-up domain far larger than competitors' and **competes at the cluster level on TCO**, avoiding layers of electrical switching. **"A per-chip performance gap can be offset by interconnect scale — and the price of that scale is a sustained increase in optical content."** GF makes the convergence point from the other side: *"whether NVIDIA moves toward CPO/NPO or Google expands from 3D to 6D, both are Scale-up strategies aimed at enabling greater inter-chip communication."*
- ⚠️ **WHAT REMAINS UNRESOLVED, AND THE TWO HOUSES DO NOT AGREE ON IT: FUNDA names TPU 9t and 2027-2028 deployment; GF explicitly says *"the exact TPU generation for the adoption of 6D torus and initial penetration rate remain unclear"* and names NO generation and NO date.** So the arithmetic is agreed and the *applicability* is not. **Falsification set (FUNDA's own list, adopted here):** ① Google's official disclosure of the **TPU 9t topology**; ② **actual per-TPU optical-port / ICI-link counts**; ③ **speed and modulation choice** (IMDD vs Coherent Lite); ④ **OCS vendor selection and capacity-expansion cadence**, plus initial volume-production timing and single-superpod scale. **GF risks as listed: AI demand slowdown, supply constraint, competition.** See [../GOOG.md](../GOOG.md), [../LITE.md](../LITE.md), [../COHR.md](../COHR.md), [../MRVL.md](../MRVL.md), [../AXTI.md](../AXTI.md), [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md). ## Recent signals (Aug 18, LinkedIn) — 🔴🔴 **THE DEMAND-SIDE MECHANISM FOR THE OCS RAMP GETS AN ARCHITECTURE: GOOGLE'S RUMOURED *6D TORUS* (TPUv9t) DOUBLES ICI LINKS PER CHIP FROM 6 TO 12, STITCHES THE RACK GRID TOGETHER IN OPTICS, AND — IN SemiAnalysis's OWN WORDS — "COMES AT THE COST OF FAR MORE OPTICS AND OCS CAPACITY"**

**Source: @SemiAnalysis_ LinkedIn post, 2026-08-18 (public post, links their networking model). ⚠️ No methodology; SemiAnalysis itself labels the v9/6D-Torus leg as RUMOUR while stating the v7/v8 figures as fact.**

- 🔴 **THE TOPOLOGY LADDER, AND WHERE THE OPTICS SIT.** 2D Torus (TPUv2) → 3D Torus (TPUv4, v5p) → **Ironwood/TPUv7: a 3D Torus of 64-CHIP CUBES scaled via OCS to 9,216 interconnected chips** → **Cloud Next 2026: TPUv8t stays on 3D Torus and extends the scale-up domain to 9,600 chips; TPUv8i introduces the new "Boardfly" topology.** In a 3D torus each chip talks to **6 neighbours (±X, ±Y, ±Z)** and edge chips **wrap to the opposite edge** — *"that wraparound is what makes it a torus rather than a mesh, and it's what the OCS layer provides"* — i.e. **OCS is not an accessory to the Google fabric, it is the component that makes the topology a torus at all.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE 6D TORUS IS AN OPTICS MULTIPLIER, AND THE MECHANISM IS EXPLICIT.** Rumoured **TPUv9t** adds **three entirely new dimensions (NOT diagonals), doubling each chip's ICI links from 6 to 12** — a **cube of cubes** in which each small cube is a TPU and **each 3D-torus block is a RACK.** Crucially for the supply chain: **inside the rack the wiring is unchanged — short COPPER, exactly as today's 3D torus — while each chip's other 6 neighbours sit at the SAME POSITION IN THE SIX ADJACENT RACKS, and the rack grid itself forms a SECOND 3D torus stitched together in OPTICS.** So the incremental links are, by construction, **the optical ones**. Payoff: **for a fixed chip count the diameter collapses — a 4,096-chip cluster runs 24 worst-case hops on a 16×16×16 3D torus vs 12 HOPS on a 4⁶ 6D torus** — plus *"bisection bandwidth multiplies."* ⚠️ **The cost line, verbatim: "however, this all comes at the cost of far more optics and OCS capacity."**
- ➤ **WHY THIS MATTERS ON THIS PAGE: it is the first ARCHITECTURAL justification for the OCS step-up the page has been debating from the revenue side.** Held against the filed numbers already here: [[LITE]]'s FY26 10-K **>$90.0m OCS**, a derived **June-2026 quarter ≈$52m ⇒ ≈$208m exit run-rate**, the **$400m 1H-FY27** target and the first triple-digit OCS quarter in the FQ1'27 guide. A topology that **doubles ICI links and moves the incremental ones into optics** is exactly the kind of demand mechanism those multiples require — and it is Google-specific, which is the right shape given Google is the anchor OCS buyer. Also relevant to the **[[COHR]] liquid-crystal-OCS rumour (08-17)**: if OCS capacity becomes a gating input to Google's scale-up roadmap, the cost of a rejected second source rises.
- ⚠️ **BUT DO NOT COLLAPSE IT INTO THE CPO DEBATE — it cuts the other way there.** MS's 07-13 scale-up primer (below) flags **Google as the architectural outlier whose OCS-based torus "may delay or reduce its need for CPO."** Both statements can be true at once: **a torus roadmap can pull LESS switched-fabric CPO while pulling MORE OCS and inter-rack optics.** Keep them as two separate questions — conflating them is how a bullish-optics datapoint gets mis-read as a bullish-CPO datapoint. See [../GOOG.md](../GOOG.md), [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md), [../LITE.md](../LITE.md), [../COHR.md](../COHR.md).

The text reads as an excerpt from a longer written note (it references 'Figure 2 below') that applies Lumentum's own OFC framework to Google's Virgo architecture. - 🔴🔴 **THE OPTICAL-MODULE STEP IS *LARGER* THAN THE OCS STEP, AND THE REASON IS TOPOLOGICAL.** **OCS sits only at the SPINE, whereas optical modules are required at BOTH ENDS OF EVERY LAYER — in-rack switch, leaf AND spine** — and the count compounds because **in-rack switches go from ONE to FOUR–SIX per rack**, multiplying uplink ports. Net: **the scale-out optical-module ratio widens from today's 1:1–1:2 to 1:3–1:4, with speeds migrating 800G → 1.6T.** ➤ **So Virgo is a bigger event for MODULES than for OCS in ratio terms — read across to the 1.6T ramp already tracked on this page and to [[COHR]]/[[LITE]]/Innolight/Eoptolink module content, not only to the OCS socket.** The stated physical anchor: **a single fabric spanning 130K+ TPUs at ~47 Pb/s of bisection bandwidth.**
- ⚠️ **WHAT THIS IS AND IS NOT.** These are **per-xPU content RATIOS from a framework, not a booked order profile** — they compound with TPU unit growth rather than replacing it, and **no OCS or module dollar figure follows without a TPU-unit and ASP assumption, which is deliberately not derived here.** **No Virgo deployment timing is given in the excerpt.** Falsifiers to watch: any Google disclosure of Virgo spine architecture or in-rack switch count, and LITE's OCS quarterly trajectory against the $400m 1H-FY27 target. ## Recent signals (Aug 18, /run-inbox) — 🔴🔴 **AN ENGINEER GRADES THE THREE PUBLISHED CPO/NPO LASER ARCHITECTURES AGAINST EACH OTHER AND ALL THREE FAIL ON THE SAME AXIS — *NOISE*, NOT BANDWIDTH. AND THE THEME GETS ITS FIRST HARD PHYSICAL THRESHOLD: LASER WALL-PLUG EFFICIENCY OF ~10%, BELOW WHICH THE ENTIRE ENERGY-SAVINGS RATIONALE FOR CO-PACKAGED OPTICS INVERTS.**

_(Source: **Irrational Analysis**, "Intel CPO Group is Alive? + Very Interesting NVDA/LITE/AEVA Bonus Papers", 2026-08-17 — [IA_18_ago.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/IA_18_ago.html). ⚠️ **SOURCE CLASS: opinionated independent Substack by a working engineer with self-declared semiconductor positions. No rating, no PT, no estimates. Author describes this particular post as "lazy low-quality stream-of-consciousness" written ahead of Hot Chips. Technical referee, not a recommendation — and single-source on every claim below.**)_

- 🔴 **THE THRESHOLD TO ADOPT WIKI-WIDE, AND IT IS THE MOST USEFUL THING IN THE NOTE: "Laser WPE of 10% is CRITICAL for making CPO/NPO make sense from an energy savings perspective. The burden is on the laser power efficiency and the system efficiency degrades EXPONENTIALLY as laser WPE drops below 10%."** ➤ **Every CPO/NPO pitch on this wiki is ultimately an energy-savings pitch, and this is the first published number that says where it stops working. It is checkable, rarely disclosed, and belongs in the question set for [[LITE]], [[COHR]], [[NVDA]], [[TSEM]] and [[INTC]] alike. Below ~10% WPE the optics stop saving power and CPO becomes a density/serviceability argument only.**
- **The two related findings from the same modelling section, one of which is counter-intuitive: (1) POWER FLATNESS (±0.5 dB across the DWDM comb) matters materially for efficiency — not for the reason usually given, but because uneven power makes thermal control and stability of the ring bus difficult ("RING SELF-HEATING EVIL"). (2) WAVELENGTH SPACING ACCURACY of ±10 GHz on a 200 GHz grid matters for OPTICAL CROSSTALK but is "basically irrelevant for power efficiency" — which the reviewer himself calls "very surprising," having assumed tight spacing was about saving ring-heater tuning power.** ➤ **Practical consequence: a supplier quoting tight wavelength spacing is making a crosstalk claim, not an efficiency claim. Do not let the two be conflated in a CPO pitch — they trade off against different things.**
- 🔴 **THE ARCHITECTURE SCORECARD, AND THE DIVIDING LINE IS SOA TOPOLOGY.** **[[INTC]] — "C+, maybe B− if we want to coddle them"**: excellent process execution (±10 GHz spacing, 16-λ array with excellent flatness, 5 dB extinction ratio, 75 dB TIA gain, 1.3 dB coupling loss) undone by amplifying ALL 8 modulated wavelengths through a SHARED SOA — every wavelength pair spawns two four-wave-mixing tones landing in-band as RIN. The reviewer accuses Intel of clipping the spectrum to hide it ("The SMSR is not 50 dB. This is a lie. Real number is 30 dB… This is cheating"), and of quoting BER at perfect timing. Compounded on the Rx side by three cascaded MZIs to de-mux ("stupid"), which kills link budget, forces a second SOA stage and mandates polarization control. **[[NVDA]] — "A+"**: BER 1e-12 across a **0.47 UI sampling offset WITH clock forwarding**; at that same window "all of the Intel CPO lanes would catastrophically fail," and even at a generous 0.2 UI Intel sits at ~1e-5. **[[LITE]]/NVIDIA-Research joint paper — split verdict**: a monolithic 9-λ InP DFB array with an **individual SOA PER CHANNEL** (so no FWM — the correct answer to Intel's problem), single TEC, single isolator; power variation, spacing accuracy and SMSR all "excellent," but **RIN ~−137 dBc/Hz is "so bad it is basically unusable for any real-world link"**, with the paper's own mitigating language dismissed as "Nvidia Research cope," plus a "horrific" expected yield on a 2.25mm × 1mm die.
- **THE COST ITEM THE BUY-SIDE SHOULD CARE ABOUT MOST, and it is an unprompted reaction rather than a claim being sold: on the Lumentum/NVIDIA package — "What the fuck this entire thing only needs ONE ISOLATOR?! This is HUGE COST SAVINGS on the isolators needed AND ACTIVE ALIGNMENT COSTS."** ➤ **This connects directly to the 08-04 transceiver-ban memo already on this page, which named ACTIVE ALIGNMENT capacity — not design — as the binding constraint on the EML→SiPho pivot. One isolator for nine wavelengths plus relaxed alignment attacks exactly that bottleneck. It is the first outside-in support this theme has for the co-integrated-laser COST argument, as distinct from the performance argument.**
- ⚠️ **A NARROWING CLAIM ON [[TSEM]], logged and NOT adopted: "Openlight/Tower can only offer SINGLE WAVELENGTH at max 20 mW (13 dBm) in waveguide. Intel and Scintil can deliver MULTI-WAVELENGTH at lower power."** ➤ **If true it splits Tower's optical opportunity in two: the SiPho PIC attach survives the CPO/NPO transition (the "all three principals use Tower" argument on that page), but the LASER content does not accrue to Tower on multi-wavelength DWDM designs. Prefaced "my understanding," no spec cited, and foundry platform maxima move with each PDK release — filed as a checkable question, not a fact.**
- **Also noted, off-coverage: an Aeva SOA paper is judged the best of the three — 22% PCE at 650 mW output at a 40°C operating point, "excellent flatness" with ASE 40 dB down, FIT < 5.** No wiki page; logged so the comparison point exists if Aeva enters the CPO laser-supply conversation.

## Recent signals (Aug 18 ingest; source dated Aug 17) — ⚠️ **THE CIRCUIT PUTS SERVICEABILITY, NOT BANDWIDTH, AT THE CENTRE OF THE IN-RACK OPTICS DELAY — USEFUL FRAMING, BUT NO NEW NUMBERS**

_(Source: **The Circuit · Ben Bajarin / Jay Goldberg, EP188, published 2026-08-17 — independent podcast**. ⚠️ **The supplied attachment is incomplete: 30 nonblank lines, ending during the Lumentum/Coherent discussion. The publisher supplies a description but no full transcript. The local source record preserves the fragment and labels the publisher-description paraphrase; no missing dialogue is reconstructed.**)_

- **THE USEFUL ADDITION IS AN ARCHITECTURE-ADOPTION CHECK:** the episode frames **LPO / NPO / CPO** as a live design contest but argues optical adoption remains predominantly **rack-to-rack**, rather than intra-rack, because manufacturing and field serviceability still matter. ➜ **This is a qualitative counterweight to [[LITE]]'s first signs of in-rack penetration and Fabrinet's NPO-first sequencing:** a hard-to-service integrated optical failure inside the rack carries a larger replacement burden and blast radius than a pluggable at the boundary. **Bandwidth need can be real before the operational design is settled.** (The Circuit · Bajarin/Goldberg, 2026-08-17)
- **THE LITE/COHR COMPARISON IS DIRECTIONALLY CONSISTENT, NOT INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION:** the available fragment contrasts Lumentum's cleaner execution with Coherent's stronger revenue outlook but weaker margin/yield debate. The episode description specifically flags Coherent's six-inch InP yield as the margin question. ⚠️ **No hard yield, margin or timing datapoint survives in the supplied text, so none is added here; the existing company-primary and broker entries govern.**
- **MODEL CONSEQUENCE: NONE.** No CPO/NPO attach curve, content assumption, TAM, deployment date or company estimate changes. The AMAT eight-quarter-visibility and NVDA $500bn-financing/compute-asset discussions are already carried elsewhere from stronger sources; Cisco/Mythos is filed separately on [[CSCO]].

**🔴 (1) FABRINET Q4 FY26 — THE MANUFACTURING/PACKAGING LAYER FINALLY SPEAKS ON THIS PAGE, AND IT SEQUENCES NPO AHEAD OF CPO.**

_(Source: **Fabrinet Q4 FY26 earnings call, 2026-08-17 — COMPANY PRIMARY**, Bloomberg **INITIAL DRAFT TRANSCRIPT** printed 08-17. ⚠️ **Fabrinet is off-coverage with NO wiki page, so this theme page is where its content lives — plain text, no ticker link, by convention.** ⚠️ **The router filed this as "JPM" — WRONG: Joseph Cardoso is on the analyst roster, not the author; this is the company's own call.** ⚠️ Draft transcript: three garbles flagged in place below and NOT guessed.)_

- **THE PRINT.** Q4 revenue **$1.316bn, +45% y/y**, above the top end of guidance; **non-GAAP EPS $4.10, a record**, also above guide (GAAP net income $139m / $3.83). **FY26 revenue $4.6bn, +36%; FY26 non-GAAP EPS $14.09, +39%.** **GM 12.2%** (+10bp q/q, −30bp y/y) on **opex of just 1.3% of revenue** ⇒ **operating margin 10.9%, the highest in three years**. **Q1 FY27 guide: revenue $1.375–1.425bn (+43% y/y at the midpoint), EPS $4.10–4.25**, with the usual Q1 expense seasonality a temporary margin headwind. Guides one quarter at a time but is *"more confident than ever"* on the longer term; **customer forecasts extend into FY27 and beyond** (forecasts, explicitly not order commitments). Balance sheet: cash + ST investments **$876m** (−$70m q/q); Q4 OCF $55m, capex $92m (Building 10 + an $11m campus purchase), **FCF −$37m**; FY26 OCF $257m, FCF $4m; ~$169m of buyback authorisation left with no meaningful Q4 repurchase. **Inventory jump = growth plus positioning for future ramps, NOT a new supply constraint.**
- 🔴 **THE NEW SEGMENT STRUCTURE IS THE MOST THEME-RELEVANT THING IN THE RELEASE, AND IT IS A REPORTING-TAXONOMY SIGNAL ABOUT THE DCI-vs-DATACOM BOUNDARY THIS PAGE TRACKS.** Fabrinet moved to **data centers / communications infrastructure / automotive-industrial-other** because *"**hyperscalers and other data center service providers are the ultimate customers of many of the products we manufacture, including some of those that have been characterized as telecom products in the past**"* — data centers now *"including data center networking with an expanded view of DCI, high-performance computing, and other AI infrastructure applications."* Purely presentational; **12 quarters of history restated in the deck.** ➜ **Read it as an issuer conceding that the telecom/datacom line item is no longer a demand boundary: the same optical hardware is being sold to hyperscalers under a telecom label. This page has been arguing the DCI/scale-across leg from the demand side (DIGITIMES 08-11, 650 Group 08-14, Meta's L3↔L4 mix of LR + DWDM/ZR, 07-09); Fabrinet is the first source to re-cut its own P&L around it.**
- 🔴 **THE SEGMENT NUMBERS, AND THEY FOOT.** **Data center $669m, +68% y/y, +13% q/q = 51% of total revenue — now the largest category — with "ECI products the largest contributor to data center growth… with an annualized revenue run-rate exceeding $1 billion"** (HPC also a substantial contributor). ⚠️ *"ECI"* is used unexpanded on the call and is **not expanded here** — pull the deck to define it before modelling. **Communications infrastructure $413m, +40% y/y, +1% q/q (31%)** on telecom systems, satcom and telecom components. **Automotive/industrial/other $234m, +8% y/y, +9% q/q (18%)** on EV-charging infrastructure with a smaller Lidar contribution. ✅ **Arithmetic check: implied prior-year quarters $398m + $295m + $217m = $910m ≈ the $907.6m that $1.316bn at +45% y/y requires. The restated segments reconcile to the total.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THAT CREATES A DIRECT COLLISION WITH THE SELL-SIDE READ OF THE SAME PRINT ALREADY LOGGED IN THE SECTION BELOW — the bear headline was measured on a taxonomy the company retired in the same report.** The Aug-17 (PM) block on this page carries, from **JPM · Joshua Meyers ("FN First Blush" / "FN Off the Call") and Vital Knowledge, 2026-08-17**: *"optical communications +7.6% q/q / +39% y/y driven very largely by DCI +30% Q/Q"* but ⚠️ *"**Datacom was DOWN on the quarter ($258mn vs $271mn consensus)**, which was the KPI the market wanted up, and FN fell 6.5%."* Against the company's own new cut, **data center revenue was $669m and +68% y/y**. The two cannot be compared: **$258mn is a legacy sub-line inside what is now a $669m category**, and the company's stated reason for retiring the old cut is precisely that telecom-labelled product is sold to hyperscalers. ✅ **The relay's q/q ties to the new segments** (data center +13% and comms infra +1% ⇒ **+8.1% combined q/q** vs the relayed +7.6%). ⚠️ **The relay's y/y does NOT: the new segments imply optical (data center + comms infra) $1,082m vs $693m = ~+56% y/y, not +39%.** ➜ **Do not net the two. The $258mn "Datacom miss" that took the stock down 6.5% is a basis artifact until it is re-run against the restated 12-quarter history in the deck — flagged, not resolved. This is the single most tradeable thing in the print and it is a measurement problem, not a demand problem.**
- 🔴 **CUSTOMER CONCENTRATION — AND THE NVIDIA LINE IS THE ONE THAT SHOULD WORRY THE NVDA-CENTRIC CONSTRUCTION OF THIS PAGE'S WHOLE THESIS. FY26 customers at ≥10% of revenue: [[CSCO]] 20%, [[NVDA]] 16%, Nokia 11%, [[AMZN]] 11% — 58% of FY26 revenue between four names. Nokia is NEW to the list. Fabrinet refused to help: asked about 1.6T timing at its largest datacom customer, Grady said *"This is a call you have to talk to NVIDIA about."*** ⚠️ **On Ciena's absence, keep the bound: CIEN manufactures across multiple partners and its own sites, so not appearing on one CM's ≥10% list is not a demand signal about Ciena — but it does sit oddly beside CIEN's own scale-across claims in item (3) below, and it is the first hard datapoint on this page that the DCI/telecom-systems pull is arriving through **Nokia** (post-Infinera) rather than through Ciena.**
- **NOKIA, FROM THE CM'S SIDE — a clean read-through on the Infinera integration this page has never had:** Fabrinet's first objective post-acquisition was that the **Infinera business stayed robust — no product-rationalisation loss, and *"the Infinera business has been rising"***; Nokia was historically **not** a big customer, is now **>10%**, and *"we feel we're just getting started."*
- 🔴 **THE CAPACITY LADDER — AND IT IS A SUPPLY-RESPONSE DATAPOINT THAT SPEAKS STRAIGHT TO THIS PAGE'S C28-OVERSHOOT RISK.** Each new building runs **~1.2m sq ft carrying ~$1.8–2.1bn of revenue capacity**; adding the additions to the Q4 exit run-rate gets Fabrinet to **$12.5–14bn of revenue capacity, versus the ~$11.5bn framed on last quarter's call** — and management is explicit that the delta is **both more square footage AND rising revenue per square foot**. Footprint: **Building 10 on track for completion by early 2027 (+2m sq ft total; 250k sq ft already qualified with a similar amount on the third floor this quarter); Pinehurst 120k sq ft converting office→manufacturing; a newly commissioned building +200k sq ft; and Great America Place, Santa Clara — two office buildings plus ~130k sq ft of manufacturing, more than doubling the Silicon Valley footprint.** ✅ **Derived, house arithmetic: $1.8–2.1bn per 1.2m sq ft = ~$1,500–1,750 of revenue capacity per sq ft; the Q4 exit run-rate annualizes to ~$5.26bn, so the additions carry ~$7.2–8.7bn ≈ 3.5–4.8 buildings' worth; and the $12.5–14bn ladder is **2.7–3.0x FY26 revenue** (2.2–2.5x the annualized Q1 FY27 guide midpoint). The quarter-on-quarter revision is +$1.0–2.5bn, i.e. +8.7% to +21.7%, on ONE quarter's information.** ➜ **Hold this against Jefferies' 08-05 warning that *"a considerable amount of capacity comes online [in C28] intended to meet demand for scale-up NPO/CPO, where roadmaps have yet to be finalized… the industry risks a classic capacity overshoot."* Fabrinet is a live instance of the mechanism: the Western optical CM layer is sizing physical capacity at ~3x current revenue against customer FORECASTS that are explicitly not order commitments. It is also the counterweight — this is where the non-Chinese assembly capacity the ban thread assumes must exist actually comes from.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE NPO/CPO SUBSTANCE — the reason this call belongs on this page at all, verbatim.** Grady, answering Cardoso: ***"NPO technology sits somewhere between pluggables — pluggable modules and CPO. As you know, we've built tens of millions of pluggable modules over the years… We're working on CPO today with a handful of customers and we're already building devices, albeit not yet at full-scale volumes."*** On positioning: *"we feel we're very well-positioned to be the leader… in manufacturing and packaging and near-packaged optics devices."* On the physics of the ramp: ***"as NPO scales to 6.4, 12.8 terabit and beyond, the manufacturing complexity and yield becomes increasingly important. It becomes critical."*** And the sequencing call: ***"NPO, I think probably represents a more near-term opportunity than CPO from what we've seen with our customers."*** ⚠️ **The hard limit on what this is worth: *"It's probably too early to talk about, let's say, revenues and margins from those opportunities and customers as they depend on program specifics."* NO revenue, NO margin, NO dated ramp, NO named customer.**
