Not corrected in place, flagged.**
  - 🔴 **WHAT FABRINET'S VOTE ADDS, PRECISELY — AND IT IS A NEW *KIND* OF EVIDENCE, NOT A SEVENTH OPINION.** This page has built the NPO-additivity / NPO-first thesis from the demand and component side: **[[LITE]]'s own call (*"The NPO opportunity is completely additive for us, significantly increasing the optical TAM"*, *"even our largest CPO customer is looking at NPO for specific new use cases"*, 2026-08-11 transcript), the LightCounting revision (NPO ADDED to the April CPO-only forecast for a >50% upward revision, JPM 08-06), [[COHR]] (content *"very comparable"*, 08-12), [[ALAB]] (NPO production-ready NOW, CPO 2027+), MS (*"NPO increasingly looks like the more tangible bridge"*, 08-03), the 650 Group (*"the pragmatic near-term bridge"*, 08-14) and FundaAI at OCP APAC (*"additive rather than cannibalistic"*, 08-17).** ✅ **Fabrinet is the first vote from the layer that has to physically BUILD the packages, and it is independent of all of the above: it is not selling a laser, a TAM or a switch. It confirms (a) the SEQUENCE — NPO is the nearer opportunity, from what its customers are doing, not from what it hopes; (b) that CPO devices are being built TODAY with "a handful of customers," at sub-full-scale volumes — which is the manufacturing-side version of NVDA/AVGO's "in production" claim and is compatible with ASE's "the ecosystem is not ready" (both can be true: real devices, unready yield); and (c) that the binding variable in the NPO ramp is YIELD at 6.4T and 12.8T, i.e. the same variable ASE listed and the same one [[TER]] said cannot be tested away at insertion 1.** ❌ **What it does NOT do, and the page should not let it: it puts NO number on NPO or CPO — no TAM, no content, no revenue, no margin, no date — and it names no customer. It cannot be used to move the attach curve in `## Reference framework — HOUSE TAM BRIDGE` (Route C) in either direction, and it does not adjudicate the 4-7× content fork.**
  - 🆕 **ONE THING IT DOES PARTLY RESOLVE, AND IT IS IN THE FRAMEWORK'S OWN OPEN-QUESTION LIST.** The Aug-17 GF Securities exhibit block below flags as *"THE ONE UNRESOLVED READ, AND IT MOVES EVERYTHING: is '3.2T'/'6.4T' the PER-OE line rate, or a platform/product name?"* — a distinction that swings every dollar figure derived from that exhibit. **A contract manufacturer describing "NPO scal[ing] to 6.4, 12.8 terabit and beyond" as the axis along which *manufacturing complexity and yield* get harder is talking about a DEVICE, not a product family** — which is supportive of the per-OE reading, alongside SemiAnalysis's AMD MI500 spec (9 optical engines at 6.4 Tbit/s uni-di each, 07-24). ⚠️ **Supportive, not conclusive: Grady does not say "per optical engine," and the uni-vs-bi-directional ambiguity (a 2× swing) is untouched.**
  - ➜ **And a structural read this page should carry forward: Fabrinet is claiming the NPO *packaging* franchise ("to be the leader in manufacturing and packaging near-packaged optics devices") from OUTSIDE the TSMC COUPE gate. That is the assembly-layer counterpart to the 650 Group's 08-14 finding that [[AMZN]], [[NVDA]] and [[AVGO]] are all exploring NPO *outside* COUPE — i.e. the NPO route is being built with merchant packaging capacity, which is bullish for merchant optical-engine supply and is the mechanism by which the COUPE/SoIC capacity ceiling (Fubon's −22% 2028 SoIC cut, 08-02) stops being the binding constraint on the NPO leg specifically.**

**🔴 (2) [[LITE]] FY26 FORM 10-K (FY ended 2026-06-27, FILED 2026-08-17) — THE FIRST FILED FULL-YEAR OCS NUMBER, AND THE FIRST *FILED* VERSION OF THIS PAGE'S "OWN THE LIGHT SOURCE" THESIS.**

- 🔴 **OCS, FILED: *"we also continued the initial phase of optical circuit switch shipments, which contributed more than $90.0 million of revenue during fiscal year 2026"*, and *"we remain on track for manufacturing expansion over the coming quarters to support future growth."*** ⚠️ **THESIS DRIFT — SUPERSEDED: the page's standing claim (from the 2026-08-17 PM coverage audit) that the 10-Q of 2026-05-06 (">$25.0m in the Mar-26 quarter ≈ $100m annualized") is *"the only FILED OCS revenue number"* is no longer true. Old framing moved to `## Changelog` with its date; the FY26 10-K figure governs from here.** ✅ **The two filings foot and give the shape: FY26 averages $22.5m/quarter, so a >$25.0m Mar-26 quarter ran ~11% above the FY average and the ">$25m × 4 ≈ $100m" annualization overstated the realized year by ~11%. With Q3 above $25m on a ramping profile, 1H FY26 (Sep-25 + Dec-25) was ≤ ~$40m combined, i.e. ≤ ~$20m/quarter.** Note also **where** it sits: OCS is inside **Systems**, alongside the cloud transceiver lines whose ASPs are falling. 🔴 **SHARPENED SAME NIGHT (the 10-Q carries TWO figures, not one — `>$25.0m` for the March quarter AND `>$38.0m` for the NINE MONTHS, so the shape is exact, not bounded): FY26 `>$90.0m` − 9M `>$38.0m` ⇒ the **June-2026 quarter alone was ≈$52m**, and 9M − Q3 ⇒ **Q1+Q2 FY26 ≈$13m COMBINED** (13 + 25 + 52 = 90 ✓). **The OCS EXIT run-rate is therefore ≈$208m annualized (4 × ≈$52m), NOT $90m — and for a forward RUN-RATE claim the exit run-rate is the honest denominator.** Re-based on it, the relay claims shrink again: the ">$1bn 2027 run-rate" is **≈4.8×** the exit run-rate (not ~11.1× the full year) and the $400m 2H-CY26 guide (≈$800m annualized) is **≈3.8×** it. The multiples in the table below are arithmetically correct against the FULL-YEAR base they are struck off, but they overstate the step being asked for, because ~58% of FY26's OCS revenue landed in the final quarter. ⚠️ The June quarter is DERIVED from two filed figures, not disclosed — LITE gave no OCS number in the Q4FY26 release or on the call.**
- 🔴 **RE-DERIVING THE RAMP MULTIPLES OFF THE FILED BASE — INCLUDING AGAINST THE THREE BRACKETED RELAY CLAIMS THE PAGE ALREADY FLAGS AS *NOT SAID ON THE Q4FY26 CALL*. House arithmetic, shown:**

- 🔴 **THE POINT OF THE RE-DERIVATION, AND IT CHANGES WHERE THE SKEPTICISM BELONGS.** Measured off the filed FY26 base the relay claims look like **11–14× moonshots** — which is the framing the page has been carrying (the 08-17 PM audit entry called the $1.25bn/yr capacity claim *"an explicit ~10-12x ramp"* off the annualized 10-Q quarter, and on the new filed base that becomes ~13.9×). **But measured off the run-rate the company has already put in a guide, they are 1.25–1.56× steps.** The 8.9× is not a forecast — it is the **$400m 1H-FY27 target plus a triple-digit FQ1 guide**, both primary. ➜ **So the load-bearing number is the $400m, not the >$1bn that follows it. Reserve the skepticism for the two-quarter 4.4× step, and stop treating ">$1bn 2027" as the aggressive part of the claim — arithmetically it is the least aggressive thing in the stack. - **A FIRST PORT-COUNT ANCHOR FOR THE OCS LEG, which the framework has never had.** At the **~$520/port** that `## Reference framework — HOUSE TAM BRIDGE` back-solves out of Redburn's Route A, the filed **>$90.0m implies ~173,000 OCS ports shipped in FY26**. ⚠️ **Derived, and it inherits the framework's own caveats: $520/port was calibrated at 2027, applied to FY26 on the page's stated physics that a circuit switch does not re-price with line rate. Treat as an order-of-magnitude anchor to test against a disclosed port count, not as a fact.**
- 🔴 **THE COMPONENTS/SYSTEMS SPLIT — THIS IS THE MARGIN-MIX FACT SEVERAL OF THIS PAGE'S CONTENT CALLS DEPEND ON, NOW IN A FILING RATHER THAN A BROKER DECK.** FY26 net revenue **$3,014.0m** vs $1,645.0m FY25 vs $1,359.2m FY24 ⇒ **+$1,369.0m / +83.2% y/y**, split **Components +$889.3m (65% of the growth) / Systems +$479.7m (35%)**. Inside Components: ***"the ramp of laser chip and laser assembly product shipment, which represent 78% of the total growth"*** across intra-data-center, DCI and long-haul, ***"complemented by a slight increase in average selling prices of laser chip products driven primarily by a shift to 200G lane speeds"***; the remaining ~22% is data transport (long-haul line subsystems + charge-pump products for undersea). Inside Systems: ***"our cloud transceiver product lines which increased by more than 173% due to an increase in shipment volume, partially offset by LOWER average selling prices."*** ✅ **House arithmetic: 78% × $889.3m = ~$693.7m of laser chip/assembly, i.e. ~50.7% of the company's ENTIRE incremental FY26 revenue came from the one product line with RISING ASPs, while the module line grew on volume with FALLING ASPs.** ➜ **That is `## Key debates`' *"own the light source, not the module"* thesis stated in a 10-K. It also corroborates the transcript detail already on the page that the CW die-shrink *"closed the margin gap considerably"* vs EML, and it is the filed counterpart to the cross-stack gross-margin ladder in item (6) below.**
- ⚠️🔴 **BUT THE SAME FILING CUTS AGAINST OVER-ATTRIBUTING MARGIN TO MIX, AND THIS IS THE MOST USEFUL SINGLE LINE IN THE 10-K FOR THE MARGIN DEBATE ON THIS PAGE.** FY26 GAAP gross margin **41.7% vs 28.0% in FY25**, decomposed by the company: ***"Approximately 54% of the gross margin dollar increase was driven by LOWER MANUFACTURING COSTS AS A PERCENTAGE OF REVENUE, primarily due to HIGHER INTERNAL FACTORY UTILIZATION. Additionally, 29% of the gross margin increase was driven by a MIX SHIFT to higher margin products. The remaining 17%… relates to the decrease in amortization of acquired intangibles."*** ➜ **Utilization, not mix, was the bigger half of LITE's FY26 margin expansion — and only ~29% was mix at all. Hold that directly against item (5) below, where Irrational Analysis argues that [[COHR]]'s thin margin expansion "can only" be explained by bad CPO-laser yield because mix does not account for it: LITE's own filed decomposition says that in this cycle the dominant margin lever is loading the factory, which is exactly the lever a capacity-constrained, mid-6-inch-transition COHR has least of. That is a mundane alternative explanation for the GM gap, sourced from the bull's own filing, and it does not require anyone's yield to be bad.**
- **CONCENTRATION AND POLICY, filed:** single end customers at ≥10% of net revenue — **Customer A 26.6% FY26** (15.4% FY25, 18.9% FY24, i.e. **+11.2pp y/y**) and **Customer B 15.0%** (16.0%, 11.4%); **non-US shipment revenue 79.2%** (81.0%, 73.8%). Backlog explicitly ***"not necessarily indicative of actual revenue or the level of orders for any succeeding period."*** Retained risk factors: ***"Our ability to sell our products to a previously significant customer has been restricted,"*** and certain significant customers **AND suppliers** have products subject to US export controls. ➜ **The NVDA-centricity this page's entire scale-up thesis rests on is now visible in a filing (one customer at 26.6% and climbing), and so is the two-sided export-control exposure the ban thread has been arguing qualitatively since 08-04 — LITE is exposed on BOTH the customer and the supplier side of the same policy lever.** Also filed: the two former reportable segments (Cloud & Networking / Industrial Tech) applied only **prior to** FY26, changed in Q1 FY26 — a second issuer in one day re-cutting its segments around the AI end-market (see Fabrinet above).

**🔴 (3) FUNDA'S GENUINELY NET-NEW LAYER — THE PER-COMPANY SCALE-ACROSS DECOMPOSITION, PLUS TWO PHYSICAL SCALING LAWS THAT COUPLE THE LEGS THIS PAGE MODELS SEPARATELY.**

_(Source: **FUNDA · "Deep|Optics: The Scale-Across TAM Is Taking Shape", 2026-08-12.** ⚠️ Independent substack. ⚠️ Its LightCounting, ANET-TAM and Nokia-order figures are SECOND RELAYS — see item (0); nothing in this item re-counts them. FUNDA's own framing quote is [[LITE]]'s Q4FY26 call, already on disk: *"Expanding inferencing and training applications are driving full-rate connectivity between data centers, while political and regulatory constraints favor smaller, more modular builds. These 2 factors, among others, are substantially increasing the demand for our pump laser solutions."*)_

- 🔴🔴 **THE OCS × COHERENT-LITE COUPLING — THE MOST DECISION-RELEVANT LINE IN THE NOTE, BECAUSE IT MECHANICALLY LINKS TWO TAM LEGS THE HOUSE FRAMEWORK PRICES SEPARATELY AND ON DIFFERENT UNITS: *"Each OCS node adds roughly 3 dB of insertion loss, so even links under 2 kilometers need coherent-lite."*** FUNDA's June note argued this OCS-paired case drives coherent-lite's **largest** volumes. ➜ **`## Reference framework — HOUSE TAM BRIDGE` states as its organizing rule that *"the three TAMs are priced on different units; blending them is the #1 error"* — CPO/NPO bandwidth-priced, OCS port-priced. This is the first sourced datapoint on this page saying the OCS and coherent/scale-across legs are not merely adjacent but **physically coupled**: inserting an OCS switch into a path imposes an optical-budget penalty that forces a coherent (rather than IMDD) transceiver on the SAME link, even at sub-2km reaches where nobody would otherwise deploy coherent. Two consequences, and they point in opposite directions: (a) **every incremental OCS port carries attached coherent-lite content**, so the OCS leg has a demand externality onto the coherent leg that neither route in the framework captures — a genuine understatement; and (b) it makes the OCS TAM's *port* pricing sensitive to something a circuit switch was supposed to be insulated from, because the buyer's decision is on the OCS+coherent bundle, not the switch alone. **Do NOT sum the two TAMs to capture this — the correct treatment is an attach ratio from OCS ports to coherent modules, and it is a new open variable, not a new addend.** Cross-referenced into the OCS section of the framework.**
- 🔴 **THE PHOTONICS-INTENSITY LAW, from [[CIEN]]: *"a single-rail line system covers roughly 100km; stretching the same link to roughly 1,000 kilometers on hyper-rail takes 4 to 5 times more photonics"*** — the farther apart the DCs, the more photonics **per link**. ➜ **This is the scale-across analogue of the copper-reach physics in `## What it is / why it matters`, and it is the first content-per-link elasticity on this page for the scale-across leg. It also prices the difference between the two deployment geometries the page already carries: DIGITIMES' 08-11 "campus data centers 2-20 kilometres apart" versus SemiAnalysis's 07-09 Meta finding that "the other Titans will go even further with scale-across, connecting campuses up to 2,000kms apart." On this law, a 1,000km-class scale-across build carries 4-5× the photonics of a 100km one — so the distance distribution of the buildout, not just the port count, sets the revenue. The 650 Group's 08-14 note that "30% [is] LINKED TO 100KM+ USE CASES requiring coherent optics, routers, or Jericho-type L3 switches" is the share; this is the multiplier on that share.**
- **[[ANET]] — the decomposition (net-new; the TAM figures are the re-relay flagged in item 0): scale-across expected to be ~30% of Arista's 2026 AI revenue target of at least $3.6bn ⇒ ~$1.1bn**, against a scale-across market **FUNDA/Arista size at $3–4bn today**. ✅ **Check: 0.30 × $3.6bn = $1.08bn ≈ the ~$1.1bn quoted, and it implies Arista alone is ~27–36% of the entire current scale-across market — a share claim worth testing, since it is derived from a "≥$3.6bn" floor and a "$3-4bn" range, so the true fraction could be materially higher.** 🔴 **Ullal's answer to the obvious bear (does scale-across fade once compute and power catch up?) is the strategic claim to carry: the industry will remain in a **constant state of scarcity for power, space and compute, making distributed AI a permanent architecture** rather than a workaround.** ➜ **That is the same argument DIGITIMES made physically on 08-11 ("a single data center faces limits in power supply, floor space, and cooling"), now upgraded from a constraint to a permanent architectural commitment by the vendor with the most to gain. Weight accordingly — but note it is the load-bearing assumption under every scale-across TAM on this page, including Redburn's >$20bn.** Products: the 7800 spine plus SRv6 routing.
- **[[CIEN]] — the biggest single TAM revision in the note: total TAM guided to roughly $50bn by 2029, ~2× today's level, with Gary Smith attributing much of the increase to scale-across, sized at $8–10bn by 2029 inside a long-haul + metro optical transport market above $20bn.** ✅ **CONSISTENCY CHECK AGAINST THIS PAGE'S SCALE-ACROSS SPREAD, WHICH MATTERS BECAUSE THE PAGE HAS AN OPEN 5× FORK: CIEN's $8–10bn (2029) sits comfortably INSIDE both Arista's ~$20bn-including-optics (2030) and Redburn's >$20bn (2030) — three independent constructions now agree on a low-$20bn-scale 2030 pool. The outlier remains the 650 Group's "$100bn+ by early next decade" (08-14), which the page already instructed should not be used as the anchor. This ingest strengthens that instruction: two more bottom-up sizings landed in the $8-20bn band and none landed near $100bn.** Also: [[CIEN]] announced the industry's **first multi-rail order for the RLS hyper-rail line system** (co-created with hyperscalers) on the June call, plus a **competitive takeaway win for coherent modules a major hyperscaler will deploy in scale-across metro and long-haul DCI**. 🔴 **And Scott McFeely dates the coherent-lite crossover: rising bandwidth keeps shortening the reach of existing interconnect, so coherent moves closer to and eventually inside the DC in a lighter form — CIEN expects that intersection in LATE 2027 INTO 2028, AT 1.6T AND 3.2T, on a roadmap shaped with the lead cloud providers.** ➜ **Note the date collision with this page's other 1.6T/3.2T clock: GS's module model has 3.2T entering at $13.3bn in 2027E rising to $21.6bn in 2028E and the TOTAL module TAM PEAKING in 2027 before falling 5% in 2028 on ASP. CIEN is placing a NEW coherent-lite content layer arriving exactly across that 2027/2028 seam. If both hold, the 2028 module-TAM decline is partly offset by a category GS's model does not carry — worth testing before adopting 2027 as the industry revenue peak.**
- **[[CSCO]] — the useful part is the NEGATIVE: NO scale-across in the order book yet, with early orders expected only in FY26Q4 and scale in FY27.** Cisco disclosed its first scale-across design wins on the FY26Q3 (May) call — **two hyperscalers selected Silicon One P200-based systems and a third followed in the opening weeks of FY26Q4** — and guided to **~$9bn of AI infrastructure orders from hyperscalers in FY26, 4.5× the prior year's total**. **Acacia booked over $1bn of orders in the quarter, tracking to grow more than 200% in FY26, and has shipped over 750,000 400G and more than 40,000 800G coherent pluggables**, fully coherent at DCI distances today. ➜ **Two reads. (1) The 750k/40k shipped-unit split is the cleanest available measure of where the coherent-pluggable installed base actually is — 800G coherent is ~5% of the 400G volume, i.e. the coherent-lite/800ZR wave that CIEN dates to late-2027/2028 has essentially not started in units. (2) A named vendor with three hyperscaler design wins reporting ZERO scale-across revenue in the order book is the hardest available evidence that the scale-across TAM discussed all season is a FY27+ revenue event, not a 2026 one — which is FUNDA's own conclusion: *"Everything said about scale-across this season is about demand, and that demand is building faster than the suppliers can expand."***
- **Nokia (off-coverage, plain text): AI and Cloud segment net sales MORE THAN DOUBLED y/y to EUR 446m** (net-new; the EUR 2.8bn order-intake figure is the re-relay flagged in item 0), with the order intake **attributed to DCI and scale-across fabrics**. 🔴 **Justin Hotard's boundary definition is the most precise one this page has: scale-across = the BACK-END fabrics joining data centers within a campus into one AI factory, while LONG-HAUL FRONT-END bandwidth between data centers remains DCI** — complementary, with the DCI portion *"underappreciated."* ➜ **Adopt this as the page's working definition, because it resolves an ambiguity that has been quietly inflating every scale-across number here: the 650 Group's "DCI ports" (<1mn → 20-40mn) and Arista's "scale-across switching and routing" are measuring DIFFERENT layers under Hotard's cut, which is part of why the sizings run 5× apart.** Also: **Nokia is ramping its own InP fab with yield and volume targets running through 2027**, and its optical roadmap runs **four DSPs** spanning scale-across / DCI / metro / long-haul. ⚠️🔴 **AND THE COMPETITIVE MARKER, which is the sharpest single line in the note for the two names this page is longest: on the Nokia call an analyst placed [[COHR]]'s InP ramp as *"already doing pretty well"* and [[LITE]] *"a bit behind."*** ⚠️ **An analyst's characterisation on a third party's call — not a disclosure, not sourced, and it cuts against this page's own record (LITE's FY26 laser chip/assembly ramp above is 50.7% of total company growth with ASPs UP; COHR is the one mid-6-inch-transition). Logged because it is the FIRST external voice ranking the two InP ramps in COHR's favour on execution rather than on capacity strategy, and because it is a checkable claim: LITE's Greensboro/Caswell ramp and COHR's 6-inch yields both report again within two quarters.**
- **MACOM (off-coverage, plain text): SAM lifted to ~$15bn for 2027, up 20–30% from 2026, with data center at ~$6bn**, and programs across scale-up, scale-out and to some degree scale-across; considers both InP photonics and TIA/driver lines multibillion-dollar opportunities; growing customer interest in **coherent-lite for short-reach DC links**. 🔴 **Two supply items that matter more than the TAM: (a) a GENERAL SUPPLY SHORTAGE OF InP DFB LASERS — a third-party corroboration that extends this page's InP-tightness thread from EML and high-power CW to DFB, i.e. across the whole InP laser class rather than just the scarce high-power bins; and (b) its own CW LASER REACHES PRODUCTION IN LATE CY2027 AT THE EARLIEST, despite intense customer pull.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THAT CW-LASER DATE IS THE SECOND INDEPENDENT ENTRANT TO LAND ON THE SAME QUARTER — WHICH MATERIALLY STRENGTHENS THIS PAGE'S "THE MOAT THESIS HAS TO SURVIVE A 2028 SUPPLY RESPONSE" FRAMING.** The `## State of play` entry of 2026-07-26 (JPM · Jerry Tsai on WIN Semiconductors, OW) carries *"the CW laser project with a major US optical chip IDM (mass production should take place in YE27)"*, and the page's own verdict there was: *"the first dated timeline on this page for third-party merchant capacity entering the CW/ultra-high-power laser bottleneck… Not a 2026 threat — a 2028 one — but it is the supply response the moat thesis has to survive."* **MACOM now dates its own CW laser to late CY2027 at the earliest. Two independent merchant CW-laser entrants, two independent sources (a Taiwan foundry read and the company's own guidance), converging on YE27 / late-CY27.** ➜ **The framing upgrades in BOTH directions and both are load-bearing: (a) the moat is now corroborated as INTACT through CY27 — the second entrant says it cannot get there sooner *even with intense customer pull*, which is exactly the yield-and-qualification barrier PhotonCap defined (*"headline specs are what challengers close first. Distribution tails, noise, and lifetime statistics close last"*, 07-29) and which S5 below prices at ±30 GHz and 0.1 nm/K; and (b) the 2028 supply response is no longer one dated datapoint, it is a converging cohort — which is precisely the input Jefferies' C28 capacity-overshoot risk (08-05) needs, and it lands in the same year the page expects scale-up NPO/CPO volumes and the largest capacity additions. **The pricing gains, not the volumes, are what a converging cohort threatens first.** ⚠️ **ONE HYPOTHESIS TO TEST AND EXPLICITLY NOT ADOPTED: MACOM is itself "a major US optical chip IDM," so WIN's unnamed partner COULD be MACOM — which would collapse two entrants back into one program. Against that: MACOM runs its own InP fabs and the date language differs ("mass production… YE27" vs "production in late CY2027 at the earliest"). Unresolved. If they are the same program, the cohort argument in (b) weakens to a single entrant and (a) is unaffected. Check WIN's customer disclosure before using the cohort framing in a model.**
