Keysight** has scale-across in its documentation but did not discuss it on the May call; materials point to coherent-module validation at **800ZR/1600ZR**; management flagged **three speed transitions running at once (800G, 1.6T, 3.2T)** — and it reports again **2026-08-18** (both off-coverage, plain text). ➜ **"Three speed transitions at once" is the demand-side explanation for [[TER]]'s complaint that its own CPO test TAM range ($300-700m by 2028) is wide because port forecasts are *"all over the map"*, and for Chroma/Anritsu order strength. Keysight's print lands tomorrow — the nearest dated read on whether test intensity is compounding as claimed.** FUNDA's own conclusion on who wins: **the companies with deep InP platforms, close co-design and proven volume manufacturing.**

**🔴 (4) THE CHINESE-TRANSCEIVER BAN, RE-ARGUED FROM THE ENGINEERING SIDE — AND THE EML→SiPho PIVOT ARITHMETIC IS A NEW BEAR MECHANISM FOR THE InP-SCARCITY THESIS.**

_(Source: **Irrational Analysis · "[Market Memo] Orange Man Attempted Chinese Transceiver Ban", 2026-08-04.** ⚠️ Opinionated independent substack, self-declared long semis; the engineering claims are the value, the tone and the trading calls are not. Trigger: Reuters 2026-08-04, drafting restrictions on **new models** of Chinese datacenter optical transceivers, potentially via the FCC's equipment-authorization and Covered List frameworks — **no formal rule text released**. The parallel FUNDA note quoted inside stresses it is undefined whether "Chinese transceiver" would be set by place of manufacture, customs origin, incorporation, ownership/control or R&D/supply-chain location, and that the proposal *"could also be revised or shelved."*)_

- **THE SCOPE MECHANIC, AND IT IS THE MOST DEFLATIONARY READ ON THIS PAGE: only NEW models would be affected, and *"everyone already has the 1.6T flavours qualified,"* so it is *"not a huge deal"* — it becomes a real issue at **3.2T, "which is far away."*** ⚠️ **COMPARE, DO NOT DUPLICATE: this page already carries the same new-model-only construct from Jefferies (Blayne Curtis et al., 08-05), read there as *"phased, bullish COHR > LITE,"* with CRDO also a beneficiary and retaliation risk because US Tx firms manufacture in China; and from [[AAOI]] management at the JPM forum (08-13) as *"originally approved designs continue to be allowed to import; but as newer generations come out, you could seek to change the supply chain."* **All three agree on the MECHANISM and diverge on the MAGNITUDE: Jefferies converts it into a ranked beneficiary trade, JPM's Cardoso sized it at a *"$10B OPPORTUNITY FOR US SUPPLIERS"* (08-05), and Irrational Analysis says the qualified-1.6T carve-out makes it close to a non-event until 3.2T. The three are not reconcilable, and the discriminator is checkable: it is entirely about **when 3.2T models get submitted for authorization**, which is the same 2027/2028 seam CIEN's coherent-lite crossover and GS's 3.2T entry sit on.** Also rejects the security rationale on engineering grounds (a transceiver is a PHY, data is encrypted at host endpoints, CMIS register space is limited and carries signed public/private-key authentication per OIF-CMIS-05.4 §9.9.2).
- 🔴 **THE WORKAROUND, AND IT IS A THIRD INDEPENDENT SOURCE SAYING THE RELOCATION IS ALREADY IN PRODUCTION: *"Innolight, Eoptolink, Accelink and others have built 'MORE THAN A SHELL' US-registered entities with the footprint in SE Asia (Malaysia, Thailand, Vietnam) — IP licensed to the entity, and nearly all major production (TOSA/ROSA active alignment, PCB assembly, module assembly, burn-in) done outside mainland China."* Conclusion: *"It's easy to get Chinese transceivers that are technically/legally 'not Chinese'."*** ➜ **Converges with Goldman's 08-11 finding (*"Eoptolink… expanding PHASE 2 capacity in Thailand in 2026E with PHASE 1 ALREADY OPERATING AT FULL UTILIZATION RATE,"* producing high-end 1.6T) and with TrendForce's 08-06 caution that the vendor criteria are undefined. **Three sources from three different vantage points — a Chinese-supply-chain sell-side analyst, a Taiwan research house and an engineering substack — now say the same thing: a rule keyed to Chinese ORIGIN has a workaround running at scale today. The page's standing "unresolved question #1" (08-04: manufacturing IN China vs corporate domicile) is therefore not just unresolved, it is the ONLY question that matters, because the origin-keyed version is already defeated.**
- 🔴 **THE REAL CHINESE ADVANTAGE IS ACTIVE ALIGNMENT, NOT DESIGN — and this is the most useful net-new mechanism in the memo:** six-axis alignment at low-single-digit-micron (sometimes sub-micron) accuracy on custom in-house machines, where *"TFC, Inno, Accelink, Eopto wipe the floor with the west"* on yield, coupling efficiency and throughput — while **Fabrinet is *"the world's best non-Chinese optical contract manufacturer because everyone else is unusably dogshit."*** ➜ **Read this DIRECTLY against item (1) above, and the two sources were written independently 13 days apart: an engineering author identifies active-alignment capacity as the binding Western constraint and names Fabrinet as the only credible non-Chinese answer; Fabrinet then claims exactly that franchise (*"to be the leader in manufacturing and packaging near-packaged optics devices"*, *"for decades, our core strength has been transforming advanced photonics components into reliable high-volume systems"*) and sizes its physical capacity at 2.7-3.0× current revenue. **That is the cleanest supply-side pairing this ingest produced, and it relocates the ban debate: the constraint on re-shoring optical assembly is not fab capacity or lasers, it is alignment throughput — a capital-and-tacit-skill constraint, and the only Western vendor at scale is now visibly building for it.***
- 🔴🔴 **THE EML→SiPho PIVOT ARITHMETIC — A NEW BEAR MECHANISM AGAINST THE InP-SCARCITY THESIS THAT UNDERWRITES [[AXTI]], [[LITE]], [[COHR]] AND [[AAOI]] ON THIS PAGE: *"a SiPho transceiver has ~50% LESS InP content than an EML-based transceiver but significantly HIGHER active-alignment content. Everyone has an MZI-based PIC for 1.6T. It is trivial to pivot manufacturing from EML based to SiPho based. The issue is active alignment capacity/throughput, not the design itself."*** ➜ **This supplies the conversion factor that has been missing between two threads this page has been running in parallel without connecting them. The Jefferies expert call of 07-14 already says that WITHIN 1.6T, silicon photonics takes >60% share (from ~35% at 800G, with Innolight guiding ~80% SiPh on 1.6T) versus EML at 35-40%; GS's 08-10 model has SiPh crossing the MAJORITY OF DOLLARS in 2027 (56%, and 62% by 2028E). At ~50% less InP per module, a mix already migrating to SiPho is mechanically DEFLATING InP content per module at the same time as unit volumes inflate — so the InP shortage and the SiPho share shift are not two independent facts, they partially net.** ⚠️ **DO NOT over-net it, and the bounds matter: (a) SiPho modules still need an external CW laser, which is InP and is the highest-value bin, so the mix shift moves InP demand from EML toward CW rather than eliminating it — consistent with LITE's filed FY26 laser-chip ASP INCREASE; (b) the 07-14 call has SiPho at >60% of 1.6T units while GS has SiPh unit penetration at only 6%→18% across the whole market, so the two are on different denominators (1.6T vs total) and cannot be blended; (c) the InP shortage this page tracks is specifically at the HIGH-POWER bins and at substrate/epi, which the pivot does not relieve. **Net: the "InP demand compounds with every unit" version of the scarcity thesis is too loose and should be retired in favour of a per-bin version — EML InP demand is exposed to the SiPho pivot, CW/high-power InP demand is not. That is a real revision to how this page has been arguing AXTI and the substrate layer.***
- **THE TOWER READ, which corroborates the street-high thesis on this page with a mechanism rather than an assertion: [[LITE]], [[COHR]] and [[AAOI]] all use Tower for the SiPho PIC, so a production shift to SiPho STILL LANDS ON TOWER; and Tower's Openlight PDK for integrated InP-on-SiPho is *"very valuable in a world where active alignment efficiency becomes a bottleneck."*** ➜ **BofA's 08-07 street-high [[TSEM]] initiation (Buy, PO $367) rests on *"superior PIC technology"* that the page flagged as ASSERTED, not quantified. This is the mechanism the assertion was missing, and it is stronger than a share argument: Tower is the common PIC supplier across all three Western module names, so the EML→SiPho pivot — whether driven by the ban, by 1.6T mix, or by InP scarcity — routes incremental volume to Tower on every path. And the Openlight InP-on-SiPho PDK attacks the alignment bottleneck directly by integrating the step that requires alignment. Consistent with the SIG/TSEM fireside already in Sources (III-V-on-Si heterogeneous laser integration, OpenLight for 400G/lane).**
- **Net stance and the trading calls, logged with their bias: *"Whatever happens, this is good for Lumentum, Coherent, and AAOI"*; [[AAOI]] called out for massive transceiver capacity expansion, *"a fun degen play that has better short-term risk/reward than AXTI"*; Fabrinet wins. On [[AXTI]]: *"China probably will retaliate and that will result in a nice 15-20% single-day drop in $AXTI,"* long-term *"easily a 2-4x within the next 12-18 months"* but position sizing must survive headline risk (the author sold out on the day), and he mocks the AXTI-down/IQE-up reaction (*"where does IQE buy the InP wafers?"*).** ⚠️ **Trading calls and price targets from a self-declared long are NOT adopted. The retaliation MECHANISM is, and it is the same one MS named on 08-04 (China restricting bottlenecked InP via AXT) and Jefferies named on 08-05 (China-InP dependency under strict export controls) — three sources, one chokepoint, now with LITE's own 10-K confirming that its suppliers are subject to US export controls too.**

**🔴 (5) THE [[LITE]]-vs-[[COHR]] CPO-LASER *DISCLOSURE ASYMMETRY* GETS ITS SECOND DATED INSTANCE — AND IT CONNECTS TO THIS PAGE'S EXISTING VCSEL THREAD RATHER THAN STARTING A PARALLEL ONE.**

_(Source: **Irrational Analysis · "Coherent Q4 FY26 Earnings — The opposite of Lumentum", 2026-08-13.** ⚠️ **SAME AUTHOR AS THE "Optics deep-dive" OF 2026-05-08 ALREADY IN Sources, AND SUBSTANTIALLY THE SAME THESIS RESTATED THREE MONTHS LATER WITH A NEW QUARTER AS EVIDENCE — the 05-08 note already said only LITE publicly discloses linewidth and RIN, *"no other company dares,"* and was already bearish on COHR 6-inch InP yields on the highest-value classes. This is a SECOND DATED INSTANCE OF ONE VIEW, NOT A SECOND SOURCE. Self-declared long semis.)_

- **THE THESIS: *"Coherent's call was crap and so were their numbers… The opposite of Lumentum."*** The argument is a process of elimination — the *"higher revenue mix is transceivers"* explanation does not account for the margin gap, therefore ***"the only way COHR's numbers make sense is if CPO-laser yield is bad."*** **COHR discloses no CPO-laser yield data**, and the author's repeated demand is specific: ***"YIELD EML AT WHAT DATARATE? YIELD CW LASER AT WHAT POWER CLASS?"*** He notes an MS analyst asked the leading version on the call (*"is your miniscule gross margin expansion [vs Lumentum] because of mix shift or 6in yield?"*), and challenges Jim Anderson to publish the plots LITE published, predicting he will not.
- 🔴🔴 **THE COLLISION WITH THIS PAGE'S OWN RECORD IS DIRECT AND MUST BE HELD OPEN, NOT NETTED.** Against *"their numbers were crap"*, the page carries from the same print: **[[COHR]] guided FQ1 FY27 gross margin to 41.5% vs Street 40.0% — i.e. UP — on a quarter that delivered GM +215bps y/y and operating margin +381bps y/y** (COHR release, 08-12); **InP output on track to double by F1Q27-end and more than double again by CY27-end, with InP laser production +80% y/y in F4Q26 underpinning data-center revenue growth *"to exceed +80% y/y"* in F1Q27** (JPM · Cardoso, 08-13); **>75% of capacity on 6-inch by CY27, *"4x supply at half the cost"*** (BofA, 08-13); and **Redburn reading the print as pointing to FY27 revenue *"in the region of $10.6bn"*** (08-13). ⚠️ **Both cannot be dismissed. What is genuinely established is narrower than either side claims: (a) COHR's GM expansion was small in ABSOLUTE terms and small RELATIVE to LITE's 50.4%, and the ~10pt gap is unchanged; (b) COHR has published NO yield data at any power class or data rate, so the yield hypothesis is unfalsifiable from outside; and (c) there is now a mundane alternative explanation from LITE's own 10-K (item 2 above): 54% of LITE's FY26 GM-dollar increase came from FACTORY UTILIZATION and only 29% from mix — and utilization is precisely the lever a capacity-constrained issuer mid-way through a 3"→6" transition has least of. **The page's position: the disclosure asymmetry is real and is the tradeable fact; the yield diagnosis is a hypothesis with a plausible competitor.***
- **LITE'S PUBLISHED CPO-LASER DATA, WALKED THROUGH — and this is the substantive value of the note regardless of the tone:** at **400mW optical output the drive-current spread is 1.2A to ~1.38A**; **power-conversion efficiency 21–24%** against an industry want of **25–30%**, which LITE hits at 40C; **RIN *"over 10x lower noise than spec,"*** where the general CPO-laser spec is **−145 dBc/Hz**; linewidth *"excellent,"* best at mid-power (200mW); relaxation oscillation ~3GHz with a feature at 15GHz. Data specified at **50C, *"frankly a pessimistic case."*** **At OFC 2026 LITE's ELSFP live demo showed module-level wall-plug efficiency of 13% against an industry target of 10%** — but the demo held the laser diodes at 30C with aggressive/unrealistic cooling; **at a realistic 40C die / 50C coldplate the WPE would be ~10–11%, *"which is still great."*** ⚠️ **The author's claim that LITE is making ~80% gross margin on its CPO laser (while COHR's *"we sell more than lasers"* answer means selling isolators and FAUs at far lower value) is HIS CONSTRUCTION, not a disclosure, and is not adopted — but note it is directionally consistent with LITE's filed FY26 laser-chip ASP INCREASE and with the *"CW die-shrink closed the margin gap considerably"* transcript line.** ➜ **The derating from 13% to ~10-11% WPE at realistic thermals is the item to carry forward: it is the first instance on this page of a published CPO-optics spec being re-derated to operating conditions, and it lands the ELSFP module essentially AT the industry target rather than above it — which tightens rather than loosens the CPO power-budget argument that PhotonCap called the real driver (*"Copper is not forcing this wave; the power budget is"*).**
- 🔴 **THE YIELD-MOAT ARGUMENT, WHICH IS THE PART THAT SHOULD OUTLIVE THE NOTE: DWDM CPO needs ±30 GHz wavelength-spacing accuracy (versus very lax CWDM spacing), and off-target lasers must be thermally tuned at ~0.1 nm/K — which pushes the TEC toward reverse/heating mode with worse output power, worse efficiency and worse electrical noise.** The author uses it as explicit push-back on the *"flood of Chinese competition is coming for LITE/AVGO lasers"* narrative. ➜ **This is the physical mechanism behind PhotonCap's working moat definition already adopted on this page (*"qualified usable power at operating temperature × spectral quality × reliability × high-volume yield (RIN / COD / SMSR / FIT)"* — *"headline specs are what challengers close first. Distribution tails, noise, and lifetime statistics close last"*, 07-29). It is also the best available explanation for WHY item (3)'s two merchant CW entrants both land at YE27/late-CY27 despite intense customer pull: ±30 GHz binning yield is not a capacity problem you can buy your way out of. **Hold the two together: the yield-moat argument and the converging-entrant cohort are the bull and bear halves of the same physics, and 2028 is when they collide.***
- **THE 6-INCH "CONSPIRACY", logged as a hypothesis and it foots with the page's substrate record:** 6-inch *should* yield BETTER (better MOCVD reactors, semi-modern ASML litho), so bad yield may not be COHR's fault — possibly **6-inch InP wafer uniformity from the supplier (Sumitomo)**; and **[[AXTI]] does not yet have 6-inch wafers (still R&D)**. ✅ **Consistent with what this page already carries: MS's 08-03 AXT read that *"6-INCH WAFERS ARE PROGRESSING BUT HAVE NOT REACHED VOLUME PRODUCTION,"* and the structure of the two AXT LTAs (LITE's reserves capacity through 2031; COHR's *"supports the DEVELOPMENT AND SUPPLY OF 6-INCH WAFERS"* — i.e. 6-inch is a development program, not a supply line). ➜ **If the 6-inch yield problem is a SUBSTRATE-UNIFORMITY problem rather than a process problem, it is not fixable by COHR on its own schedule, and BofA's *"4x supply at half the cost"* by CY27 is hostage to a supplier that has not reached volume. That is a materially different risk from an execution risk, and it is a checkable one.***
- 🔴 **THE VCSEL LEG — CONNECTED TO THE EXISTING THREAD, NOT RESTARTED.** The note repeats the objection first logged here from the same author's 05-08 deep-dive: the optimal data rate for a VCSEL is **32G or 64G NRZ**, and **200G PAM4 on a VCSEL *"will not work from a reliability perspective… going to fail GR-468"*** — and even if it passed, energy efficiency suffers (driver complexity, FEC for crosstalk, Rx TIA sensitivity burden). It asks whether COHR's **"PhotonLink"** is the brand for its VCSEL CPO/NPO, and concedes COHR *"has great VCSELs, second only to Broadcom."* ⚠️ **THE PAGE ALREADY HOLDS THE REBUTTAL AND IT IS NOT REFUTED BY THIS RESTATEMENT: PhotonCap's answer (07-29) was that the AI VCSEL use case is NOT a high-speed serial one — LITE showed at OFC 2026 a 1060nm VCSEL + photodetector 2D monolithic array co-packaged with a host ASIC targeting *slow-and-wide* protocols such as UCIe and PCIe, above 150°C, with channel sparing, on a base of >10bn emitters shipped. **The two claims do not contradict: "200G PAM4 per VCSEL lane fails GR-468" and "many slow NRZ lanes in parallel work" are compatible, and the second is the architecture actually being demonstrated. So the 05-08 dismissal and its 08-13 restatement are both attacking a use case the vendors are not pursuing.** What remains genuinely open, and is the right question, is whether the slow-and-wide VCSEL path wins any DESIGN — it has no announced socket on this page — and whether LightCounting's InP-91%→79% / VCSEL-9%→21% lane-share migration survives the GR-468 objection at whatever data rate the winning architecture picks.**

**(6) [[NVDA]] UNIT LADDER — THE MIZUHO NUMBERS RELAYED HERE ON 08-17 ARE NOW SOURCED TO THE PRIMARY, AND THE PRIMARY ADDS ONE LINE THE RELAY DROPPED (plus a unit collision the relay did not surface).**

_(Source: **Mizuho · Vijay Rakesh (with Dale Gai in Japan, Kevin Wang in HK) — "AI Server Call: 2027 VR with HBM and NPO/CPO, Adv. Packaging EMIB-T/CoPoS Ramps", 2026-08-16.** ⚠️ **RELAY→PRIMARY UPGRADE: item 7 of the Aug-17 (PM) section below carries these same marks as *"Mizuho via TMT Breakout, 2026-08-17."* The primary is dated 08-16 and the relayed numbers tie exactly — no unit corruption, no range compression, which is worth logging because this page's standing rule is that relays distort. LITE is Outperform, PT $1,140 at px $926.14.)_

- **THE MARKS, PRIMARY-CONFIRMED: initial NPO/CPO ramps with Quantum-X / Spectrum-X in late-2026E, accelerating into 2028E with VR-Ultra; CPO/Spectrum-X ~80–100K in 2027E and 130K+ in 2028E, with Spectrum-X6/7 at 80%+ of the mix.**
- 🆕 **THE LINE THE RELAY DROPPED, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS TO THE FRAMEWORK: *"laser suppliers' capacity is FUNGIBLE between NPO and CPO, with SIMILAR-TO-HIGHER CONTENT ON NPO (positive LITE)."*** ✅ **THIS FOOTS — it is a third independent vote on NPO content ≥ CPO content, and the page's guardrail convention is satisfied rather than tripped. It agrees with [[COHR]]'s *"the amount of content we would have in an NPO application versus a CPO is very comparable"* (08-12), with LITE's own content-parity claim, and it is the mild version of Hurlston's stronger JPM-forum line that NPO engagement INTENSITY (number of optical lanes) is *"actually HIGHER than NVDA['s CPO]… considerably higher"* (08-13). And the FUNGIBILITY half is a separate, useful fact: if the laser capacity is interchangeable between the two architectures, then the NPO-vs-CPO mix question stops being a capital-allocation risk for [[LITE]] and [[COHR]] and becomes purely a content/ASP question — which is the mechanism behind MS's 08-03 offset (*"much of the underlying LASER AND OPTICAL-COMPONENT STACK IS SHARED between NPO and CPO, so LITE and COHR are positioned either way"*), now stated as capacity fungibility rather than as design similarity.**
- 🔴 **THE UNIT COLLISION THE RELAY DID NOT SURFACE, AND IT IS ON *IDENTICAL* UNITS — unlike the GF cross-check already flagged in item 7 below (switch systems vs optical engines, correctly refused). Mizuho's CPO switch-system counts sit FAR BELOW the Aurelion / Global Semi Research channel numbers this page carries in Route B: 80–100K (2027E) vs 200,000–300,000 (2027), i.e. Mizuho is 2.0–3.75× LOWER; and 130K+ (2028E) vs 600,000–800,000 (2028), i.e. 4.6–6.2× LOWER. Growth shape also collides: Mizuho compounds ~1.4× from 2027 to 2028 against the channel's ~2.7×.** ✅ **AND THE COLLISION RESOLVES IN MIZUHO'S FAVOUR ON PROVENANCE, WHICH IS WHY IT MATTERS: the framework already establishes that the channel counts are *"pure back-solves of the NVDA CW-laser guide ÷ 144 (40m÷144≈278k; 100m÷144≈694k) — zero independent information."* Mizuho's is an independent forecast. Inverting it on the page's own convention: 100K Spectrum-X CPO switches × 144 CW lasers/system ≈ 14.4m CW lasers in 2027E — only ~14-19% of the 40m→100m NVDA CW guide the channel divided up. ➜ **Either that CW guide covers far more than Spectrum-X scale-out CPO (NPO, scale-up, ELS modules — which is what LITE's and COHR's own commentary implies) or the channel back-solve materially over-counts CPO switches. On this evidence Route B's ~$22-43bn 2028 CPO figure should be treated as the outer bull pole it already is, and the page's Route C reading (single-digit-to-10% attach) gains support.** ⚠️ **FLAGGED, NOT NETTED, per the guardrail: three incompatible 2027-28 CPO unit sizings now sit on this page (Mizuho 80-100K '27 / 130K+ '28; channel 200-300k '27 / 600-800k '28; SemiAnalysis 10-15k CPO switch units in 2026 as the starting point) and unit volume is still not forecastable until NVDA's rack architecture is fixed.**
- **Context from the same primary, for the record and cross-filed elsewhere: VR200 NVL72 production on track for a SepQ launch with DecQ volume ramp, potentially 2.5–3K NVL72 racks in 2026E driven by [[META]] and xAI; 2027E >50K VR racks and >20K GB, with NVDA limited by CoWoS and DC power; VR-Ultra 2028E potentially only 2-die (from 4-die) and Kyber (Feynman) delayed with midplane headwinds; CoWoS +>75% y/y in 2027E; CSP capex +111%/+50% in 2026E/2027E to ~$1T/~$1.5T. Preferred names into 2027E: DELL / CRDO / NVDA / AVGO / LITE.** ⚠️ **The note's CSP RPO/backlog figure carries an internal inconsistency (summary *"up 3.5x y/y"* vs body *"up >250% y/y"*, while the stated endpoints ~$735bn in 1Q25 → ~$2.3T now are 3.1× / +213% and are NOT y/y) — endpoints logged, neither growth rate adopted.**

## Recent signals (Aug 17, PM — /wiki-ingest) — 🔴🔴 **THE CPO TIMELINE SPLITS ON STAGE AT OCP APAC: [[NVDA]] AND [[AVGO]] BOTH SAY "IN PRODUCTION", THE OSAT THAT ACTUALLY BUILDS THE PACKAGES SAYS THE ECOSYSTEM IS NOT READY — AND THE RESOLUTION IS THAT *NPO IS ADDITIVE, NOT A BRIDGE THAT GETS TORN DOWN***

_(Source: **FundaAI · "Research | Optics & Power: OCP APAC Summit Takeaways", 2026-08-17** — the house attended the summit in Taipei. Company-page rows filed on [[LITE]], [[COHR]], [[ALAB]], [[TSEM]], [[ADVANTEST]], [[CRDO]], [[AAOI]], [[AXTI]], [[AIXA]].)_

**1. The disagreement, stated plainly.** "Nvidia and Broadcom both assert that they have **CPO in production**. **ASE, one of the OSATs that assemble and test CPO packages, said the ecosystem is not ready.**" ASE's own slides list the open problems — *new system architectures, optical engine 3D package integration, assembly yield, fiber connectivity, known-good optical engine testing, thermal-mechanical interaction* — and **ASE sizes the CPO market at $1.45bn in 2025 rising to only $4.12bn by 2034**, with packages scaling past 120×120 mm and more than nine reticles of silicon. **[[ALAB]] dates it: NPO production readiness NOW, CPO 2027 and beyond**, arguing CPO field replaceability is limited and standards are still in progress.
- What each vendor actually has: **NVDA — Spectrum-X Photonics, a CPO chip on micro-ring modulators built as a 3D-stacked silicon photonics engine on TSMC's COUPE process, claiming 4× fewer lasers, 5× lower power, 10× higher MTBI** vs pluggable-transceiver switch systems. **AVGO — three generations shipped (TH4 Humboldt 2021, TH5 Bailly 2023, TH6 Davisson 2025, billed as the first 100T CPO Ethernet switch) with a fourth tracking for 2027.**
- 🟢 **The resolution FundaAI lands on is the single most useful line for this page: "NPO is an intermediate, ADDITIVE step rather than a cannibalistic threat to CPO. Companies that benefit from both, such as Lumentum, Coherent and Tower Semiconductor, are well positioned while the CPO timeline stays uncertain."** ➜ This directly supports the **Aug 12 "additive TAM"** framing already on this page and cuts against reading a CPO slip as a demand loss.
- ⚠️ **Watch item FundaAI itself flags: "Will Broadcom's fourth CPO generation hold to the 2027 date on its own slide?"**

**2. Copper's ceiling got a number — and a counter-simulation in the same note.** **GlobalFoundries data: direct-attach copper cannot span a single 2-metre rack at 400 Gbps/lane; an AEC cannot span more than two such racks and draws more power than optical at 400 Gbps/lane; optical spans beyond 500 m and carries >10 Tbps per fiber.** **[[ALAB]] simulates 14.5 dB insertion loss at 53 GHz for 224G against 48.0 dB at 112 GHz for 448G**, arguing copper at 200 Gbps/lane is manageable only with power-hungry DSP equalisation and at 400 Gbps is "prohibitive beyond centimeters." ⚠️ **But TE Connectivity and Semtech presented 448G simulations at OCP EMEA where PAM6 closes a 1-metre passive channel through co-packaged copper connectors, with active re-drivers reaching past 1.75 m and the re-driver models correlated to measured Semtech silicon. FundaAI's own verdict: "We view this as potential for copper, not definitive."** Ayar Labs' framing of why any of this matters: **token demand compounding ~10× a year against compute supply ~2× (1.4× power × 1.4× silicon), leaving a ~5×/yr gap for architecture and software to close** — and it calls copper "the enemy of Open Compute."

**3. Rubin Ultra content, and the HBM de-spec rumour that will not die.** Industry discussion of the GTC-2026 rack form factors has **Rubin Ultra NVL576 on eight Oberon racks (copper backplane inside each rack, CPO + NPO between them)** and **Feynman NVL1152 on eight Kyber racks (PCB midplane + flyover cables inside, CPO/OCI-MSA between)**. 🔴 **"A possible Rubin Ultra HBM DE-SPEC came up repeatedly in our team's conversations at OCP APAC. The version being discussed moves Rubin Ultra NVL72 on HBM4E 12-Hi at 384GB to Rubin Ultra 'Lite' NVL576 on HBM4 8-Hi at 192GB"** — the same de-spec thread carried on [themes/hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md) since Aug 10, now sourced from a third venue. **Scale-up content per GPU in the same discussion: $3,812 post-margin on NVL72 Oberon vs $11,265 on NVL576 — nearly a tripling; per domain, $274,492 across 72 GPUs vs $6.49mn across 576.** ⚠️ Unverified industry discussion, not an NVDA disclosure — FundaAI lists confirmation of the memory stack height, capacity and the 576-GPU domain as its own open watch item.

**4. Test is the pick-and-shovel nobody is pricing.** **ASE counts FOUR test insertions per CPO package** — EIC and PIC wafer test, chip-on-wafer test (needing a double-sided wafer prober with active alignment), optical engine unit test, and CPO package test. **GlobalFoundries' known-good-optical-engine flow is built on [[ADVANTEST]] 93K ExaScale tools** (200 Gbps PAM4 electrical and optical, 1,024+ source-measure units, up to 224 Gbps BER testing directly on wafer) and GF says it "is easy to deploy to OSATs." **FundaAI: "Every CPO package goes through these test steps regardless of who builds it. Advantest is the only test equipment vendor named at OCP… We see test as a CPO play without betting on a single winner."** Thermals, for sizing the packaging problem: **GUC puts a 120×130 mm package at 3.3 kW with optical engines at 84.6°C against an 85°C spec** (HBM 84.6 vs 95, SoC/IO 78.7 vs 125), on a Wiwynn cold-plate co-design assuming 45°C coolant inlet and 1.5 L/min per kW.

**5. The [[NVDA]] partner map, verbatim from the NVLink Fusion slide.** Optical interconnect: **Ayar Labs, Lightmatter, [[MRVL]]**. Custom silicon: **Alchip, [[ALAB]], GUC, [[MRVL]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[SAMSUNG]]**. Technology: **[[CDNS]], [[SNPS]]**. CPU: **[[ARM]], Fujitsu, [[INTC]], SiFive**.

**6. Upstream, the substrate is tightening for the third time this year.** **"AI-driven demand for optical networking is causing severe shortages of indium phosphide (InP) substrates and epitaxial wafers, with industry sources expecting another 10%+ price increase in 4Q26 — the 3rd price increase for substrates and second for wafers this year"** (UDN via Jefferies · Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-17). ⚠️ **This directly contradicts the "substrate bottleneck is EASING" item logged on [[AXTI]] on Aug 4 — two weeks apart, mutually exclusive, and the 4Q26 print settles it.** Both are kept. Chain exposure: [[AXTI]] (substrates), [[AIXA]] (MOCVD tools), [[LITE]]/[[COHR]] (lasers), [[AAOI]] (modules — for whom the hike lands in COGS before it lands in price). **[[COHR]] +8% and [[LITE]] +5% on 08-17.**

**7. Mizuho's unit ladder, for the record.** **Initial NPO/CPO ramps tied to Quantum-X / Spectrum-X begin late 2026 and accelerate into 2028 with VR-Ultra; CPO/Spectrum-X reaching ~80-100K units in 2027 and 130K+ in 2028, with Spectrum-X6/7 >80% of the mix; CoWoS growth >75% y/y in 2027** (Mizuho via TMT Breakout, 2026-08-17). ⚠️ Cross-check against the **GF Securities NPO OE forecast in the Aug 17 (AM) section above (11mn 2027E → 40mn 2028E)** — the two are on different units (switch systems vs optical engines) and must not be compared directly. 🆕 **2026-08-17 night — RELAY→PRIMARY: these marks are now sourced to the primary (Mizuho · Vijay Rakesh, "AI Server Call: 2027 VR with HBM and NPO/CPO, Adv. Packaging EMIB-T/CoPoS Ramps", 2026-08-16) and the relayed numbers TIE EXACTLY — no unit corruption, no range compression. The primary adds one line the relay dropped: *"laser suppliers' capacity is FUNGIBLE between NPO and CPO, with similar-to-higher content on NPO (positive LITE)."* 🔴 **And it surfaces a collision the relay did not: on IDENTICAL units, Mizuho's 80-100K (2027E) / 130K+ (2028E) CPO switch systems sit 2.0-3.75× and 4.6-6.2× BELOW the Aurelion/Global Semi channel counts (200-300k '27 / 600-800k '28) carried in Route B of the HOUSE TAM BRIDGE — which the framework already labels a pure back-solve of the NVDA CW guide ÷ 144 with "zero independent information." Full working in the Aug-17 night section at the head of this page. Flagged, not netted.**

**8. The scale-across leg gets a bottom-up TAM and a reported-revenue corroboration.** **Rothschild & Co Redburn (Mike Harrison), reiterating BUY on [[LITE]] with PT $1,270 on 2026-08-17: "the scale-across market could expand from ~$0.5bn today to more than $20bn by 2030," with LITE holding 70-80% of pump lasers, "sold out for the foreseeable future," pump shipments +80% y/y and narrow-linewidth +130% in 4Q, and 1.6T going "from roughly 10% to 50% of Ethernet switches over the next year."** Management arithmetic for the CPO leg: **~8,000 lasers per scale-up rack → roughly $750mn of revenue per GW of AI capacity**, ~15% of LITE's ~$5bn annualised run-rate. ⚠️ **Note the 5× spread against the X-sourced "$100bn+ scale-across / 20-40mn DCI ports" estimate carried in the Aug 14 section — use Redburn's bottom-up >$20bn as the anchor.** Corroboration from a printed quarter: **Fabrinet FQ4 optical communications +7.6% q/q / +39% y/y "driven very largely by DCI +30% Q/Q"** — but ⚠️ **Datacom was DOWN on the quarter ($258mn vs $271mn consensus), which was the KPI the market wanted up, and FN fell 6.5%** (JPM · Joshua Meyers, "FN First Blush" and "FN Off the Call"; Vital Knowledge, 2026-08-17). **On the call FN was "optimistic" on NPO "which sounds like it could be happening sooner," said Datacom demand is "robust and accelerating" into F1Q, and that customer visibility extends "well into end of 2027 and beyond."**

## Recent signals (Aug 17) — 🔴 **GF SECURITIES PUTS THE FIRST UNIT FORECAST ON *NPO OPTICAL ENGINES* — 11MN 2027E → 40MN 2028E. DECODED, IT IS NOT A CONTENT CALL: IT IS A ~4× ATTACH CALL ON NVDA PLUS AN AWS/OTHER-CSP LEG THE HOUSE BRIDGE DOES NOT CARRY AT ALL.**

_(Source: **GF Securities (Hong Kong) Brokerage**, "Figure 1: NPO OE Shipments Forecast (mn units)", sources line "Company data, GF Securities (Hong Kong) Brokerage" — **pasted exhibit, broker CONFIRMED by Felipe at ingest 2026-08-17; ⚠️ THE NOTE DATE WAS NOT CAPTURED AND THE UNDERLYING NOTE IS NOT ON DISK.** Treat the date as unknown, not as 2026-08-17. Pull the PDF into `_inbox/` when available.)_

**The exhibit as printed (mn units):**

| NPO OE for scale-up | 2027E | 2028E |
|---|--:|--:|
| Nvidia's 3.2T NPO | 6 | 19 |
| AWS' 6.4T NPO | 5 | 12 |
| Other CSPs' NPO | — | 9 |
| **Total scale-up NPO OE** | **11** | **40** |

- 🔴 **WHY THIS MATTERS MORE THAN ANOTHER TAM PRINT: it is the first source on this page to forecast NPO in UNITS with named customers, and units are exactly the variable the house TAM bridge above declares unresolved** (*"that is the residual fork to close (NVL576 volumes, first OE/ELS pricing), not to average"*). It is also the first mark that sizes **AWS' NPO as comparable to NVDA's** — 5 of 11mn OEs in 2027E — which corroborates the 650 Group's 08-14 line that NPO's early adopters are **[[AMZN]], [[NVDA]] and [[AVGO]]** running *outside* TSMC's COUPE gate, and cuts against the NVDA-centric construction of Routes A and C.
- 🔴 **DECODED INTO AN ATTACH RATE, WHICH IS THE ONLY WAY TO COMPARE IT TO ANYTHING ELSE ON THIS PAGE. Taking 3.2T as per-OE bandwidth and Rubin scale-up at 14.4 Tbit/s/GPU (CPO Book) ⇒ ~4.5 OEs per GPU ⇒ GF's NVDA line implies ~1.3mn attached GPUs in 2027E (~20% of the 6.6mn GS rack-scale base) and ~4.2mn in 2028E (~39% of 10.7mn).** Against the house Route C neutral default of **10% attach in 2028** and the Redburn-implied **~6.9%**, GF is running **~4× the house attach on NVDA alone** — and then adds AWS and other CSPs on top. ⚠️ **This is the single most useful thing in the exhibit: GF is not disagreeing about content or price, it is disagreeing about HOW FAST scale-up optics penetrates, and by roughly 4×.**
- 🔴 **AND IT FAILS THE TAM RECONCILIATION GATE HARD — logged as a red flag, not adopted.** Total bandwidth: **51mn Tbps 2027E, 178mn Tbps 2028E**. Priced at either self-consistent content pole on this page — Redburn's (111 lasers/GPU × $19/chip ÷ 14.4T = **$146/Tbps of laser**) or the CPO Book's (144 CW × $82 ÷ 115.2T = **$102/Tbps**) — and grossed at the 33/67 laser split, GF's units imply **$16bn of NPO optics in 2027E and $55bn in 2028E**. That is **~7× house Route C's 2028 ($7.9bn), ~10× Route A's CPO 2028 ($5.7bn), ~8× LightCounting's entire scale-up slice, and 62% of BofA's ENTIRE CY30 optical TAM two years early.** ⚠️ **GUARDRAIL RED. GF's units and this page's content marks cannot both be right.**
- **INVERTING IT LOCALIZES THE DISAGREEMENT, AND IT IS NOT IN THE CONTENT.** For GF's units to fit house Route A/C, NPO optics would have to price at **~$32–44 per Tbps** — versus **$309–442/Tbps** from the two bottom-up BOM poles and **$374/Tbps for a 1.6T pluggable** (GS CY27 ASP $599 ÷ 1.6T). That would require NPO to be **~10× cheaper per bit than a pluggable**, which contradicts [[COHR]]'s *"the amount of content we would have in an NPO application versus a CPO is very comparable"* (2026-08-12) and LITE's own content-parity claim. ➜ **So the gap sits in GF's UNITS, not in the $/bit. Read the exhibit as a bull-case attach curve, and do NOT cross-multiply it with the BOM content on this page.**
- ⚠️ **THE ONE UNRESOLVED READ, AND IT MOVES EVERYTHING: is "3.2T"/"6.4T" the PER-OE line rate, or a platform/product name?** Per-OE is the strong reading — SemiAnalysis has AMD's MI500 at **9 optical engines of 6.4 Tbit/s uni-di each** (2026-07-24), an exact match for AWS' "6.4T" label — and the clean 2× ratio between the two rows supports it. But if the labels are platform names and the true per-OE rate is a few hundred G, every bandwidth and dollar figure above falls by that ratio and the reconciliation gate closes on its own. **Confirm against the note text before this is used in a model.** A second, smaller ambiguity: uni-directional vs bi-directional (a 2× swing). 🆕 **2026-08-17 night — PARTIAL SUPPORT FOR THE PER-OE READING, FROM THE ASSEMBLY SIDE: Fabrinet's Q4 FY26 call (company primary, 2026-08-17) describes *"as NPO scales to 6.4, 12.8 terabit and beyond, the manufacturing complexity and YIELD becomes increasingly important"* — a contract manufacturer using those numbers as the axis along which DEVICE yield gets harder is talking about a device, not a product family, which is consistent with SemiAnalysis's AMD MI500 spec (9 optical engines at 6.4 Tbit/s uni-di each, 07-24). ⚠️ Supportive, NOT conclusive — Grady never says "per optical engine," and the uni-vs-bi-directional 2× ambiguity is untouched.**
- **A METHOD BYPRODUCT WORTH KEEPING — repricing the fork per unit of bandwidth largely dissolves it.** The page logs the Redburn-vs-CPO-Book content fork as *"4-7× apart, a genuine content disagreement"* on $/chip ($19 vs $82). But the two poles also disagree **6.2× in the OPPOSITE direction on lasers-per-Tbps** (7.7 vs 1.25), so the products nearly cancel: **$146/Tbps vs $102/Tbps — only 1.4× apart.** ⚠️ **Suggestive, not proof: the two are different systems (a scale-up GPU vs a scale-out switch), and that alone could explain a 1.4× spread. But it is the first framing on this page in which the two poles nearly agree, and it says the $/chip fork is substantially a laser-COUNTING convention rather than a price disagreement. Worth testing when the first OE/ELS pricing is disclosed.**
- **Read-through to [[LITE]]:** at the house attach curve, NPO is worth roughly **$0.1–0.3bn to LITE in CY27E (1.7–4.3% of the $7,884m consensus) and $0.8–3.4bn in CY28E**; at GF's literal units it is **$8.1bn in CY28E, which exceeds LITE's entire current consensus revenue** — the cleanest single demonstration that the exhibit cannot be taken literally at BOM content. Full working on [../LITE.md](../LITE.md). Also relevant to [[COHR]] (second ELS source), [[AAOI]], [[AVGO]] and [[AMZN]].

## Recent signals (Aug 14) — 🔴 **THE 650 GROUP PUTS A UNIT NUMBER ON *SCALE-ACROSS* (DCI PORTS <1MN → 20-40MN BY END-DECADE, TAM $100BN+), SAYS THE SCALE-UP CROSSOVER IS ~2031 NOT NOW, AND NAMES **NPO AS THE PRAGMATIC BRIDGE** — WITH [[AMZN]], [[NVDA]] AND [[AVGO]] ALL EXPLORING IT *OUTSIDE* TSMC's CONSTRAINED COUPE PLATFORM**

**Source: J.P. Morgan HW & Semis Management Access Forum — 650 Group · Alan Weckel, plus a private-company optical panel (Ranovus, Avicena), relayed by JPM · Joshua Meyers, "Chips for Breakfast", 2026-08-14.** ⚠️ **Sales-desk summary of a third-party analyst panel; no ratings, PTs or estimate changes attached.**

- 🔴 **THE SCALE-ACROSS SIZING, which is the piece this page has had least hard data on: "**DCI PORTS EXPAND FROM <1MN TODAY TO 20-40MN BY END OF DECADE, with the TAM CROSSING $100BN+ BY EARLY NEXT DECADE — 30% LINKED TO 100KM+ USE CASES** requiring coherent optics, routers, or Jericho-type L3 switches."** ➔ **Direct read-through to [[CSCO]], [[ANET]] and the coherent vendors ([[LITE]], [[COHR]], [[CIEN]]). Note the 30% qualifier: only that slice needs true coherent/long-haul, so do not apply the whole $100bn TAM to the coherent names.**
- 🔴 **THE SCALE-UP TIMELINE, AND IT CUTS AGAINST THE NEAR-TERM CPO BULLS: "the NEAR-TERM WINNER IS THE EMBEDDED/NVLINK ARCHITECTURE (benefiting CLS), but the END-OF-DECADE TRANSITION TOWARD DISAGGREGATED SCALE-UP SWITCHING FLIPS THE TAILWIND TOWARD ANET AND CSCO," with "**$100K+ ASP SWITCH SYSTEMS PLAUSIBLE** in next-gen builds as ASIC density and optical-engine requirements scale."** **On CPO itself: "**CPO TIMELINES ARE LARGELY INTACT BUT VOLUMES WILL BE LIMITED IN SCALE-OUT INITIALLY — the MORE MEANINGFUL CROSSOVER IS IN SCALE-UP AROUND ~2031**."** ➔ **This is a third independent voice on the same fence this page has been sitting on since Aug 3 (NVDA says CPO is in mass production; MS says the near-term architecture is NPO). 650's version dates the real volume crossover to ~2031 — later than the bulls, and it does so without disputing that CPO ships today.**
- 🔴 **THE NPO ITEM IS THE MOST ACTIONABLE LINE IN THE PANEL: "NPO is positioned as the **PRAGMATIC NEAR-TERM BRIDGE**, with **[[AMZN]], [[NVDA]], AND [[AVGO]] ALL EXPLORING IT OUTSIDE TSMC'S CONSTRAINED COUPE PLATFORM**."** ➔ **If the three largest buyers are routing NPO around COUPE, then the COUPE/SoIC capacity constraint tracked on this page (Aug 2) is being solved by ARCHITECTURE rather than by capacity — which is bullish for merchant optical-engine suppliers and less bullish for the TSMC-platform-gated CPO path specifically.**
- **THE CUSTOM-SILICON TAM EXPANDER, named: "the **FLEET MODEL — MULTIPLE ASIC TYPES PER CUSTOMER ACROSS TRAINING AND INFERENCE**," which "broadens the opportunity for incumbent leaders while opening niches for newer entrants." And on supply: "constraints are concentrated in **INCREMENTAL UPSIDE TO ORDERS RATHER THAN PLANNED BUILDS**, favoring vendors with concentrated, predictable customer sets over those managing broad installed bases."**

**The private-company panel (Ranovus, Avicena) — two contrasting bets, both arguing interconnect is the next bottleneck:**
- **RANOVUS: building a full-stack NORTH-AMERICA-BASED CPO/NPO supply chain — owning photonics, lasers, TIA and control — with **NPO EXPECTED TO BE >HALF OF REVENUES NEXT YEAR AND CPO CONTRACTS RAMPING IN 2028**. Its 3.2T integrated chip (driver, TIA, photonics, photodetector, control) is "architected around **FIELD SERVICEABILITY**, a deliberate design choice that differentiates it from being absorbed into the NVDA or AVGO supply chains." **Announced customers: [[AMD]] (merchant silicon), [[MEDIATEK]] (custom silicon), and [[CEREBRAS]] (wafer-scale).**
- **AVICENA: the radical bet — **REPLACING LASERS ENTIRELY WITH MICROLEDs** driven directly at a few Gbps per lane, eliminating SerDes, DSP retiming and complex modulation, and escaping edge-I/O constraints by transmitting light through the FULL 2D DIE SURFACE via extruded plastic fiber packing **1,500-2,000 CORES PER SQUARE MILLIMETRE** — targeting the few-millimetre to 20-metre interconnect range.**
- ⚠️ **BOTH CEOs "converged on interconnect as the next structural bottleneck after compute" — and JPM flags the corollary explicitly as a RISK, not an opportunity, for the incumbents: "the need for innovation in the face of the constraints driving up networking costs (**a risk to watch for [[LITE]], IIVI and [[COHR]]**)."** ➔ **Worth holding against this page's Aug 7 street-high initiation and the Aug 11 LITE beat-and-raise: the same cost pressure that is inflating optical revenue today is what funds the search for architectures that route around the incumbents.**


## Recent signals (Aug 13) — 🔴🔴 **BOTH SUPPLIERS NOW REFUTE THE CPO PUSH-OUT ON THE RECORD, ONE OF THEM NAMING THE REPORT — and a second NPO customer gets a ~4-month deadline**

**The push-out thesis is now held by third-party research against the direct statements of both merchant suppliers.** In 48 hours:

- **[[LITE]] (BTG Pactual post-earnings call with a Lumentum executive, Capstone attended, 2026-08-13):** management **explicitly refutes "recent market speculation (e.g. from the SemiAnalysis report)"** of a CPO push-out and reports **INCREASED demand from [[NVDA]] for CPO in the scale-up timeframe starting 2H CY2027**. *"**Nvidia is all in on CPO**,"* which is expected to be **required for rack-to-rack connections in the "Rubin Ultra" timeframe (Phase 1 of CPO scale-up)**, becoming *"a **very robust ramp in CY2028**."*
- **[[COHR]] (FQ4 FY26 call, 2026-08-12; and Barclays' 2026-08-13 review):** CEO Jim Anderson — *"we've seen **absolutely no push-out of CPO demand. In fact, it's been the opposite** — demand INCREASE and demand getting PULLED IN."* Barclays confirms from the outside: **CPO adds revenue in the December quarter for scale-out, "no indication of a push-out"; scale-up CPO 2H CY27** (Barclays · Tom O'Malley, 2026-08-13).

**🔴 NPO — THE SECOND CUSTOMER GETS A DEADLINE, AND THE ARCHITECTURE CLAIM SHARPENS.** Lumentum expects to **announce another "meaningful" NPO customer within ~4 months**, beyond NVIDIA. Mechanics: **NVIDIA uses 400mW lasers for external light sources, absorbing roughly HALF of Lumentum's future Greensboro capacity**; the new engagements may run NPO **"inside the box" with mid-power lasers (120–150mW)** or as ELS. **NPO content ships Q4 CY2027, with a "very robust ramp in CY2028."** ⚠️ **The differentiator: these new NPO opportunities target "scale-up WITHIN the rack," implying HIGHER OPTICAL DENSITY than NVIDIA's initial hybrid approach (rack-to-rack optical, in-rack copper).** CEO Hurlston, separately at the JPM HW/Semis forum the same morning: **~6 meaningful NPO engagements**, and *"**the intensity of those engagements (# of optical lanes) is actually HIGHER than NVDA** — # of NPO optical lanes from north to south is considerably higher — more opportunity, more customers."* His framing of the substitution: *"**NPO gets you a lot of the performance cheaper (Tesla 3 versus Model X); but eventually everyone will have to go to CPO.** Also building NPO helps develop confidence and competence, so will lead to NPO players moving confidently to CPO over time"* (JPM · Joshua Meyers, raw notes, 2026-08-13). **[[COHR]] independently: NPO/CPO engagements up significantly over 3–6 months, content "very comparable" between the two, "final plans of customers still in flux"** (MS · Meta Marshall, 2026-08-13) — and **[[AAOI]]'s CFO/CSO Stefan Murray takes the other side on timing: *"there's a disconnect between the speed at which investors think things happen and what actually needs to happen to bring CPO to market at scale… End-2027 they expect to start and see some orders, but it's a 2028 story,"* while still seeing **CPO as the major player over NPO** and reporting **no change in NPO demand** (JPM forum, 2026-08-13).**

**🔴 THE CAPACITY LAYER — THE CONSTRAINT REMAINS INDIUM PHOSPHIDE, AND THE TOOL VENDOR JUST CONFIRMED IT FROM THE OTHER END.** [[COHR]]: InP output **on track to double by F1Q27-end and more than double again by CY27-end**, with **InP laser production +80% y/y in F4Q26 underpinning data-center revenue growth "to exceed +80% y/y" in F1Q27** (JPM · Cardoso, 2026-08-13) — a **one-quarter lead from laser output to datacom revenue**, the cleanest transmission rule this theme has. BofA adds **>75% of COHR capacity on 6-inch by CY27, "4x supply at half the cost"** (2026-08-13). ✅ **And from the equipment side: [[VECO]] disclosed a $200M advanced-packaging order plus an additional LUMINA+ MOCVD order, "incremental to the $250M+ InP order from last quarter," while investing to MORE THAN DOUBLE capacity in AP and SiPho — with capacity arriving closer to 2H CY27 (Barclays · O'Malley, 2026-08-13). The tool orders and the supplier ramps are consistent; the timing gap (tools land 2H CY27) is the thing to watch.**

**⚠️ THE NEW BEAR THREAD IS A SUBSTITUTION QUESTION, NOT A DEMAND QUESTION — and it comes from a bull.** Barclays on [[COHR]]: *"**800G volume in CY27 continues to be significant but UNCLEAR AT THIS POINT IF IT GROWS amidst the 1.6T ramp**"* (2026-08-13). ➜ **If 1.6T cannibalises rather than layers on top of 800G, unit growth decouples from revenue growth across the transceiver complex. Note this cuts the OTHER way on margin: LITE puts 1.6T transceiver GM at 30–35% vs 25% for 800G, so mix substitution is margin-accretive even where it is unit-neutral (BTG call, 2026-08-13).**

**⚠️ POLICY — THE FCC/CHINA BAN IS NOT A CLEAN POSITIVE FOR THE US SUPPLIERS, AND LUMENTUM SAID SO.** ~**65% of the world's transceivers come from Chinese-headquartered companies**; a ban would be *"very disruptive for the industry"* short-term, and the definition is messy (**manufacturing location vs company HQ**). Lumentum *"would like to have this business"* but it **"needs to be margin-sensible, as current transceiver margins might not justify taking on all volume"** — and the company is lobbying for *"a more gradual and thoughtful shift,"* partly to protect its **significant Chinese laser-chip customers** (BTG call, 2026-08-13). BofA reaches the same conclusion from the outside on [[COHR]]: *"considering a potential Chinese transceiver ban, COHR would be in the **best position to benefit** as it is **third in global share** and has a large US-based footprint"* (2026-08-13). **[[AAOI]]'s read on Washington's intent is the most specific: the government "wants to make sure that Chinese growth in the transceiver market doesn't become a threat," but is "also very focused on ensuring that US AI growth isn't crippled… They are not saying it needs to be 100% in the US; but they want a meaningful part of it to be here." The mechanism floated: "originally approved designs continue to be allowed to import; but as newer generations come out, you could seek to change the supply chain" (JPM forum, 2026-08-13).**

**Read-through to the ban's beneficiaries, with a caveat:** [[AAOI]] — *"customers are willing to pay a premium for US capacity"*; **400G a slight margin drag, 800G accretive, 1.6T better still; margins to mid-30% by mid-2027, and "1.6T and even more margin-accretive CPO could take them to 40%+ GMs over time"**; **all production now on 4-inch wafer, with 6-inch "not necessary/desirable for at least a year or two… ultimately an economic/cost decision"** — ⚠️ **a deliberate divergence from COHR's 6-inch strategy, and worth tracking as a cost-position gap if the InP shortage persists** (JPM forum, 2026-08-13).

**A ONE-LINE SUPPLY-CHAIN DEMAND READ-THROUGH FROM AN OFF-COVERAGE NAME — datacenter signal-integrity/connectivity content is compounding at a rate consistent with everything else on this page: **Semtech (SMTC)**'s datacenter revenue runs from roughly **$13MM in C1Q23 to $97MM in C1Q26** (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, with Natalia Winkler / Alex Kivali, "Semtech Corp — Increasing Estimates Following Cellular Module Sale", Buy, 12m PT $225, px $134.92, 2026-08-13, Figure 1) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ued50151.html).** ⚠️ **OFF-COVERAGE, NO WIKI PAGE — logged as a demand datapoint only. The note itself is about divesting the low-margin cellular module business and is OFF-THEME; nothing else from it is carried here. The intermediate quarters in the exhibit came through the text layer scrambled and are deliberately NOT quoted — endpoints only.**

## Recent signals (Aug 12) — 🔴 **NPO GETS A DATE AND A DEFINITION ("ADDITIVE TAM, LATE-2027 SCALE-UP RAMP"), THE SUPPLIER'S SHIPMENT CLOCK IS DECOUPLED FROM NVIDIA'S DEPLOYMENT CLOCK, AND [[COHR]] PRINTS WITH GROSS MARGIN GUIDED *UP***

**The morning-after sweep on [[LITE]]'s print (JPM, Jefferies, MS, Barclays, R&Co Redburn — all 2026-08-12) plus [[COHR]]'s own print that evening moved four of this theme's open questions.**

- **🔴 NPO IS NOW DEFINED AS ADDITIVE, NOT SUBSTITUTIONAL, AND IT HAS A RAMP DATE.** Management: **NPO is additive to the TAM, ramping in late 2027 for scale-up applications**, while **CPO production plans "remain on track, with an increased demand signal"** (JPM / Joseph Cardoso, 2026-08-12). Barclays: **scale-up CPO at the lead customer ("we think [[NVDA]]") on track for 2H CY27 shipments / CY28 deployments**, with other customers *and* NVDA looking at NPO **"which will be incremental to LITE's scale-up optical opportunities"** (Tom O'Malley, 2026-08-12).
- **🔴 THE MID-POWER-LASER QUESTION — this theme's sharpest open dispute — GETS A COMPANY ANSWER (not evidence, but on the record).** MS: management sees **"a wide variety of engagements on NPO, even from their lead CPO customer,"** spanning **mid-power to high-power lasers**, and cautions that **"one shouldn't naturally assume that competitive differentiation / margin opportunity is less with the mid-power lasers"** (Meta Marshall, 2026-08-12). ⚠️ **This directly answers the Jefferies challenge logged on 08-07 — *"these are shorter scale-up connections — you might not need a high-powered laser"* — which was the single largest threat to the laser-content thesis in this theme. The dispute is NOT settled (management assertion vs analyst engineering judgement), but the supplier has now taken a public position and can be held to it.**
- **🔴 THE SUPPLIER/CUSTOMER TIMING DECOUPLING — new, and it changes how CPO-slip headlines should be traded:** *"while NVDA's plans for scale up deployments may push to **1H 2028**, **LITE's timeline has not changed, with ultra-high power laser shipments still expected to begin in 2H27**"* (Jefferies / Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-12). ➜ **Because the capacity is under take-or-pay, the component maker ships to its own schedule even if the deployment slips. Every prior CPO-pushout headline on this page was implicitly modelled as a 1:1 revenue deferral for the suppliers. That assumption is wrong.**
- **A CONTENT UNIT, from the only house that published one: R&Co Redburn (Mike Harrison) — "we estimate 25m lasers per GW" for scale-up CPO** (2026-08-12). ⚠️ **File as a single-source estimate. It is the first per-GW laser-content figure in this theme and is worth cross-checking against the Aurelion/Global Semi Research channel numbers already here (NVDA guiding LITE+COHR to ~40MM CW units in January rising to ~100MM by April/May, back-solving to 200–300k CPO switches in 2027 and 600–800k in 2028) — the two framings are not obviously reconcilable and should not be blended.**
- **🔴 THE MARGIN QUESTION SPLITS IN TWO, and the split is the useful part:**
  - **Structural/long-run: [[LITE]] RAISED its out-year operating-margin target to 40–44% (from 38–42%)** (JPM, 2026-08-12) — the first move in that frame since the March analyst day.
  - **Near-term: Jefferies dates the offsetting drag — "the steep ramp of that lower-margin [Cloud Light / transceiver] revenue likely softens the potential for margin expansion over the NTM" before scale-up UHP unfolds in 2H27.**
  - **And [[COHR]] guided the OTHER way: FQ1 FY27 gross margin 41.5% vs Street 40.0%, i.e. UP, on a print that also delivered GM +215bps y/y and op margin +381bps y/y** (COHR release, 2026-08-12). ⚠️ **Two optical primes, same week, both guiding margin higher into a transceiver ramp. That is material evidence against the "transceiver mix inevitably dilutes" bear this theme has carried. The ~10pt GM gap between LITE (50.4%) and COHR (40.2%) is unchanged — but the DIRECTION of travel is now the same for both.**
- **✅ SUPPLY REMAINS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT, restated with numbers (Jefferies, 2026-08-12): EML demand still running 30%+ ahead of supply; pump lasers SOLD OUT against a planned 4x increase underwritten by largely take-or-pay; 200G EMLs above 25% of EML revenue on a path to majority volume by mid-2027; 1.6T Tx adoption intensifying from fiscal Q1.** MS adds the mix math: **200G at ~25% of units and "probably more like 40% of revenue," from 5%/10% two quarters ago.**
- **🔴 OCS — a competitive datapoint that surprised the analyst who reported it: "the view that they will win MAJORITY SHARE AT GOOGLE in C27 surprised us"** (Jefferies, 2026-08-12), alongside **LITE's first triple-digit OCS revenue quarter in F1Q** and **no upside to the $400M 2H26 figure**.
- **⚠️ THE CHINA/InP SUPPLY BEAR GETS A MANAGEMENT REBUTTAL (assertion, unverified): LITE has seen "no impact from newly announced Chinese InP capacity," citing differentiation in EMLs, high-/mid-power lasers and even CW lasers "where it is pricing UP on superior yields," and notes several Chinese suppliers "are not yet delivering into the market"** (JPM, 2026-08-12). Same evidentiary caveat as the 07-29 IR meeting — this is the company grading its own competition.
- **⚠️ NON-AI SOFTNESS, corroborated across two issuers in one week: [[COHR]]'s Industrial segment fell short of expectations, and [[LITE]] reported Industrial Tech flat q/q with continued softness in Industrial Laser.** ➜ **Treat as an industrial end-market signal, not company noise.**
- **Read-through to the buyers of this content: [[MRVL]] previews into a 2Q print where GS expects a CY26/27 guide raise "driven by higher hyperscaler CapEx, strong optical networking demand, sustained momentum at existing ASIC customers, and the ramp of a new custom silicon customer"** (GS / James Schneider, 2026-08-12).

## Recent signals (Aug 11, /run-inbox — PRIMARY PDF) — GOLDMAN TAKES THE **CHINESE INCUMBENTS'** SIDE OF THE IMPORT-BAN DEBATE, AND PUBLISHES THE FULL MODULE TAM: **$34bn (2025) → $51bn (2026E) → $73bn (2027E) → $69bn (2028E)** — note the 2028 DECLINE
- **The note is scoped exactly to the ban thread this page opened on 08-04: "the Trump administration is drafting a ban on US imports of Chinese data center components, with the FCC working on measures to bar imports of Chinese made optical modules" — and Goldman is explicit that it takes NO VIEW on the outcome while staying positive on the CHINESE leaders (Buy on FOCI, RoboTechnik, Landmark, Eoptolink, VPEC)** (Goldman Sachs · Allen Chang / Verena Jeng / Ting Song, "Optical Networking: Top 3 investor debates on impact of potential US import restrictions on Chinese optical transceiver modules", 2026-08-11). ⚠️ **Recorded as the counter-position to this page's Western-optics rally read: the same event that the desks traded as a [[COHR]]/[[LITE]]/[[AAOI]] positive is being argued, by the analyst closest to the Chinese supply chain, to leave the incumbents' share INTACT. No US name is discussed in the note — no company page was patched from it, deliberately.**
- 🔴 **THE TAM TABLE, which is the most granular module forecast on this page and the first with a 3.2T line:**

| US$m | 2025 | 2026E | 2027E | 2028E |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| **Total module TAM** | **34,211** | **50,883** | **72,593** | **69,135** |
| Below 400G | 16,827 | 16,886 | 16,166 | 13,196 |
| 400G | 4,209 | 1,745 | 969 | 287 |
| 800G | 9,318 | 13,530 | 14,715 | 10,761 |
| 1.6T | 3,857 | 18,722 | 27,403 | 23,264 |
| 3.2T | — | — | 13,340 | 21,625 |

⚠️ **THE 2028 DECLINE IS THE ITEM TO ARGUE WITH, because nothing else on this page has it: total TAM falls 5% in 2028E even as UNIT shipments rise (403mn → 452mn → 528mn → 556mn). The mechanism is ASP — Goldman assumes like-for-like price declines every year (blended ASP $85 → $113 → $137 → $124; 800G $440 → $396 → $327 → $244; 1.6T $1,342 → $904 → $734 → $435; 3.2T $768 in 2028) that eventually outrun mix. Whether 2027 is the revenue peak for this industry is now a live, dated question rather than a vibe.**
- **Mix and materials, which is the CPO-adjacent part: 1.6T goes 11% → 37% → 38% → 34% of TAM value and 3.2T enters at 18% (2027E) rising to 31% (2028E). SiPh overtakes EML on VALUE in 2027 (56% vs 44%, from 28% in 2025) while SiPh UNIT penetration is only 6% → 10% → 15% → 18% — i.e. SiPh is concentrated in the high-ASP tiers, which is the quantitative version of the CPO/SiPh migration thesis this page carries.** **AI-rack context in the same note (chart read): NVL72-class racks 19k (2025) → 50k → 92k → 148k units, a 118% CAGR 2025-28E, with AMD NVL72-class at 5k → 13k → 15k; general servers +18% CAGR; AI servers (8-GPU) +26% CAGR.**
- **Goldman's three debates, answered in the incumbents' favour: (1) WHY LEADERS KEEP WINNING — "such a fast evolving technology environment only INCREASES customers' reliance on existing leaders rather than reducing it," across speeds (1.6T/2.4T/3.2T), form factors (pluggable/LPO/NPO/CPO), materials (SiPh/EML/lithium niobate) and fiber types; the SKU count itself is the moat. (2) CAPACITY & COST — "SEVEN OUT OF THE GLOBAL TOP 10 optical module suppliers by revenue are based in China and EXPANDED market share in 2025 vs 2024," on self-designed production lines and proprietary equipment; and the 1.6T-and-above step-up "add[s] difficulties in design but ALSO IN MANUFACTURING, given that the SIZE of optical modules is NOT INCREASING while at the same time requiring MORE FIBERS / LASERS PER BOX… adding difficulties to coupling and thermal dissipation." (3) SITE DIVERSIFICATION — Thailand and SEA capacity is already producing high-end 1.6T, with "Eoptolink… expanding PHASE 2 capacity in Thailand in 2026E with PHASE 1 ALREADY OPERATING AT FULL UTILIZATION RATE."** ➤ **That last point is the sharpest practical objection to the ban thesis on this page: the relocation is not prospective, it is running at full utilisation — so an import ban keyed to Chinese ORIGIN rather than Chinese OWNERSHIP has a well-developed workaround already in production. Cross-filed [china-export](china-export.md).** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Optical_Networking__Top_3_investor_debates_on_impact_of_potential_US_import_restrictions_on_Chinese_optical_transceiver_.html)

## Recent signals (Aug 11) — 🔴 [[LITE]] PRINTS A BEAT-AND-RAISE AND THE STOCK GOES NOWHERE (THE BAR IS NOW THE WHISPER, NOT CONSENSUS); MANAGEMENT PUTS **IN-RACK** OPTICS ON THE BOARD FOR THE FIRST TIME; AND **SCALE-ACROSS / COHERENT** GETS ITS OWN ARCHITECTURE WRITE-UP

**🔴 (1) THE PRINT: [[LITE]] BEAT EVERY LINE AND GUIDED SEPTEMBER ~8% ABOVE THE STREET, AND TRADED FLAT-TO-DOWN.** Q4FY26 **revenue $1.006–1.010B** vs Street $988–990M · **GM 50.4%** vs 48.5–48.8% · **OM 36.6%** vs 35.2–35.5% · **EPS $3.23** vs $2.97. **F1Q27 guide $1.225–1.275B (mid $1.250B) vs Street $1.151–1.16B; OM 39.5–40.5%; EPS $4.05–4.35 (mid $4.20) vs $3.58–3.61** — reaching the target model *"more than a quarter ahead of schedule."* Stock **-0.4% to -0.7%** AH.
- **🔴 WHY IT MATTERS FOR THE WHOLE COMPLEX, and this is the transferable lesson into [[COHR]]'s 08-12 print: "the tepid response feels like an EXPECTATIONS PROBLEM: last week, a few folks said my Bars seemed aggressive, but BY THE END OF THE DAY TODAY FOLKS WERE SAYING THE COMPANY HAD TO DO $1.3b IN REVENUE GUIDE TO WORK… so this feels somewhat REMINISCENT OF THE TXN PRINT"** (JPM / Joshua Meyers, "LITE… First Blush", 2026-08-11). The EPS beat was **13c ahead of JPM's Buyside Bars** and **GM beat consensus by 610bps.** ➜ **In this group, clearing published research is no longer sufficient — the marginal buyer is trading off an intraday whisper. Both UBS and Vital Knowledge reached the same verdict independently ("presumably on elevated expectations"; "the numbers and guide are both great, but expectations were fairly elevated").**
- **Context on the week: LITE and COHR had fallen -9% / -14% on Monday 08-10 on the FCC/China-ban speculation, which Jefferies could not source — *"didn't come across any good reasons tbh (perhaps all the long optical short memory chorus on X over the weekend was a reverse indicator?)"* — while noting sell-side notes arguing the FCC ban is unlikely to go through, and that Chinese optical names Innolight/Eoptolink were UP +3%/+4% overnight** (Jefferies / Rankie Wong, 2026-08-11). **Also flagged: Innolight-H was the #1 bought HK-listed stock by Korean retail since its 30-Jul listing.**

**🔴 (2) NET-NEW FROM MANAGEMENT: OPTICS ARE STARTING TO PENETRATE **IN-RACK** CONNECTIVITY, AND THERE IS A FIRST **ELS MODULE** ORDER.** Hurlston, in the Q4FY26 release: *"key growth drivers such as **OCS solutions and our cloud module business, where we are advancing 1.6T adoption**, are beginning to layer in. **Increasing demand for ultra-high-power CPO lasers, an INITIAL ORDER FOR ELS MODULES, as well as our breadth of NPO ENGAGEMENTS are the first signs that OPTICS ARE STARTING TO PENETRATE IN-RACK CONNECTIVITY, SIGNIFICANTLY UPPING OUR OPTICAL TAM.**"* ➜ **This page has dated scale-up CPO variously to late-2027 (LITE IR), ~2030 (LITE's own SiPh R&D Director) and 2028/29 (buy-side). Management has now given the first *qualitative* evidence of in-rack penetration plus a first ELS order — but NO dated ramp and NO quantified TAM revision. The dating spread survives the print intact; the direction is confirmed.**
- **The pre-print buy-side view, for the record (Papa Sylla, expert/client call relayed 2026-08-11, hours before the print):** wanted **scale-out CPO with [[NVDA]]'s Spectrum switches visible in the September guide**; estimates **LITE's CPO run-rate at ~$100M in cal-3Q and $250M+ in cal-4Q** (his estimate, hard to map 1:1 given internal-vs-supplied mix); dates **scale-up CPO to late 2028/2029**; reiterates **LITE sold out through 2028** supporting **~15% price increases**; puts LITE share at **~35–40%**; and argues a China ban is *"tactical negotiation"* because **China supplies ~70% of the rare-earth feedstock and would retaliate on lasers.** ⚠️ **But: even absent a ban, de-risking could take hyperscaler allocation to LITE from 40% → 30% over two years, which he frames as a positive for [[COHR]] — bounded by the fact that COHR only wins if China does not retaliate, and that LITE is itself a large laser customer.** Growth frame he attributes to the street: FY26→27 **+100%**, FY27→28 **+80%**.

**🔴 (3) SCALE-ACROSS GETS A DEDICATED ARCHITECTURE WRITE-UP, AND IT IS A COHERENT-OPTICS STORY (DIGITIMES research, "AI infrastructure moves toward scale-across; demand for coherent optics supply chain heats up", 2026-08-11).**
- **The driver is physical, not architectural preference:** *"**Because a single data center faces limits in power supply, floor space, and cooling, hyperscalers are distributing GPU clusters across campus data centers 2-20 kilometers apart**, then linking them with high-speed optical networks so that dispersed computing resources can work together for AI model training and inference."*
- **The technology fork:** *"**As AI interconnect bandwidth rises to 800G and 1.6T, traditional intensity modulation/direct detection (IMDD) technology is increasingly insufficient for high-capacity, long-distance data transmission, making COHERENT OPTICS THE MAINSTREAM TECHNOLOGY FOR SCALE-ACROSS.**"*
- **🔴 The cost problem and the two named answers, which is the part worth carrying:** coherent optics were built for long-haul backbone and are expensive, so vendors are rolling out **(a) "COHERENT LITE" — lower module cost via simplified DSPs and relaxed optical-component specs, targeting 1.6T campus DCI; and (b) "MULTI-RAIL" — higher line-system density, sharing cabinets, power and cooling across multiple rails, cutting repeater-hut deployment cost.** *"Scale-across optical supply chain led by [[MRVL]], [[CIEN]], and others."* ⚠️ **COHR/LITE are not named in the available excerpt — treat this as TAM/architecture evidence and a supplier-league datapoint for MRVL/CIEN, not as a share claim for the US laser names.**
- ➜ **Why this matters to this page's central debate: scale-across is a THIRD demand vector alongside scale-out and scale-up, it is a coherent/DSP story rather than an InP-laser story, and it arrives on a 2–20km distance regime where copper was never a competitor. It also explains the [[APH]]/CommScope datapoint the same day — "demand is strongest in advanced optical interconnect for AI SCALE-OUT AND SCALE-ACROSS" (MS / Meta Marshall, 2026-08-11).**

**(4) THE FIBER LAYER: THREE COMPANIES, ONE SHORTAGE, THREE DIFFERENT OUTCOMES (MS / Meta Marshall · Antonio Jaramillo, 2026-08-11).** **Fujikura is taking "like-for-like price increases of ~25% in some instances" for shorter lead times, with its Sakura plant near full utilisation and external sourcing of fiber and preforms bridging supply (+7.6% on the guide raise); [[APH]]/CommScope "retains adequate supply… without constraining shipments" and had FY26 revenue raised to $4.6B from $4.1B with EPS accretion doubled to $0.30 from $0.15 on IT-datacom nearly doubling y/y; [[GLW]] cannot monetise the scarcity at all because its capacity is committed under hyperscaler LTAs — "GLW's limited pricing commentary looks more reflective of capacity committed under LTAs than weaker demand."** Capex intensity for the same buildout: **GLW's $2.0B FY26 plan implies 8.3% of sales in 1H rising to ~12.6% in 2H, vs APH "near or above the high end of its 3-4% of sales range."** ➜ **The generalisable point: in this shortage, price accrues to whoever is NOT under a long-term agreement. That is the mirror image of the LTA argument running on [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md), and worth holding side by side.**
- **New structural demand leg, bounded by the analyst: the [[NVDA]]-backed long-haul build — 16 US routes built or overbuilt, NVDA leasing fiber on 8 existing routes, new builds designed for ≥1,728 fibers per route, "~15mm fiber miles vs LUMN total of ~58mm miles today… only ~25% of the size, but we still see as a positive."**

## Recent signals (Aug 10, PM) — 🔴 NVIDIA'S CPO LEG GETS DATED TO **NVL576**, NOT NVL72; JAPAN'S OPTICAL SEASON CLOSES WITH TWO GUIDE HIKES IN UNDER TWO MONTHS; AND THE TWO US NAMES PRINT THIS WEEK INTO A ~50% RALLY

**🔴 (1) THE CPO TIMELINE FINALLY GETS ATTACHED TO A SPECIFIC RACK SKU (Morgan Stanley Asia · Charlie Chan · Daniel Yen · Daisy Dai, "AI Supply Chain: GPU Architecture Optimization Given Memory Shortage and Supernode Challenge", 2026-08-10).** *"Given ongoing **PCB and thermal challenges**, **first-generation Rubin Ultra server racks will likely continue to use the OBERON solution for NVL72** while **adopting CPO/NPO technologies to enable NVL576-scale deployments**."* MS remains *"optimistic about Nvidia's adoption of CPO as a **key differentiator in scale-up performance versus China's supernode architectures**"* and confirms **inter-rack CPO connectivity as Nvidia's preferred long-term direction**, with **Kyber blade delayed with no concrete timeline**.
- ⚠️ **Why this is the most decision-useful CPO datapoint of the week:** this page has carried "CPO is in mass production" (NVDA, Aug 3) alongside "the near-term architecture is NPO" (MS, Aug 3) as an unresolved pair. **The reconciliation is now explicit and it is a PACKAGING-TIER split, not a timing fudge — CPO/NPO rides NVL576, pluggables + Oberon carry NVL72 through the first Rubin Ultra generation.** For the component names that means **conventional pluggable capacity and yields are what get paid this cycle**, with CPO content dated to the NVL576 window. Cross-ref [../NVDA.md](../NVDA.md), [../LITE.md](../LITE.md), [../COHR.md](../COHR.md).

**🔴 (2) JAPAN'S OPTICAL EARNINGS SEASON ENDED WITH THE CLEANEST DEMAND SIGNAL OF THE MONTH — AND IT IS THE GUIDE-HIKE CADENCE, NOT THE BEAT (Morgan Stanley desk · Amir Amerian, 2026-08-10).**

MS (Meta Marshall) previews **both slightly cautious** with buyside expectations *"balanced"*, sees **more margin upside for COHR**, expects **less chance LITE formally pulls forward its Analyst Day targets**, and warns *"this set of results **will not solve the longer-term debates**: **execution on the CPO ramp and durability of margins**."* Managements expected to emphasise **NPO over CPO** — consistent with (1) above. Buy-side framing: *"**both of these stocks are up around 50% since the late-July lows**"* (Crux Capital / Gaetano, 2026-08-10). **UBS is the outlier with a Neutral on LITE (PT $960, David Vogt): "growth in the Sept and Dec quarter is likely to be 'PEAKISH' as comps become much tougher,"** and — against this page's substrate thesis — cites *"**increasing supply of InP**"* as one of the two things pressuring the multiple. Marshall's substantive point is a supply one: *"there **isn't enough supply from non-Chinese companies to meet global AI capex needs**,"* and **US CSPs have been qualifying more non-Chinese suppliers** in anticipation. **Both LITE and COHR have publicly advocated for tighter controls.** ⚠️ **The desk's own hedge is the right one to carry: "let's see if this is posturing into the Trump-Xi September meeting."** Named beneficiaries on the InP/laser layer if it lands: **Sumitomo Electric (InP substrate, EML, CW lasers) and JX Advanced Metals (InP substrate)** — with **AXTI and Sumitomo Electric the dominant global InP substrate suppliers**, and **JX's InP capacity expansion re-framed from a laser-margin risk into a non-China supply positive.** (Morgan Stanley desk / Amir Amerian, 2026-08-04, re-circulated 2026-08-10.)
