## Reference framework — the SemiAnalysis **CPO BOOK** (published 2026-01-01): the physics, the taxonomy, the TCO and the supply chain that sit UNDERNEATH everything above

⚠️⚠️ **STANDING BACKGROUND, NOT NEWS — READ THE DATE BEFORE READING THE NUMBERS.** This is a **reference book published 2026-01-01, ~7 months stale as of 2026-08-13**, first ingested to the wiki on **2026-06-22** (company pages [../AVGO.md](../AVGO.md), [../INTC.md](../INTC.md), [../MRVL.md](../MRVL.md), [../NVDA.md](../NVDA.md), [../TSM.md](../TSM.md)) and transcribed onto this theme page for the first time on 2026-08-13. It is the single best structural source this theme has, and it is filed here as the layer *beneath* the dated signals above — **every "Recent signals" section above is FRESHER and GOVERNS where the two conflict.** Use this section for the **mechanism, the arithmetic and the BOM** — not for the timeline. (SemiAnalysis · Dylan Patel, Daniel Nishball, Myron Xie and 3 others, "Co-Packaged Optics (CPO) Book: Scaling with Light for the Next Wave of Interconnect", 2026-01-01) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Co_Packaged_Optics_CPO_Scaling_with_Light_for_the_Next_Wave_of_Interconnect.html) — ⚠️ **ONE ARTICLE, TWO SLUGS on disk: the June ingest above and a second URL-derived copy, [`newslettersemianalysiscom_api_v1_post_pdf_postId178153689.html`](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/newslettersemianalysiscom_api_v1_post_pdf_postId178153689.html). Not a second source — do not double-count it.**

⚠️🔴 **AND THE HOUSEKEEPING RULE THAT MATTERS MOST ON THIS PAGE: THERE ARE NOW AT LEAST *THREE* DISTINCT SEMIANALYSIS POSITIONS IN PLAY ON CPO TIMING, AND THEY MUST NOT BE MERGED OR NETTED.** In date order: **(i) this Jan-01-2026 BOOK — the framework layer, whose timing read is that Rubin Ultra is too early and *"the Feynman generation appears to be the focal point for CPO injection into the Nvidia ecosystem"*; (ii) the 2026-06-09 institutional note — scale-out CPO revised DOWN through FY27 and scale-up pushed to FY29, *"CPO output is driven by a single ASIC that is very limited"* (the note behind the June selloff, already logged in `## State of play` and Sources); (iii) the 2026-07-06 read that NVL576 slips and the CPO NVSwitch does not land until Feynman; and (iv) the recent push-out report that [[LITE]] management named and refuted on 08-13 and that [[COHR]]'s Jim Anderson contradicted on 08-12 (*"absolutely no push-out of CPO demand"*). ➜ **This section is (i) ONLY. It is the OLDEST SemiAnalysis item on the page and it is NOT SemiAnalysis's current view. Where the book's framework predates and EXPLAINS a later call — the supply-chain-readiness and manufacturability argument that recurs in (ii) and (iii) — say so with both dates attached; never collapse them into "SemiAnalysis thinks."**

**🔴 THE CENTRAL CALL, AND IT IS A *SPLIT* CALL — this is the frame the whole page should be read against.** *"CPO will provide some benefit and bring more options to scale-out networking, but it will be **central to scale-up networking. CPO will be the main driver of bandwidth increases in scale-up networking for the latter part of this decade and beyond**."* Against that, pluggables remain the default for **scale-out** on *"their relative cost-effectiveness, familiarity in deployment, and standards-based interoperability"* (CPO Book, 2026-01-01). ➜ **Do not trade "CPO adoption" as one variable. Scale-out CPO is a marginal-TCO argument that the book itself says is not compelling yet; scale-up CPO is a physics argument with no alternative.**

**1) THE PHYSICS — WHY COPPER RUNS OUT, STATED AS A RANGE PROBLEM AND A SERDES PROBLEM.**
- **Copper scale-up today delivers *"tremendous bandwidth of 7.2 Tbit/s per GPU – soon to be 14.4 Tbit/s per GPU in the Rubin generation, yet copper-based links are limited in range to two meters at most, meaning the scale-up domain world size is limited to ONE OR TWO RACKS at most."*** (CPO Book, 2026-01-01) ⚠️ **The binding constraint is REACH, not bandwidth — which is why the copper bulls and the optics bulls can both be right on bandwidth and still disagree on world size.**
- **The Rubin doubling is a one-off trick, not a roadmap:** NVIDIA gets there *"through bi-directional SerDes"* — full-duplex 224 Gbit/s transmit **+** 224 Gbit/s receive sharing one channel — but *"doubling bandwidth on copper by developing ever-faster SerDes is a highly challenging vector of scaling that is **a slow grind**."* True unidirectional 448G on copper *"remains another challenging feat with an uncertain time to market"*; PAM4 to 448G would need **~244 Gbaud**, *"likely untenable due to excessive power consumption and insertion loss."* NVLink's own history makes the point: **NVLink 5.0 is >11x NVLink 1.0's bandwidth on only 32 → 36 lanes, i.e. a 10x SerDes lane-speed increase (20G → 200G); NVLink 6.0 stays on 200G SerDes and doubles LANE COUNT via bi-directional SerDes** (CPO Book, 2026-01-01).
- **The transceiver's structural handicap, and this is the number to remember: the front-panel cage sits *"typically 15-30cm from an XPU or switch ASIC,"* forcing an LR SerDes drive plus a DSP to recondition the signal. Move the optical engine onto the package and *"the DSP can be eliminated and lower power SerDes can be used… This can reduce energy required to transmit data by MORE THAN 50% when compared to DSP Transceivers - with many aspiring to reduce energy requirements per bit by as much as 80%."*** (CPO Book, 2026-01-01) **Supporting arithmetic: the DSP is ~50% of an 800G SR8 module's power and 20-30% of its BoM — *"some regard DSPs as public enemy number one of cost and power efficiency."*** An 18k GB300 two-layer InfiniBand cluster carries **18,432 800G DR4 + 27,648 1.6T DR8 transceivers**; at **6-7W per 800G DSP and 12-14W per 1.6T DSP** that is **~480kW of DSP power for the back-end network alone, ~1.8kW per rack**.
- **Bandwidth density is the second axis, and it is where wide I/O beats SerDes outright: UCIe-A offers ~10 Tbit/s/mm of shoreline (≈330 Tbit/s along a reticle-long edge, 660 Tbit/s bi-directional off both edges) against Blackwell's 23.6 Tbit/s of total off-package bandwidth ≈ ~0.4 Tbit/s/mm; UCIe-S on substrate ~1.8 Tbit/s/mm vs ~0.4 for 224G SerDes** (CPO Book, 2026-01-01). ⚠️ **But [[AVGO]] and [[NVDA]] stay on electrical SerDes anyway, deliberately — *"they believe they can still scale SerDes and that they need to design for copper"*, and hybrid co-packaged copper + optics *"will be here to stay, requiring them to optimize for both"* to avoid multiple tape-outs. ➜ The wide-I/O upside is real and is NOT in the incumbents' near-term roadmap; treat it as an option, not a base case.**

**2) THE TAXONOMY — DSP TRANSCEIVER vs LPO vs NPO vs OBO vs CPO vs CPC, with the definitional trap flagged.** (all CPO Book, 2026-01-01)
- **LPO** = kill the DSP, drive the transceiver's TX/RX elements straight off the switch SerDes. *"LPO has not yet taken off."*
- **NPO** = the OE *"doesn't sit directly on the ASIC's substrate, but is co-packaged onto another substrate,"* remains **socketable/detachable**, and the electrical signal still crosses copper from the XPU SerDes. ⚠️ **THE TRAP, and it matters for how the page reads NVDA's own product claims: NVIDIA's Quantum-X800-Q3450 uses six DETACHABLE optical sub-assemblies per ASIC, so *"purists may consider this to be technically 'NPO' not strictly 'CPO'"* — SemiAnalysis judges the extra loss immaterial. The CPO/NPO line on this page is partly a marketing line.**
- **OBO** (optical engine on the system PCB) is dismissed outright: *"the WORST OF BOTH WORLDS… it combines the complexity of CPO while inheriting some of the limitations of front-pluggable optics."*
- **CPC (co-packaged copper)** = twinax cables socketed on the package substrate itself, bypassing lossy CCL traces. *"CPC remains significantly simpler than CPO"* and *"could be the technology that enables 448G line speeds"*, attractive for **in-rack scale-up**. ➜ **CPC is the competitor to scale-up CPO that this page under-weights; [[MEDIATEK]]'s CPC-today/CPO-beyond-400G roadmap (07-31, above) is the same argument from the ASIC side.**
- **CPO** = OE on the host package; substrate first, interposer later.

**3) THE TCO AND POWER BUDGETS — the arithmetic that says scale-out CPO is a WEAK trade, and it is the most useful bear input on this page.** (all CPO Book / SemiAnalysis AI Networking Model, 2026-01-01)
- **Where the money and power sit:** back-end fabric = **85% of networking cost and 86% of networking power** for a 3-layer GB300 NVL72 InfiniBand cluster on X800-Q3400 switches. Networking = **15% of total cluster cost on a 3-layer network, 18% on 4-layer**; transceivers = **60% of networking cost and 45% of networking power** (3-layer, NVIDIA LinkX). A **200,000-GPU GB300 NVL72 3-layer cluster draws 435 MW of critical IT power, of which 17 MW is optical transceivers alone.**
- **Device-level power saving is large: an 800G DR4 pluggable burns ~16-17W vs ~4-5W per 800G for the OE+ELS in NVIDIA's Q3450 CPO switch = a 73% reduction.** Independently corroborated by Meta at ECOC 2025 on [[AVGO]]'s Bailly 51.2T: **15W for an 800G 2xFR4 pluggable vs 5.4W per 800G for OE+laser = 65%**.
- **🔴 BUT THE CLUSTER-LEVEL DILUTION IS BRUTAL, AND THIS IS THE PUNCHLINE:** DSP → LPO cuts transceiver power **36%** and network power **16%**; a full CPO transition cuts transceiver power **84%**, though OEs and ELSs *"now consume 23% more power in aggregate."* Net: **3-layer CPO = −23% networking power but only −2% TOTAL CLUSTER power**; flattening to 2 layers = **−48% networking power but only −4% cluster power** — *"the wrinkle is that networking is just **9% of total cluster power** to begin with."*
- **🔴 AND THE COST CASE INVERTS ON THE SWITCH VENDOR'S MARGIN.** Q3450 uses **72 OEs at 1.6 Tbit/s**, later likely **36 OEs at 3.2 Tbit/s at ~$1,000/unit incl. FAU = ~$36k of OE cost per system** vs **$72,000** to populate the non-CPO X800-Q3400's 72 OSFP cages with $1,000 1.6T DR8 twin-ports. *"However, this doesn't take into account the switch vendor's margin. If we were to apply a 60% gross margin, optical engine cost to the end buyer would end up at **$80k-$90k USD – which is HIGHER cost than the transceiver equivalent**."* Net on a 3-layer network: **switch cost +81%, transceiver spend −86%, total networking cost −31%, total cluster cost −3%**; flattening to 2 layers gets **transceiver −86%, networking −46%, cluster −7%**.
- **➜ THE CONCLUSION TO CARRY:** *"if CPO offers only up to **7% cost savings and up to 4% power savings** on one hand, but on the other hand raises concerns about difficult field servicing, anxiety… over reliability and blast radius, and the **loss of bargaining power** that one has with multiple transceiver vendors – why is it being adopted by GPU clouds? The simple answer is that **it is not being adopted widely just yet**."* Book estimate for the first wave: **10-15k CPO switch units in 2026**, with NVIDIA's scale-out launch *"serving as a practice run and pipe-cleaner for the real high-volume deployment."* ⚠️ **This is the cleanest quantified bear case on scale-out CPO the wiki holds — and note it is a MARGIN-STACK bear, not a technology bear. It is neutralised the moment the switch vendor gives margin back, and it says nothing about scale-up.**

**4) WHY SCALE-UP IS THE "KILLER APPLICATION," AND WHY THE TAM SITS THERE.** (all CPO Book, 2026-01-01)
- **The bandwidth asymmetry, quantified: 5th-gen NVLink on Blackwell = 900 GByte/s (7,200 Gbit/s) unidirectional per GPU, *"9x more bandwidth per GPU than the 100GByte/s (800Gbit/s) per GPU on the back-end scale out network"* (CX-8 NIC, GB300 NVL72).** Hence: *"the TAM of scale-up interconnect (and eventually, scale-up CPO) has already considerably **dwarfed** that of scale-out networking. **CPO TAM is likely to be dominated by scale-up** rather than scale-out."*
- **The world-size argument: GB200 took the all-to-all domain from 8 → 72 GPUs; Kyber for Rubin Ultra scales to 144 GPU packages (576 GPU dies), *"4x denser than the already dense GB200/300 NVL72 rack."*** Optics inverts the approach — *"scaling across multiple racks to increase world size, rather than packing more accelerators in a dense footprint."* Once CPO is deployed for scale-up, *"the scale-up domain will no longer be limited by the interconnect reach… customers would be able to grow scale-up domains to arbitrarily large sizes"* — bounded thereafter only by switch radix.
- ⚠️ **THE BOOK'S TIMING CALL, WHICH IS THE PART THIS PAGE HAS SINCE OVERTAKEN:** *"Rubin Ultra is targeting a launch in 2027 (we think that ends up being late 2027) and the supply chain won't be ready to ship tens of millions of these CPO endpoints… **This is why the Feynman generation appears to be the focal point for CPO injection into the Nvidia ecosystem**."* **OVERTAKEN BY THE AUG-2026 EVIDENCE ABOVE** — [[LITE]] reporting an INCREASED NVDA CPO demand signal for 2H CY2027 scale-up, and [[COHR]] reporting *"absolutely no push-out"* (both 2026-08-12/13). ⚠️⚠️ **BUT BE PRECISE ABOUT WHAT LUMENTUM NAMED: the report management refuted on 08-13 is the RECENT SemiAnalysis push-out note, NOT this January book. Same publisher, different item, seven months apart. The book's Feynman line is its own dated call and should be quoted as such.** **Retained, not netted: the book's objection is about SUPPLY-CHAIN READINESS AND ENDPOINT VOLUME (*"the supply chain won't be ready to ship tens of millions of these CPO endpoints"*), while the supplier rebuttals are about ORDER SIGNAL. They are not the same claim, and the tension is unresolved.**

**5) RELIABILITY — the best third-party evidence in the theme, and the book deflates it in the same breath.** Meta/[[AVGO]] at ECOC 2025 across **15 Bailly 51.2T CPO switches**: **no failures and no uncorrectable codewords up to 1,049k 400G port-device-hours** (one instance of FEC bin >10), extended in the talk to **15M 400G port-device-hours with zero UCWs through the first 4M**, and **MTBF of 2.6M device-hours for CPO vs 0.5-1M for 400G 2xFR4 pluggables (550k globally)**. ⚠️ **The deflation, and it is the honest part: 15M *400G port*-hours across 15 51.2T switches is only ~7,812 wall-clock hours ≈ 325 days — *"this 15M hour number is often cited as simply 'hours'… with the 'port' part left out"* — and *"the industry needs far more than just 15 CPO switches tested for 11 months in a lab setting before it… commits billions of dollars."* The 0.06% unserviceable failure rate carries a **blast radius of 64× 800G ports**, and the study is FR-optics-based while the next generation is DR.** (CPO Book, 2026-01-01) **The counterweight the book supplies for the bull side: at ~1M links a large cluster today sees *"dozens of link interruptions per day,"* and **80% of optical modules returned for link failure are "no trouble found"** — i.e. the pluggable baseline is worse than the failure statistics suggest, and CPO removes *"the 'human' element of optical link provisioning."***

**6) MODULATORS — MZM vs MRM vs EAM, the fork that decides whether this ends up an OPEN or a BOOK-ENDED market.** (all CPO Book, 2026-01-01) **Area, the cleanest single comparison: MZM ~12,000 µm² · GeSi EAM ~250 µm² (5×50µm) · MRM 25-225 µm² (5-15µm diameter).**
- **MZM** — easiest to implement, low thermal sensitivity, high linearity, **200G/lane proven and 400G/lane believed possible with non-coherent PAM**, and *"the most capable"* on baud rate with a clear path to 200 Gbaud. Costs: millimetre-scale footprint caps modulator/channel density, and high drive power (0-5V swing). Used by **Nubis** (acquired by [[CIEN]] Oct 2025) and by [[AVGO]]'s pre-COUPE generations.
- **MRM** — compact, built-in WDM mux/demux, low chirp, low drive voltage. ⚠️ **But *"10-100 times more temperature-sensitive than MZMs and EAMs"*: 70-90 pm/°C means a 2°C variation shifts resonance 0.14nm against a 0.1nm shift *"at which MRM performance collapses."*** Non-linear (complicates PAM4/6/8) and hard to standardise. **[[NVDA]] has *"a clear preference for MRMs"* and claims first to production; [[TSM]] COUPE is biased to MRM (heaters already in the PDK); Ayar Labs, Lightmatter and Ranovus follow.** **The GTC-2025 milestone the book flags: NVDA+TSMC shipping 200G MRMs in production *"matches the fastest MZMs today and disproves the industry notion that MRMs are limited to NRZ modulation."***
- **EAM (GeSi)** — the thermal outlier: *"EAMs can tolerate an instantaneous temperature shift of up to 35°C"* and ~80°C ambient, against MRM's collapse threshold above. Costs: insertion loss **4-5dB vs 3-5dB** for MRM/MZM, needs a separate mux for WDM, and the band edge sits in **C-band (1530-1565nm)** — shifting GeSi quantum wells to **O-band (1260-1360nm)** is *"a very difficult engineering problem,"* so **GeSi EAMs *"will likely form part of a BOOK-ENDED CPO system and cannot easily be used to participate in an open chiplet-based ecosystem."*** **Celestial AI is essentially alone here** — necessary for it, because its EAM sits *"within an interposer beneath a high-XPU power compute engine that dissipates hundreds of watts."* ⚠️ **And the cost of that choice: *"they will also have some work ahead of them integrating their EAM design into a foundry whereas other CPO companies can lean on TSMC COUPE where MRMs and related heaters are already part of the PDK."*** ➜ **For [[MRVL]] holders that is the single sharpest execution risk in the Celestial asset: a non-COUPE modulator on a C-band laser ecosystem, inside a roadmap where everyone else is converging on COUPE + MRM + O-band.**

**7) LASERS / ELS — the industry has converged, and the convergence is what makes the laser names the toll booth.** (all CPO Book, 2026-01-01) On-chip lasers lose on three counts (**highest-failure-rate component with whole-chip blast radius · heat sensitivity next to the hottest silicon · insufficient output power**), so *"the one approach where the industry has come to a consensus is to use an External Light Source (ELS)"* in a pluggable form factor. **The power tax: each laser must deliver 24.5 dBm to cover connector, coupling and modulator losses, and *"lasers and thermo-electric coolers account for ~70% of ELS power consumption."*** **Per-system content, NVDA X800-Q3450: 18 ELS modules × 8 CW DFB chips at ~350mW each.** **Named CW-laser suppliers: [[AVGO]] (US), Furukawa (JP), [[LITE]] (US), [[COHR]] (US), Yuanjie (CN), Shijia (CN)** — and the book's supply call: ***"We expect Lumentum to be the SOLE SUPPLIER for Nvidia's initial batches of CPO switch shipments, and Coherent may enter as the second supplier in late 2026,"*** with Chinese entrants credible later because CW is *"relatively standardized and commoditized"* while *"there is still somewhat of a moat around building the HIGH POWER laser sources required for CPO."* ⚠️ **Date-check this against the fresher page above: the 07-29 Aurelion/PhotonCap item already names THREE ELS vendors in NVIDIA's own technical blog (LITE, Sumitomo, COHR) and the 08-13 items have NVDA on 400mW lasers absorbing ~half of Greensboro. The book's 350mW / sole-source frame is the January baseline, not the current state.** NVDA's broader laser roster at VLSI: **[[LITE]] for single high-power DFBs, Ayar Labs for DFB arrays, Innolume for quantum-dot mode-locked combs, and Xscape / Enlightra / Iloomina for pumped nonlinear resonant combs**; VCSEL arrays explored for *"wide-and-slow"* but *"not an immediate priority."*

**8) THE BANDWIDTH-SCALING VECTORS — "fast and narrow" vs "slow and wide," and where each one jams.** (all CPO Book, 2026-01-01) Total fiber bandwidth = **fibers × speed per lane × wavelengths per fiber**.
- **Fiber count:** minimum pitch today **127µm = max 8 fibers/mm**; industry pushing **80µm pitch plus multicore fiber**. Manufacturing is the gate — *"aligning fibers, which still involves a lot of manual process, is prone to yield loss, and the FAU yield suffers with each successive fiber."* **Densest FAU shipping is Nubis's 2D 36-fiber unit**; edge coupling caps the array at one row, grating coupling allows multiple rows.
- **Speed per lane:** baud **100 Gbaud today → 200 Gbaud targeted** (MZM most capable); modulation **NRZ → PAM4 today, research to PAM6 (~2.6 bits/symbol) and PAM8**, with DP-16QAM at 8 bits/symbol.
- **WDM:** commercial DWDM is **8λ or 16λ**; **Ayar Labs' SuperNova = 16λ (laser supplied by Sivers), Scintil's wafer-scale InP laser up to 16λ, Xscape targeting a tuneable comb to 64λ.** ⚠️ **The constraint that makes WDM non-optional: *"most implementations of CPO proposed today are limited by the number of fibers that can be attached to the optical engines."***
- **OE bandwidth today: 1.6T-3.2T.** NVDA Quantum CPO = 1.6T engine, 3.2T for Spectrum; [[AVGO]] Bailly 6.4T and [[MRVL]] 6.4T both need **two FAUs**, so *"its bandwidth density may be similar to what Nvidia's offering."* 🔴 **The uncomfortable line for the CPO bull case: *"the 3.2T OE implementation in Nvidia's Spectrum-X photonics switches DOES NOT OFFER MORE SHORELINE BANDWIDTH DENSITY than pluggables that are driven by long reach SerDes."*** ➜ **Gen-1 CPO buys power and reach, not density. Density arrives with the interposer.**

**9) STANDARDS AND SERVICEABILITY — the two adoption blockers that are commercial, not physical.** (all CPO Book, 2026-01-01) Pluggables get interoperability from **OIF (electrical) + IEEE 802.3 PMDs (optical) + MSAs (mechanical)**; **CPO *"is in the 'wild west' phase, with some solutions and architectural decisions leading towards completely proprietary form factors"*, which the OIF's high-density/**CPX** effort is trying to address.** The customer's real objection is stated plainly: *"by adopting CPO, they potentially surrender their ability to control costs. It is much easier to squeeze a larger number of transceiver companies on costs than a smaller set of switch vendors."* On servicing: FAUs are detachable, but replacing one means going **inside the chassis** and re-routing through the fiber cassette without disturbing neighbouring fibers, *"far more onerous than replacing a broken pluggable transceiver which is simply a hot swap at the front of the face plate."*

**10) THE CPO-FOCUSED COMPANIES — and the Celestial AI number, quoted in full because the wiki carries a version of it.** (all CPO Book, 2026-01-01)
- **🔴 CELESTIAL AI / [[MRVL]] — the headline estimate, verbatim: *"major Hyperscalers are already starting to plan out their scale-up CPO strategy and are committing to suppliers. For instance, **Celestial AI is estimating that they could generate a $1B revenue run rate by the end of calendar year 2028** - we believe this will primarily be driven by a **CPO scale-up solution shipping with Amazon's Trainium 4**."*** The mechanics behind it: **Marvell's transaction summary puts the Celestial run rate at ~$500M exiting January 2028 (F1/28)**, and at the Barclays Global Technology Conference management said this *"run rate is expected to double to $1B by the end of Calendar Year 2028."* **An additional $2.25B earn-out to Celestial equity holders is contingent on cumulative revenue of at least $2.0bn by January 2029, with the first third payable at $500M cumulative** — so *"Celestial will need to add additional customers to the order book to achieve the $2B earn-out target."* **The [[AMZN]] tell: Marvell's 2025-12-02 8-K issued Amazon warrants at a $87.0029 strike through 2030-12-31, vesting *"based on Amazon's purchases of Photonic Fabric products,"* which SemiAnalysis reads together with a hyperscaler design win disclosed at Marvell's Industry Analyst Day as pointing at Trainium 4, ramping late 2027.** ⚠️ **Company-sourced ambition plus an inference, not a disclosure. Note the earn-out is the harder number: $1B exiting CY28 is only HALF the $2bn cumulative test.** First product: a **16 Tbit/s Photonics Link** into a chiplet, with a possible **Marvell 115.2T scale-up switch ASIC** on the other end; the differentiated pitch is that the scale-up ASIC *"can double as both a router and a memory endpoint,"* replacing XPU-attached HBM with a chiplet into a shared pool (**PFA: 2U, 16 Photonic Fabric ASICs, each 2.5D with two 36GB HBM3E stacks and eight external DDR5; a 16-radix switch for up to 16 XPUs, one fiber link per XPU**) — *"able to sidestep the silicon beachfront constraint that limits the number of HBM stacks one can attach to the XPU."* Cross-ref [hbm-memory](hbm-memory.md).
- **Ayar Labs** — TeraPHY OE chiplet, *"the world's first UCIe optical retimer chiplet."* Gen-2 "Eagle" = **4 Tbit/s unidirectional, eight 512 Gbit/s ports at 32 Gbit/s NRZ × 16λ, MRM, 24 fibers per chiplet**, SuperNova ELS from Sivers, MSA-compliant. **Gen-3 pivots off GlobalFoundries 45nm monolithic to [[TSM]] COUPE at >3x the bandwidth — 13.5 Tbit/s unidirectional per OE, 8 OEs ≈ ~108 Tbit/s of package scale-up bandwidth in an Alchip/GUC XPU reference design**, at ~200 Gbit/s per lambda on PAM4. **Backers: GlobalFoundries, Intel Capital, [[NVDA]], [[AMD]], [[TSM]], Lockheed Martin, [[AMAT]], Downing.** ⚠️ **The structural point the book makes with Ayar: monolithic SiPho *"stops at geometries below around 35nm,"* so it *"makes monolithic integration a dealbreaker for scaling"* — which is why everyone converges on heterogeneous 3D integration, i.e. on COUPE.**
- **Nubis (acquired by [[CIEN]], Oct 2025)** — the standards-first counter-position: **MZM for interoperability/reliability/maturity, IEEE/OIF-compliant electrical interfaces, Vesta 100 1.6T socketable NPX engine (16 lanes × 100G, 6×7mm)**, and a **2D 36-fiber array (16 Tx / 16 Rx / 4 laser) coupled through a laser-drilled glass FAU on the PIC surface**, avoiding WDM entirely — *"no one apart from Nubis is shipping this yet."* Next PIC: **16 × 200G/lane at 0.5 Tbps/mm unidirectional beachfront**, plus a **32 × 200G (6.4T) module snap-in compatible with Samtec's Si-Fly HD co-packaged copper connector** — *"a common copper and optical footprint… could create an open pluggable ecosystem for deploying CPO."*
- **Lightmatter** — **NPO optical engines first in 2026/2027** (soldered to the baseboard, LR SerDes over copper from the XPU; up to 3 FAUs × 40 strands = 120 fiber strands), on **COUPE + GF 45nm SPCLO**, then CPO *"in earnest in 2027 and 2028"* and the **M1000 4,000mm² optical interposer from 2029**. **Its GUIDE ELS is the differentiator: *"the industry's first Very Large Scale Photonics (VLSP) laser,"* hundreds of InP lasers on one silicon chip supporting up to 50 Tbit/s, with over-provisioning for "self repair" — Lightmatter claims TWO GUIDE units could replace the 18 ELSs in NVIDIA's Quantum-X CPO switch.** ⚠️ **If that claim holds at volume it is a direct attack on the discrete-CW-laser content thesis that underwrites [[LITE]] and [[COHR]] on this page. Unproven, sampling only — but it is the right bear to track.**
- **The NPO rationale, stated by a vendor rather than a broker:** hyperscalers' *"first step in adopting CPO will be to first gain operational experience with NPO, which derisks the product given the Hyperscaler need not 'commit' to CPO as it can elect to ultimately use either optical or copper scale-up solutions."* ➜ **Consistent with the Aug-2026 "NPO is additive, not substitutional" framing above, and it arrives from the opposite direction.**
- **🔴 [[AVGO]] — the strategic read, and the [[GOOG]] line is the one to argue with:** *"we believe the first mass-produced CPO system delivered by Broadcom will be in their customers' AI ASICs… We understand this was a key factor that led **OpenAI** to choose Broadcom. Interestingly, **Google, Broadcom's largest ASIC customer, is the hyperscaler that is MOST HESITANT to deploy CPO** in their datacenters. Google's infrastructure philosophy places more emphasis on **reliability over absolute performance**, and CPO's reliability is a deal breaker for them. **We do not expect Google to adopt CPO any time soon**."* Cross-ref [[OPENAI]], [custom-asic-tpu](custom-asic-tpu.md).
- **[[INTC]]'s roadmap** (Foundry Direct Connect): 2023 package-to-package electrical I/O → **2024 gen-1 CPO with direct fiber attach (OFC 2024: 4 Tbit/s bidirectional OCI chiplet on a concept Xeon, 64 lanes × 32 Gbit/s, ~5 pJ/bit)** → **2025 gen-2 with a detachable glass optical bridge** → **2027 target of 3D-integrated photonics via vertical expanded beam coupling.**

**11) SUPPLY CHAIN — the named-supplier map and the per-system content, which is the part with the most trading utility.** (all CPO Book, 2026-01-01; NVDA X800-Q3450 unless stated)
- **Optical engines:** 72 × 1.6T initially, later ~36 × 3.2T at **~$1,000/unit incl. FAU ⇒ ~$35-40k BOM per system**.
- **Fiber content: each 1.6T OE's FAU carries 20 fibers (8 Tx / 8 Rx / 4 laser) ⇒ 1,440 fibers per system, 1,152 of them Tx/Rx.** ➜ **The per-switch fiber number to hold against [[GLW]]'s content-per-GPU framing elsewhere on this page.**
- **FAU:** **TFC Optical (300394.SH), Senko (9069.JP), FOCI (3363.TW)** — TFC favoured for Q3450 (3-4 years of NVDA CPO co-design, cheap skilled labour in China, building advanced packaging in Suzhou), **Senko *"the very likely candidate for both the Spectrum X CPOs and Broadcom's Tomahawk 6 CPO systems"*** (SEAT detachable platform, working with GlobalFoundries on wafer-level-testable edge coupling), FOCI leaning to scale-up. Also Sumitomo and AFR (close to [[AVGO]]'s chain). ⚠️ **The labour bottleneck, quantified by [[GLW]]: FAU coupling-loss test *"cannot yet be fully automated"* — *"Corning estimated an average 10-15 minutes per FAU testing for the Spectrum X CPO systems."***
- **Fiber-attach / coupling tools:** **FiconTEC (Germany) leads at *"over $300k per unit"***; **All Ring Tech (TW)** has ~10% of its workforce on it and expects revenue traction from 2026; **GMT Global (TW)** targeting ~75% below Japanese machines that price at **$200-250k**.
- **Shuffle box:** >1,000 fibers emerge from the OEs. **T&S Communications (300570.SH) leads** with patented automated alignment and **[[GLW]] as its major customer** — *"Corning helps clients (Nvidia, Broadcom, etc.) design the fiber networks… and subcontract the shuffle box part to T&S."* Pricing ladder: **48-fiber ~$150 · 300-fiber ~$1,000 · 500-fiber ~$1,600 ⇒ >$3,000 for the Q3450**. **Molex ~20% more expensive on lower manufacturing efficiency.**
- **MPO connectors: 144 per Q3450** — US Conec (US), T&S (CN), Senko (JP), Broadex (CN), Optec (CN). **MT ferrules:** US Conec (three decades, expected a major Q3450 supplier), Fukushima, FOCI, TFC, T&S, Sumitomo — the last three vertically integrating into their own FAUs/shuffle boxes.
- **OSAT / assembly:** **ASE/SPIL (3711.TW), Amkor, Shunsin (6451.TW)** — *"ASE stands out as a key supplier in Nvidia's supply chain, including involvement in the future Rubin-rack CPO systems, while Shunsin maintains close ties with Broadcom."* Also **Fabrinet** (long the assembler of NVDA's in-house optical modules, now building OE packaging/test/system assembly, and a candidate for [[AVGO]]'s CPO box assembly alongside Micas and Foxconn), TFC and Foxconn.
- **🔴 E/O TEST — and this corroborates the 08-01 [[TER]] item above from an independent source:** *"other key equipment providers in the CPO supply chain include **Keysight, Ficontec, Teradyne, Advantest, FormFactor, Chroma, Anritsu, and Multilane**"*, with the industry *"still developing and has yet to fully converge on a standardized testing methodology for photonic engines."* On [[TER]] specifically: *"a large player in the field, with a strong historical presence in electrical testing, but the company has been **'very serious' about getting into photonic testing** … recently acquired a startup specializing in **packaged optical testing**."* **Ficontec's edge is wafer-level photonic test — *"the industry's first double-sided wafer testing machine,"* compatible with existing semi ATE architecture.** ⚠️ **[[ADVANTEST]] is named but not characterised; [[TER]] is the only ATE name the book gives a strategic verdict on. Cross-ref [semicap-wfe](semicap-wfe.md).**
- **Foundry:** [[TSM]] COUPE is *"the solution of choice for next generation CPO endpoints"*; **GlobalFoundries and Tower have strong SiPho *"but their lack of leading edge CMOS and advanced packaging constrain their ability to provide the higher bandwidth OEs of the future."*** Full COUPE detail is filed on [CoWoS / Advanced Packaging](cowos-packaging.md).

## Reference framework — the HOUSE TAM BRIDGE for CPO / NPO / OCS (built 2026-08-17, skeptic-audited; live artifact)

⚠️ **HOUSE FRAMEWORK, NOT A SOURCE.** Built 2026-08-17 by rebuilding the R&Co Redburn initiation ("AI Networking: Tripping the Light Fantastic", Mike Harrison, 2026-05-01 — primary ingested this date, see Sources) line-by-line and cross-checking it against everything above (SemiAnalysis CPO Book, GS module model, 650 Group, Aurelion channel, COHR/LITE disclosures). Interactive version with editable assumptions and live guardrails: [Optical TAM Bridge artifact](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/1a41d187-cd29-4695-989f-ae63329b864f). Adversarially double-checked 2026-08-17 (verdict **PARTIAL**: raw sourcing verified verbatim; the derived reconciliation gates below carry the corrections).

**THE ORGANIZING RULE — the three TAMs are priced on different units; blending them is the #1 error in the sell-side numbers on this page:**
- **CPO/NPO is bandwidth-priced:** `TAM = switching $pool × optical share × (1 − captive GM)`. Rides $/bit, which deflates (GS annual 1.6T module ASP **$840 → $435 '25→'28E, −48%** — ⚠️ the "$1,342" figure is a **quarterly pseudo-ASP** the GS section above warns against quoting as spot). A bandwidth-priced TAM can roll over on ASP even as units grow — that is the entire mechanism of GS's 2028 module-TAM decline.
- **OCS is port-priced:** `TAM = XPU units × ports-per-XPU × $/port × external share`. A circuit switch doesn't re-price with line rate (house physics reasoning, not sourced) — insulated from $/bit deflation, hostage instead to **one customer** (*"the customer base is thin. Google is the risk there"* — Aurelion on LITE mgmt, 2026-07-29) and **one ratio** (ports/XPU **1.5:1 today vs 2–10:1** claimed — Lumentum via Redburn Fig 49; Redburn's own attribution for why COHR says $4bn and LITE says $10bn).
- **NPO double-count rule — PRECISE FORM ONLY (the loose slogan was retired by the 2026-08-17 coverage audit): never add an NPO TAM to a TAM built from TOTAL scale-up penetration** (Redburn-style — NPO is already inside the penetration variable). **But where the base forecast was CPO-only, NPO IS an addend: LightCounting ADDED NPO to its April CPO forecast for an upward revision of >50%, to CPO+NPO $18bn+ by 2030** (JPM · Joseph Cardoso, "Optical: Thoughts into C2Q26 EPS Prints & Updated Market TAM Forecast", 2026-08-06 — e-mail primary surfaced by the audit, not yet ingested at audit time; INGESTED 2026-08-17 PM, see Sources). ⚠️🔴 **DOUBLE-RELAY WARNING (added 2026-08-17 night): FUNDA · "Deep|Optics: The Scale-Across TAM Is Taking Shape" (2026-08-12) restates this SAME LightCounting release number-for-number — datacom optics >$70bn by 2030 at ~28% CAGR from ~$20bn 2025, 1.6T ~$40bn of 2030 datacom raised by about a third, 3.2T ~$14bn, telecom & DCI $4bn→$9bn. ONE RELEASE, TWO RELAYS: do NOT count FUNDA as a second source, and do NOT treat it as external corroboration of this revision. Same rule already codified on this page as "ONE SOURCE, TWO LINKS" for the SemiAnalysis CPO Book. Two further FUNDA figures are also re-relays of items already here — ANET's $15bn/$20bn-incl-optics 2030 scale-across TAM (already logged from MS · Derrick Yang, 2026-08-05) and Nokia's EUR 2.8bn order intake (already logged from JPM · Scott Silver, 2026-07-26).** The votes: [[COHR]] folds — Q3FY26 call (2026-05-06) said **CPO-only** ">$15bn… probably a conservative estimate over the coming years", Q4FY26 (2026-08-12) says **"CPO, NPO, and other forms of integrated optics… >$15bn"** — same number, WIDER pool (⚠️ audit correction: the "before" is the Q3 call, not OFC; the lone "conservative" on the Q4 call was about the $4bn OCS TAM; and no 2030 date was ever attached to the $15bn). [[LITE]]: NPO *"additive to the TAM"* (JPM, 2026-08-12). LightCounting: added. Model NPO as share-of-pool × content parity (~1.0, COHR: *"very comparable"*) × merchant-capture premium (NPO runs **outside TSMC's COUPE gate** — AMZN/NVDA/AVGO per 650 Group, 2026-08-14). **LightCounting shape marks to hold against Routes A/C: only ~40% of the $18bn+ is scale-up (NPO just 33% scale-up, on latency), and META is expected to LEAD NPO/CPO deployments by 2030 (then GOOG/MSFT/AMZN/ORCL) — both cut against the NVDA-centric, scale-up-dominant construction of the Redburn build and the house GPU route.** **→ 2026-08-17 PM transcript check: LITE's "additive" is now PRIMARY and stronger than the relay — Q4FY26 call (2026-08-11, transcript on disk): *"The NPO opportunity is completely additive for us, significantly increasing the optical TAM"*, plus *"even our largest CPO customer is looking at NPO for specific new use cases"*.**

**CPO — the two bottom-up routes and the open fork (the load-bearing item):**
- **Route A (Redburn Fig 41, merchant/COGS basis):** NVDA networking rev (VA cons $54.6bn CY26 → $113bn CY30) × scale-up share ~66% (built from disclosed Spectrum-X $8→$10bn annualized + NVL72 $1bn/1Q26) × scale-up CPO penetration **20→80% CY27-30 (the estimate everything hinges on)** × **(1−80% GM)** + scale-out + non-NVDA (half of a 30% XPU share) = **$2.3bn '27 → $5.7bn '28 → $14.8bn '30**. Redburn's Figs 85/108 use this TAM as the merchant CPO BoM (33% laser / 67% non-laser, COHR at 25% of non-laser) — same ruler as COHR's ">$15bn"; the numeric match Redburn itself calls *"coincidental"*, twice. ⚠️ Two source defects: the 2026 total ($114m) doesn't foot from its own rows (~$103m), and the penetration base includes **all NVLink revenue incl. copper NVL72 backplane value CPO does not address** (base too big, content too small).
- **Route B (channel × content):** the Aurelion/Global Semi switch counts (200-300k '27 / 600-800k '28) are **pure back-solves of the NVDA CW-laser guide ÷ 144** (40m÷144≈278k; 100m÷144≈694k) — zero independent information, and **144 CW/system is a scale-out Quantum-X figure; scale-up carries ~22× the laser density per GPU (~111 vs ~5, Redburn 2026-08-12)**. At SemiAnalysis's ~$36k of OE content per system (a **floor** — excludes the 18 ELS modules; ~$54k grossed at Redburn's own 33/67 split) → **$22-43bn by 2028**.
- 🔴 **THE FORK, localized:** Redburn's component split implies **$19 per CW chip** ($1.9bn laser line ÷ ~100m chips) and **$4.8-6.4k of non-laser optics per switch**, vs **~$82/chip and $36k** from the CPO Book BOM — **4-7× apart, a genuine content disagreement, not a basis artifact**, with Redburn the LOW outlier while LITE is pricing lasers UP on yields. Close it with evidence (NVL576 volumes, first disclosed OE/ELS pricing), not averaging. Timing overlay unresolved: suppliers 2H27/"very robust CY28" on the record vs MS Feynman-2029 vs 650 Group ~2031 vs SemiAnalysis FY29 — and take-or-pay decouples supplier revenue from deployment TAM in any slip window (Jefferies, 2026-08-12). **→ 2026-08-17 PM: the first on-record OE/ELS pricing signal exists — LITE Q4FY26 call (2026-08-11, transcript on disk): first ELS module PO, delivery 2H CY27, ASP *"meaningfully higher than the set of lasers we ship in"*, margin above corporate average but below lasers. Direction only, no $/module — the 4-7× content fork stays open.**

**ROUTE C (added 2026-08-17 PM, skeptic-audited same day) — bottom-up from GPUs DEPLOYED, the leg that collapses the fork into one variable (the attach curve). Lives in `Optical_TAM_Bridge_v2.xlsx` (P:\Felipe Monteiro\Semis MAP) sheet "GPU route" + the artifact:**
- **Universe = NVL-class RACK-SCALE GPUs**, not total units: GS chart-read racks ×72 → 3.6m/6.6m/10.7m '26-28 (⚠️ the page's own "118% CAGR" label doesn't tie to 19k→148k = 98% — transcription quirk; AMD listed as a separate series). ⚠️ **Two basis traps caught by the skeptic: (1) JPM's 8.9m/9.9m are TOTAL NVDA units — rack-scale ≈67% of total on the '27 overlap, mixing them understates 2028 by ~45%; (2) canonical conflict — Nomura 54.5-62k NVL72 racks 2027F (assumptions.md) is 1.5-1.7× BELOW GS 92k on the identical metric; do not blend (haircut lever in the workbook, implied-attach readouts move 1:1 with it).**
- **Content = 111 lasers per attached GPU × $19/CW chip ÷ 33% laser share ≈ $6.4k optics per attached GPU.** 111 = Hurlston's pre-print "8,000 lasers per rack" ÷ 72 (single source; the only assumption is which rack he meant — the 25m/GW PUE/250kW ladder is DOWNSTREAM and does not condition it; source expects it UP at Kyber, so flat out-years are a conservative hold). $19 = back-solved from Redburn's own Fig-85/108 UHP laser line ÷ the ~100m channel chip guide — **a same-source pair: count × price is an IDENTITY that re-slices one $/GPU (UHP-basis $19/0.33 = ELS-basis $38/0.66), NOT independent corroboration.** ⚠️ **FORBIDDEN MIX: 111 × the Book-derived $82/chip ⇒ $27.6k optics/GPU ⇒ ~$61bn of 2028 optics at just 20% attach ≈ 69% of the BofA CY30 $88bn optical canon two years early, and ~$20bn of lasers vs JPM's >$9bn — fails TAM-level sanity (per-GPU it is ~35% of Fubon's $78-80k Rubin ASP — high, but the TAM level is the kill). Audit caveats on the $19: a 2028 laser line divided by a 2026-dated build guide, and the 40m/100m are two snapshots of ONE rolling guide that the channel back-solve books as separate 2027/2028 volumes.**
- **The readout that matters: inverting Route A through this content ⇒ Redburn's curve implies ~6.9% unit attach in 2028 (GS units basis; ~11% on Nomura).** Corpus timing evidence — MS "Feynman more likely scaling 2029", 650 Group crossover ~2031, SemiAnalysis scale-up FY29 — **sits on the single-digit side: the readout mildly VALIDATES Redburn's level**; the bull side is the suppliers' on-the-record "very robust CY28". At neutral defaults (10% attach '28) Route C prints **~$7.9bn 2028** vs Route B's ~$30bn — the channel's 700k switches embed **~0.65 switches per attached GPU**, far more hardware than 10% attach needs: either the switch counts include non-CPO trays or real attach is higher. **That is the residual fork to close (NVL576 volumes, first OE/ELS pricing), not to average.**
- **🆕 2026-08-17 — THE FIRST EXTERNAL VOTE ON THE ATTACH VARIABLE, AND IT IS FAR ABOVE THIS BUILD. GF Securities (HK) forecasts NPO optical engines at 11mn 2027E / 40mn 2028E** (see the Aug-17 signal at the head of this page). Decoded at 3.2T per OE and 14.4 Tbit/s scale-up per Rubin GPU ⇒ **~39% NVDA attach in 2028E vs this route's 10% neutral default and Redburn's implied ~6.9% — roughly 4×** — plus an AWS leg (12mn OEs 2028E) and an other-CSP leg (9mn) that this NVDA-centric route does not carry at all. ⚠️ **NOT adopted into the build: priced at either content pole it implies $55bn of NPO optics in 2028, ~7× this route's own 2028 and 62% of the BofA CY30 all-optical canon. Logged as the bull pole on the attach slider and as a red reconciliation flag. It does, however, confirm the audit's own warning that the NVDA-only universe understates the pool — the AWS/other-CSP legs are a real gap in Routes A and C, independent of whether GF's level is right.**
- **Realized-revenue rule (skeptic-corrected): near years, contracted take-or-pay volume is a FLOOR for supplier revenue, not a ceiling** — *"the component maker ships to its own schedule even if the deployment slips"* (Jefferies, 2026-08-12) — and CPO delays leak revenue back into pluggables/EML (the cushion). **Do not model realized = min(demand, supply).** Out years are attach-gated. **LITE-side floor is now primary too: pump-laser LTAs ~3yr, *"in most instances… take or pay"*, with built-in pricing levers (Q4FY26 call, 2026-08-11, transcript on disk).**

**OCS — the conta and what the skeptic pass changed:**
- **Route A (Redburn Figs 46-48):** Google capex (VA $179bn '26) × 85% AI × 50% servers × 75% TPU × **8% OCS share** (calibrated off an NVDA benchmark; reproduces only if the −30% capex saving applies to the scale-out leg alone: 16%×57%×65% + 16%×21%×85%×0.7 ≈ 7.9%) × externalization 25%('27)→60% + others at 0→50% of Google = **$2.0bn '27 → $6.2bn '30**.
- ⚠️ **The "$1.27bn Google-derived 2027 ≈ LITE >$1bn run-rate" match is CIRCULAR — Redburn states it calibrated Fig 48 to the LITE/COHR disclosures** (*"we reflect these datapoints in Fig 48"*). The non-circular residual **skews the TAM UP**: LITE's internal OCS capacity *"tops out at c$1.25bn/yr"*, it is adding CM capacity, and it expects the majority of Google's incremental volumes — holding that at ~60% share needs **Google externalization ~40%+ in 2027 vs Redburn's 25%**; Redburn's 2027 pool looks too small to hold LITE + Google in-house + COHR's ten LC-OCS customers. **→ 2026-08-17 PM transcript check: the >$1bn-'27 run-rate and c$1.25bn/yr capacity marks are still relay-only on disk (NOT said on the Q4FY26 call) — the call's primary OCS record: tracking to the $400m 2H-CY26 target (*"would not say tracking ahead"*), first triple-digit OCS quarter in the FQ1'27 guide (*"meaningfully above the three-digit mark"*), CM capacity adds started, crossover to #1 merchant supplier *"sometime in early 27"* absorbing *"the vast majority"* of the internal-source customer's incremental volumes (customer unnamed on the call; Google per Aurelion above), in-tray OCS additive (2028).**
- 🔴 **🆕 2026-08-17 night — THE FILED OCS BASE IS NOW A FULL YEAR, AND IT MOVES WHERE THE SKEPTICISM BELONGS. [[LITE]]'s FY26 Form 10-K (filed 2026-08-17) discloses OCS revenue of *"more than $90.0 million… during fiscal year 2026"* (the "initial phase" of shipments), with expansion *"on track… over the coming quarters."* ⚠️ **SUPERSEDES** the page's standing claim that the 10-Q of 2026-05-06 (">$25.0m" Mar-26 quarter ≈ $100m annualized) is *the only filed OCS revenue figure* — old framing in `## Changelog`. The two filings foot: FY26 averages $22.5m/qtr, so a >$25.0m Mar-26 quarter ran ~11% above the FY average and the ">$25m × 4" annualization overstated the realized year by ~11%; on a ramping profile 1H FY26 was ≤~$40m combined. **RE-DERIVED MULTIPLES (house arithmetic): the PRIMARY marks — $400m in 1H FY27 (=2H CY26) and a first triple-digit OCS quarter in the FQ1'27 guide — are already 4.4× the entire prior fiscal year in two quarters (≈$800m annualized = 8.9×), with ONE guided quarter exceeding all of FY26. Against that guided run-rate the three BRACKETED RELAY claims are small steps, not moonshots: ">$1bn 2027 run-rate" = ~11.1× FY26 but only ~1.25× the annualized 1H-FY27 target; "$1.25bn/yr capacity" = ~13.9× FY26 but only ~1.56×; "~$10bn OCS TAM" = ~111× filed FY26 revenue and remains the genuinely unbridged one (vs Redburn's $2.0bn '27 / $6.2bn '30 pool and COHR's >$4bn). ➡ **The load-bearing number is the $400m, not the >$1bn that follows it — the audit's "~10-12× ramp" framing (now ~13.9× on the filed base) is arithmetically right and analytically misleading, because the 8.9× has already been guided.** Port anchor, derived: at the ~$520/port this section back-solves from Route A, >$90.0m ⇒ **~173,000 OCS ports in FY26** — order-of-magnitude only, to be tested against a disclosed port count.**
- 🔴 **🆕 2026-08-17 night — A COUPLING THE THREE-DIFFERENT-UNITS RULE DOES NOT CAPTURE, AND IT IS NOT AN ADDEND: *"Each OCS node adds roughly 3 dB of insertion loss, so even links under 2 kilometers need coherent-lite"* (FUNDA, "Deep|Optics: The Scale-Across TAM Is Taking Shape", 2026-08-12; its June note argued this OCS-paired case drives coherent-lite's LARGEST volumes).** The organizing rule above forbids blending the bandwidth-priced and port-priced TAMs — correctly. But this is the first sourced claim that they are **physically coupled**: inserting an OCS node imposes an optical-budget penalty that forces a COHERENT transceiver onto the same link at reaches (<2km) where nobody would otherwise deploy one. **Two consequences pointing opposite ways: (a) every incremental OCS port carries ATTACHED coherent-lite content, an externality neither OCS route captures — a genuine understatement of the scale-across leg; (b) it makes the OCS port price sensitive to the bundle rather than to the switch, weakening the "a circuit switch doesn't re-price with line rate" insulation. ⚠️ DO NOT sum the two TAMs to capture this. The correct treatment is an OCS-ports→coherent-modules ATTACH RATIO — a new open variable, not a new addend.**
- **Port route calibration:** at ~6.5m TPU units 2027 ([[AVGO]] order book, assumptions.md) × 1.5 ports/XPU × 25% external, Route A implies **~$520/port**. The 2030 spread (COHR >$4bn / Redburn $6.2bn / LITE ~$10bn) is ~linear in the ports/XPU slider.

**Traps codified out of this exercise (all caught or confirmed by the 2026-08-17 skeptic pass):** quarterly pseudo-ASPs quoted as spot ($1,342); "annualized"/year labels invented on channel numbers (the 40m→100m CW guide is Jan→Apr/May **2026**, basis unlabeled); 144 lasers/switch applied outside scale-out; 650 Group's 20-40m DCI ports has an **unlabeled basis** (650's convention is annual shipments — unusable for annual TAM either way until confirmed); and the full-stack check — this framework's 2030 optical stack (~$30bn transceivers + $14.8bn CPO + $6.2bn OCS ≈ **$51bn**) is only **~58% of the BofA $88bn CY30 optical canon** (assumptions.md) — the GS-rollover-vs-BofA-compounding shape conflict, logged there, **log both / blend neither**.

## Key debates
- 🆕🔴🔴 **THE *NATURE ELECTRONICS* CPO REVIEW ALREADY AT THE HEAD OF THIS PAGE FINALLY GETS ITS TECHNICAL CONTENT — VIA A THIRD-PARTY READ-THROUGH, BECAUSE THE ARCHIVED PRIMARY HAS A CONFIRMED ZERO-CHARACTER TEXT LAYER.** ⚠️ **NOT A DUPLICATE, AND THE DISTINCTION MATTERS: the underlying FACT — an [[SKHYNIX]] researcher as corresponding author on a co-packaged-optics roadmap review published 2026-08-20 — was already ingested on 2026-08-20 from the SK hynix Newsroom COMPANY PRIMARY, in the block at the top of this page that explicitly records that *nothing beyond the headline facts was extractable* from the archived copy. What is added below is one independent author's READING OF THE PAPER'S CONTENTS, which fills exactly that recorded gap.** (**DAMNANG — independent Substack author, NOT a broker and NOT a research house — "HBM Density Peak: Why AI Value Is Moving From Memory to Connectivity", 2026-08-20.** ⚠️ **No rating, no PT, no TAM anywhere in the note. Everything below is his reading of the paper's text and figures, NOT quotations from the paper itself, and no dollar or unit forecast attaches to any of it.**)
  - 🔴 **THE PHYSICS THE PAPER PUTS BEHIND THE WHOLE OPTICS-INTO-THE-PACKAGE ARGUMENT, AND IT IS THE DIVERGENCE NUMBER THIS PAGE HAS NEVER HAD FROM A PEER-REVIEWED SOURCE: compute throughput has TRIPLED ROUGHLY EVERY TWO YEARS while, over the same two-year cycle, INTERCONNECT BANDWIDTH ADVANCED ONLY ~1.4-FOLD.** The mechanism named: even when HBM lifts bandwidth *inside* the package, resistive loss and capacitive loading on copper traces reappear once data crosses the board and cables, and at high rates equalization, retimers, DSP and FEC correct for that **while adding power and latency**. ➜ **A ~3x-per-2yr compute vs ~1.4x-per-2yr interconnect divergence is the cleanest single justification on this page for why the optical conversion point has to move toward compute — and it is now attributable to a technical review with a memory maker involved rather than to vendor marketing. ✅ It is also the physical statement of the same point Marvell makes commercially through pJ/bit; note the standing RULE on [cxl-memory-fabric.md](cxl-memory-fabric.md) that pJ/bit figures are never quoted without their scope and never chained.**
  - **THE INTEGRATION LADDER, WHICH MAPS ONTO THIS PAGE'S OWN NPO→CPO TAXONOMY RATHER THAN COMPETING WITH IT: pluggable → on-board optics → 2.5D CPO → 3D TSV → hybrid bonding → monolithic integration, with the optical conversion point moving closer to compute at each step. 2.5D is named as the compromise between manufacturability and performance; 3D delivers higher bandwidth density and lower energy per bit at the cost of thermal and bonding yield.** ➜ **On that reading, the NPO reported for NVL576 is *"an INTERMEDIATE STEP that lowers package redesign and serviceability burden, rather than the end of the optical transition,"* with CPO gaining economic footing as bandwidth density and I/O power become the binding constraints. ✅ THIS IS THE PEER-REVIEWED VERSION OF THE ORDERING THIS PAGE ALREADY ADOPTED: "NPO IS ADDITIVE, NOT A BRIDGE THAT GETS TORN DOWN" (08-17), Fabrinet's NPO-before-CPO sequencing (08-17), and the 650 Group's "NPO as the pragmatic bridge" (08-14). Three commercial sources and now one technical review, same sequence.**
  - ⚠️ **AND A PROVENANCE CORRECTION THIS PAGE SHOULD CARRY, BECAUSE IT DATES NVIDIA'S CPO LEG TO NVL576: the author separates public disclosure from industry reporting. NVIDIA has disclosed that NVL576 binds EIGHT RACKS OF 72 GPUs into a single 576-GPU NVLink DOMAIN with DIRECT OPTICAL CONNECTIONS BETWEEN RACKS — but *"NVIDIA's public technical material does not state whether the packaging topology of the optical link is NPO or CPO."*** **The NPO attribution is SemiAnalysis's read, not an NVIDIA statement.** ➜ **The 08-10 PM dating of NVIDIA's CPO leg to NVL576 should carry that NPO-vs-CPO ambiguity with it rather than reading as a disclosed topology.**
  - **THE LONG-DATED ARCHITECTURE, WITH THE AUTHOR'S OWN BOUND ON IT — AND THE BOUND IS THE POINT.** The paper's **Fig. 4b** shows an **XPU POOL and a MEMORY POOL separated and linked by a PHOTONIC INTERPOSER and optical fibre**; **Fig. 4c** a multi-chip-module cross-section with **ASIC, CPU, H[BM] and HBM chiplets placed together on a photonic interposer with EMBEDDED OPTICAL WAVEGUIDES** (one word is truncated mid-line in the extracted text and has not been reconstructed); **Fig. 4d** sets out **vertical connection between stacked PIC and EIC through OPTICAL TSVs**; and **Fig. 3** gives representative energy and bandwidth-density values per integration method (**the pluggable column on a transceiver basis**). ⚠️⚠️ **The paper does NOT treat this as a finished architecture: it names an OPTICAL MEMORY INTERFACE THAT PRESERVES COHERENCE WHILE LOWERING LATENCY, LOW-POWER PHOTONIC SWITCHING and MULTI-HOP SIGNAL REGENERATION as problems STILL TO BE SOLVED, and the author is explicit that the drawing is *"a research direction for the optical architecture that separat[ing] memory and compute would require, not an SK hynix commercial product roadm[ap]."*** ✅ **THIS ALSO ADVANCES — WITHOUT CLOSING — THE UNVERIFIED CORPUS LEAD FLAGGED AT THE HEAD OF THIS PAGE ON 08-20: the "photonic interposer that links to memory" claim (@aleabitoreddit) IS in the paper, AS A RESEARCH DIRECTION IN A FIGURE. Verified as a paper figure; still NOT verified as any vendor's product roadmap. That is a meaningful upgrade from "unverified" and a long way short of a product.**
  - 🔴 **THE STRUCTURAL OVERLAP WITH A SHIPPING VENDOR, WHICH IS THE INVESTABLE PART — PLUS ONE SPEC THIS PAGE DID NOT CARRY.** Marvell's **Photonic Fabric Module**, obtained through the **Celestial AI acquisition completed 2026-02-02**, is a **2.5D package placing an ASIC, HBM CACHE and DDR5 CHANNELS together on an ACTIVE PHOTONIC INTERPOSER** — on Marvell's disclosed specification, **up to EIGHT DDR5 DIMMs, 72GB of HBM CACHE and 7.2Tbps of OPTICAL BANDWIDTH.** The author's observation: if Fig. 4b is the XPU-pool/memory-pool structure, **Marvell's memory module + PF-NIC configuration is closer to an implementation of that structure AT MODULE LEVEL, ARRIVING FIRST** — and the problem definitions overlap (the paper points at **I/O density at the die periphery limiting channel count and routing flexibility in 2D**, while **Marvell takes optics out from the MIDDLE of the die rather than the die edge**: different implementation, same constraint). ⚠️⚠️ **THE AUTHOR'S OWN LIMIT ON THAT OBSERVATION MUST TRAVEL WITH IT: *"The inclusion of Celestial [AI's] Photonic Fabric in the paper's table of industrial platforms is verifiable, but there [is no] announcement that SK hynix and Marvell are co-developing in the optical memo[ry] space. The confirmed contact points between the two remain custom HBM and C[MM-]Ax. Sharing a structure and having a joint product are two different things."*** ➜ **Recorded here precisely because the figure-vs-product resemblance is easy to over-read into a partnership nobody has announced. Marvell's CXL/optical memory stack — CMM-Ax, the Structera ladder, the 32TB shared warm KV tier and the NVIDIA $2B investment / NVLink Fusion silicon-photonics co-development (Mar-2026) — is filed on [cxl-memory-fabric.md](cxl-memory-fabric.md), not duplicated here.**
  - **THE SUPPLY-CHAIN CONCLUSION, WHICH IS THIS PAGE'S OWN "OWN THE LIGHT SOURCE" THESIS EXTENDED ONE LAYER DOWNSTREAM: as optics moves closer to compute — pluggable → NPO → CPO → photonic interposer — supply-chain value moves with it. VOLUME GROWTH IN OPTICAL ENGINES AND LASER SOURCES COMES FIRST, then SILICON-PHOTONICS PICs, DRIVERS AND TIAs, FIBRE COUPLING, 2.5D INTERPOSERS AND 3D BONDING gain weight after that.** *"If CPO and optical memory reach high-volume production, the beneficiary set is not limited to light source makers but widens across photonics and advanced packaging."* ➜ **Directionally identical to the Redburn hybrid-bonding-equipment leg already in these debates (cumulative ~50 BESI CPO bonders by 2030, accelerating from 2H28) and to the ASE/TSMC packaging rows in "Who's exposed" — what it adds is the SEQUENCING that ties packaging to the laser thesis rather than leaving them as parallel lists.** ⚠️⚠️ **NO TAM, NO UNITS, NO DATES ATTACH TO IT. The author's own framing is a call for *"a second round in optical investing"* — that is positioning, not a forecast, and it is one independent author's book. Nothing here is a rated view.**
- **Bull / where the money is — own the light source.** In a CPO module the laser is ~30–40% of BOM vs only 10–15% in a pluggable, because CPO lasers are far less commoditized (more IP, higher complexity) — so the differentiated money is in lasers, not module assembly (Mike/Rothschild, 2026-05-19). CPO TAM ~$15B by 2030 (Rothschild), which Coherent itself now calls "conservative"; scale-across ~$7B; OCS and scale-up Ethernet each ~$6B (Mike/Rothschild, 2026-05-19). Corning's content/GPU rises from low-hundreds of dollars toward 1.3–1.5x in scale-up, and a fully-optical scale-up world needs ~10x the fiber of today's 16 fibers/GPU (Corning, 2026-05-12). 🆕 **2026-08-17 — THE THESIS NOW HAS A THREE-RUNG GROSS-MARGIN LADDER FROM ONE WEEK OF PRINTS, WHICH IS THE CLEANEST EVIDENCE ON THIS PAGE THAT THE VALUE SITS IN THE LIGHT SOURCE: [[LITE]] GM 50.4% (F4Q26) > [[COHR]] 40.2% > Fabrinet 12.2% (Q4 FY26, on 1.3% opex for a 10.9% operating margin, a three-year high) — laser/component prime, integrated laser+systems prime, and contract manufacturer of the same optical hardware. And the filed version of the same point: [[LITE]]'s FY26 10-K attributes 78% of Components growth to laser chip and laser assembly *"complemented by a slight INCREASE in average selling prices… driven primarily by a shift to 200G lane speeds"* (≈$693.7m, ~50.7% of the company's ENTIRE incremental FY26 revenue) while the Systems cloud transceiver lines grew *"more than 173% due to an increase in shipment VOLUME, partially offset by LOWER average selling prices."* ⚠️ **But the same 10-K bounds the mix argument: of LITE's FY26 GM-dollar increase (GAAP GM 41.7% vs 28.0%), ~54% came from LOWER MANUFACTURING COST AS A % OF REVENUE on higher internal factory UTILIZATION, only ~29% from MIX SHIFT, and 17% from lower intangible amortization. In this cycle the dominant margin lever is loading the factory — which is a mundane alternative explanation for the LITE-vs-COHR GM gap and does not require anyone's CPO-laser yield to be bad.**
- **Bear / what could break it.** (1) **Capacity flooding** — any rampant-growth cycle invites overbuild fear, and China oversupply + valuation re-rating is already driving a "weaker pure-play optics" narrative (LITE, AAOI, FN, CIEN, IQE) vs "stronger" copper+optics names (SMTC, GLW, ALAB, CRDO, MRVL) (@crux_capital_, company-specific briefing 2026-06-19). (2) **Multiples** — the basket re-rated from teens to 25–30x CY28; MS flags least control of output = most caution, ranking by destiny-control: Lumentum > Corning > Coherent > Ciena (Meta Marshall/MS, 2026-05-07). (3) **CPO-in-scale-out is modest** — replacing scale-out transceivers with CPO saves only ~2–3% of cluster power and you lose the field-replaceability of a pluggable, so scale-out CPO penetration should lag scale-up materially (Mike/Rothschild, 2026-05-19).
- **CPO vs pluggables vs LPO — coexistence, not winner-take-all.** The Feb panic that pluggables were "older generation / going to be very bad" has not shown up in demand — pluggables see no negative impact, even stronger demand; shortage in everything (Andy/MS, 2026-05-07). The likely split: CPO penetration much higher in scale-up than scale-out; pluggables/IMDD persist for scale-out and DCI; LPO and linear re-drivers fill copper-to-optics edges. Teradyne sees SiPh and EML coexisting (Jevons-paradox adoption as SiPh commoditizes) rather than one replacing the other (Teradyne/Arcuri, 2026-05-04).
- **OCS — Google is the anchor, externalization is the catalyst.** OCS keeps signals optical via MEMS mirror arrays (Lumentum) or liquid-crystal (Coherent), avoiding power-hungry O-E-O conversions. Google historically built OCS in-house and is now sourcing externally — a natural buyer for Lumentum's MEMS arrays; LITE targets $400M OCS in 1H-FY27 and ~$1–2B in 2027, gated by test-time/throughput not demand, with the roadmap "fracturing" into high-Radix (training), low-Radix (inference) and scale-up variants (Lumentum/Michael+Wupen, 2026-05-07). Mike/Rothschild has more confidence in LITE's field-tested MEMS than COHR's less-proven liquid-crystal (2026-05-19).
- **Hybrid-bonding equipment is a new, levered way to play CPO-into-scale-up.** Redburn forecasts a cumulative **50 BESI hybrid bonders to serve CPO applications by 2030, accelerating from 2H28** — because the EIC-PIC optical-engine bond uses D2W hybrid bonding (heterogeneous die favor D2W over W2W), feeding TSMC's COUPE/SoIC roadmap. Redburn raises BESI FY26-30 revenue estimates +9–20% and EBIT +13–27% (CPO + China LogicFolding + mainstream OSAT recovery; OSAT capex expectations +122% YTD, ASE utilization 59%→80% by 1Q26), Buy, PT €350 (Redburn "BESI", 2026-06-15). Read-through: CPO scale-up is now showing up in *equipment* order books, not just module/laser forecasts.
- **Timeline for optics inside the rack.** Scale-out CPO is the near wave (now); scale-up arrives in stages — uplinks/multi-rack first (OCP/October-26 timeframe for industry awareness), then true inside-the-rack GPU-to-GPU "realistically the very end of this decade," because copper stays cheaper and known until you're forced off it (650 Group, 2026-06-04). The disaggregation bet matters: hyperscalers are forcing Broadcom/Marvell to build switch chips (next-gen Tomahawk) that support an open CPO ecosystem, which is what lets Ciena/Nubis (Vesta CPO, Nitro linear re-driver) play against captive solutions (Ciena, 2026-06-12).

| [COHR](../COHR.md) | $1.5B→$1.8B, +27% pro forma ↑ | InP-constrained; 6-inch ramp 1 qtr early ↑ | 1.6T ramp "faster than expected" ↑ | OCS TAM >$4B, CPO TAM >$15B, NVDA $2B ↑ | 38.2%→39.6%, target >42% ↑ | Orders into CY28, LTAs to end-decade ↑ |
| [CIEN](../CIEN.md) | $911M→$1.57B, +40% YoY ↑ | Demand outstripping supply (pumps/memory) ↑ | >2x pluggables, exiting '26 ~#1 800G ↑ | Hyper-Rail 1st order, Nubis ~$5B opp ↑ | 43.5%→44.9% adj, 3rd straight raise ↑ | Backlog $7.7B, "visibility into 2027" ↑ |
| [GLW](../GLW.md) | Optical +51%→+36% YoY → | Capacity constraints showing; capex ramp ↑ | Content/attach scales 800G→1.6T ↑ | $10B Photonics MAP, NVDA prepay+warrant ↑ | Op margin 18%→20.2%, hit goal early ↑ | Meta $6B + 2 more hyperscaler LTAs ↑ |
| [ANET](../ANET.md) | $1.93B→$2.71B, +35% YoY ↑ | "Demand best ever"; outstripping supply ↑ | 800G Etherlink >100 cust; 1.6T '26-27 ↑ | XPO optics, scale-up "Bluebox" ~2027+ → | 64.2%→62.4%, memory cost drag ↓ | PCs $8.9B, deferred-rev 6-8 qtr cycles ↑ |
| [AAOI](../AAOI.md) | $43M→$151M, +51% YoY ↑ | Capacity-gated; true demand $1.4-1.5B ↑ | 800G only $4.6M, step-function 2H'26 ↑ | 1.6T late-'26 ramp '27; CPO laser early ↑ | 22.5%→29.2%, 40% by Q3/Q4'27 target ↑ | FY26 raised >$1.1B; 3-yr LTAs in talks → |
| [CRDO](../CRDO.md) | $223M→$437M, +157% YoY ↑ | Supply-chain "significant tightness" ↑ | AEC bridge; optical DSP to 1.6T ↑ | Optical raised >$600M FY27, Dust Photonics ↑ | 67.6%→68.3% non-GAAP → | FY27 raised >80% YoY; 4 cust ≥10% ↑ |

**Read:** The signal is uniform and load-bearing — every member is compounding top-line at 27-157% YoY with the bottleneck being capacity/supply (InP lasers, pumps, memory), not demand, and all are layering CPO/scale-up/OCS new vectors onto multi-year locked visibility (LTAs, $7.7B+ backlog, take-or-pay). The two cracks in the consensus: ANET's gross margin is the lone decliner (memory inflation) and AAOI/CRDO carry the most execution risk, with their arcs riding on a 2027 ramp that has yet to print.

linewidth best at 200mW; relaxation oscillation ~3GHz plus a 15GHz feature; all at 50C, *"frankly a pessimistic case"*), the **OFC 2026 ELSFP live demo at 13% module wall-plug efficiency vs a 10% industry target but derating to ~10-11% at a realistic 40C die / 50C coldplate**, and 🔴 **the yield-moat argument: DWDM CPO needs ±30 GHz wavelength-spacing accuracy and off-target lasers must be thermally tuned at ~0.1 nm/K, pushing the TEC toward reverse/heating mode with worse power, efficiency and noise.** ⚠️ **NOT adopted: the central claim that *"the only way COHR's numbers make sense is if CPO-laser yield is bad"* (COHR discloses no yield data at any power class or data rate, so it is unfalsifiable from outside, and it collides with COHR's own FQ1 FY27 GM guide of 41.5% vs Street 40.0% already on this page), the author's ~80% CPO-laser gross-margin construction for LITE, and the trading tone.** The 6-inch hypothesis (bad yield possibly from **Sumitomo 6-inch InP wafer uniformity** rather than COHR process; **[[AXTI]] still R&D on 6-inch**) foots with MS's 08-03 AXT read and the structure of the two AXT LTAs. Its VCSEL objection (optimal 32G/64G NRZ; **200G PAM4 *"going to fail GR-468"***) is a restatement of the 05-08 dismissal and is answered, not refuted, by PhotonCap's 07-29 slow-and-wide UCIe/PCIe framing already on this page. [Irrational Analysis — "[Market Memo] Orange Man Attempted Chinese Transceiver Ban" (2026-08-04)](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/IA_transceiver_ban.html) — the engineering read on the same regulatory event this page carries from Reuters/MS/Jefferies/GS/TrendForce: **new-models-only, so 1.6T is already qualified and it is *"not a huge deal"* until 3.2T, *"which is far away"***; 🔴 **the SE-Asia workaround is *"more than a shell"* — Innolight, Eoptolink, Accelink and others with US-registered entities and Malaysia/Thailand/Vietnam footprints, IP licensed in, and nearly all major production (TOSA/ROSA active alignment, PCB assembly, module assembly, burn-in) outside mainland China** (a third independent voice converging with GS 08-11 and TrendForce 08-06 that the relocation is already in production); **the real Chinese advantage is ACTIVE ALIGNMENT, not design** (six-axis, low-single-digit-micron to sub-micron, custom in-house machines — *"TFC, Inno, Accelink, Eopto wipe the floor with the west"*), with **Fabrinet *"the world's best non-Chinese optical contract manufacturer"***; 🔴 **the EML→SiPho pivot arithmetic — *"a SiPho transceiver has ~50% less InP content than an EML-based transceiver but significantly higher active-alignment content. Everyone has an MZI-based PIC for 1.6T. It is trivial to pivot manufacturing from EML based to SiPho based. The issue is active alignment capacity/throughput, not the design itself"*** (the conversion factor that partially NETS this page's InP-scarcity thread against its SiPho-share thread — bounded per-bin in the Aug-17 night section); and the **[[TSEM]] read: LITE, COHR and AAOI all use Tower for the SiPho PIC so a SiPho shift still lands on Tower, with the Openlight InP-on-SiPho PDK *"very valuable in a world where active alignment efficiency becomes a bottleneck"*** — the mechanism BofA's 08-07 street-high *"superior PIC technology"* assertion was missing. Also rejects the security rationale on engineering grounds (transceiver = PHY, data encrypted at host endpoints, limited CMIS register space with signed key authentication per OIF-CMIS-05.4 §9.9.2). ⚠️ **NOT adopted: the [[AXTI]] price calls (*"a nice 15-20% single-day drop"* on retaliation; *"easily a 2-4x within the next 12-18 months"*) and the [[AAOI]] *"fun degen play"* framing — self-declared long, trading tone. ## Changelog

- **🆕 2026-09-01 (/newsletters-to-vault — the SECOND publication in the same 09-01 lane, after the DAMNANG block: **FundaAI (Substack)**, *"Deep|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray, and the Bandwidth Opportunity Could 10x Scale-Up"*, 2026-09-01. ⚠️ **INDEPENDENT SUBSTACK PUBLISHER, NOT A BROKER — no rating, no price target, no house model, and NO dollar TAM in the note by design.** Inserted: 1 new dated sub-block inside the existing 09-01 section, 1 Sources entry. ✅ **NOTHING SUPERSEDED — no CPO/NPO date, TAM, content estimate, unit forecast, PT or rating on this page was replaced.**)**
  🔴 **NET-NEW: a FOURTH tier inward of scale-out/scale-up/scale-across — **scale-in**, defined as die-to-die and chip-to-memory optical links at *"millimeters to about one meter"* — plus the four-rung bandwidth ladder on Rubin public specs (**~1.6 Tb/s scale-out → 14.4 Tb/s scale-up (NVLink 6, one way) → 35-70 Tb/s pooled → 176 Tb/s local HBM4**; *"each step inward is about 10x, end-to-end about 100x"*), the three-way TAXONOMY CONFLICT on the word "scale-in" ([[NVDA]] = a BlueField-4 DPU services network at 800 Gb/s per Vera Rubin compute tray; [[META]] = electrical chiplet DMA in MTIA 400; Azure = autoscaling instance removal), and the in-tray technology fork (**SiPh/DWDM vs VCSEL/micro-VCSEL vs microLED**, with Lightmatter Passage M1000 at **114 Tbps**, LightXcelerate at **10 Tbps/mm / <2 pJ/bit**, Avicena at **200 fJ/bit** and kits **0.5-0.9 → 1 Tbps**, [[CRDO]]/Hyperlume at **~$92m**).** This page already carried the TERM from [[LITE]]/Barclays (08-25) and one @jukan05 line (08-31) — not the definition, reach, ratios or taxonomy split.
  ⚠️ **TWO GUARDRAILS COPIED FROM THE SOURCE INTO THE BODY, BECAUSE THEY ARE THE PART MOST LIKELY TO BE MISUSED: (1) the **176 Tb/s local-HBM figure is a COMPARISON POINT ONLY** — *"local HBM stays electrical for as far as we can see"* — and must never be chained into a TAM bridge; (2) the **20-40% of HBM traffic** that yields the 35-70 Tb/s pooled number is the **publisher’s ASSUMPTION**, not a disclosure.
  ⚠️ **A DIRECT TENSION WITH THE DAMNANG BLOCK IMMEDIATELY ABOVE IT, LOGGED AND NOT NETTED: DAMNANG routes optical scarcity UP the InP chain (*"three firms hold 80-90%"* of InP substrate); FundaAI says that at in-tray reach *"InP and TFLN fall short on density and power"* and the winners are SiPh and VCSEL. **Different chains on different clocks — today’s pluggable/CPO vs a 2029-30+ in-tray tier. Both stand.**
  ✅ **CAPITAL FIGURES GREPPED BEFORE INSERT AND ALL ALREADY PRESENT — logged as restatement, not as new money: **$2bn each from [[NVDA]] into [[LITE]] and [[COHR]]** (March 2026), **$3.25bn [[MRVL]]/Celestial AI**, and the **$120bn** [[GOOG]]-warrant ceiling. ⚠️ **ONE DISCREPANCY FLAGGED, NOT FIXED: FundaAI still describes the **Kyber rack generation as "planned for 2027"** with Rubin Ultra at **1 TB of HBM4E announced** (August-2026 reports of 192-256 GB testing). **This page carries Kyber as CANCELLED (08-18) vs PUSHED OUT (08-20) — an already-logged unresolved conflict — and `themes/hbm-memory` holds the Rubin Ultra de-spec as a live 192/256/288/384GB debate against the previewed 1TB. His 192-256GB band corroborates the low end; his Kyber framing does not reflect the cancellation.**

Not logged as a disagreement.** 🔴 **What they genuinely disagree about is the BRIDGE: Mizuho/SA argue copper and [[CRDO]] AEC for the interim; JPM argues NPO broadens the customer set away from a CPO ecosystem that "has largely been [[NVDA]]." Same 2028, two different intermediate trades — that is now the live question on this page.**
  **New content:** 🔴 **NPO split into TWO products with separate ramp dates for the first time** — an **ELS version (2H27, aligned to CPO ELS)** and an **EMBEDDED version (2028, medium-power laser, ASP between the NVDA ultra-high-power part and a 200G EML, and a HIGHER barrier to entry)**; 🔴 **the merchant CW opportunity sized at 10-20% of [[LITE]]'s 2027 chip output**, driven by silicon photonics going from 20-30% of mix to 50-60%, **with a CW supply imbalance being converted into concessions ACROSS the portfolio (OCS, transceivers, EML)** — the mechanism by which a single-component shortage shows up as margin rather than revenue; **multi-year LTAs reaching the transceiver line**, with [[LITE]] and [[COHR]] taking opposite positions on pump lasers; **[[COHR]] InP 50/50 3-inch/6-inch → 75% 6-inch in CY27**; the **transceiver-margin gap and Hurlston's raised bogey (sub-30% / mid-20s modelled → "mid 30s or high 30s")**; **[[COHR]]'s unlaunched "photo link" NPO kit**; **micro-LED with [[MSFT]] and [[GOOG]] named**; and the constraint that **[[LITE]] is not permitted to use contract manufacturers for transceivers because [[GOOG]] and [[MSFT]] object**. Plus the **Jalapeño interconnect topology** (copper inside the rack, optical between, 8-rail global domain, 1.6T front-panel transceivers, an OCS per rack — SemiAnalysis's inference — and scale-up networking at ~10% of system cost).

  🔴🔴 **THE MATERIAL ADDITION IS THE PAPER'S PHYSICS, WHICH THIS PAGE HAS BEEN ARGUING WITHOUT: compute throughput has tripled roughly every two years while interconnect bandwidth advanced only ~1.4-fold over the same two-year cycle — with the copper mechanism named (resistive loss and capacitive loading return once data crosses board and cables; equalization, retimers, DSP and FEC correct for it while ADDING power and latency). ➜ A ~3x-vs-~1.4x-per-2yr divergence is now the page's cleanest justification for optics moving into the package, attributable to a technical review with a memory maker involved rather than to vendor material.**
  ✅ **AND IT SUPPLIES A PEER-REVIEWED VERSION OF AN ORDERING THIS PAGE ALREADY ADOPTED COMMERCIALLY: pluggable → on-board optics → 2.5D CPO → 3D TSV → hybrid bonding → monolithic, with 2.5D the manufacturability/performance compromise. On that ladder the NPO reported for NVL576 is an INTERMEDIATE step that lowers package-redesign and serviceability burden, not the end of the transition — the same conclusion as "NPO IS ADDITIVE, NOT A BRIDGE THAT GETS TORN DOWN" (08-17), Fabrinet's NPO-before-CPO sequencing (08-17) and the 650 Group's "NPO as the pragmatic bridge" (08-14).   🆕 **THE LONG-DATED ARCHITECTURE IS NOW ON THE PAGE AS A PAPER FIGURE WITH ITS UNSOLVED-PROBLEM LIST ATTACHED — Fig. 4b XPU pool + memory pool linked by a photonic interposer and optical fibre; Fig. 4c an MCM cross-section on a photonic interposer with embedded optical waveguides; Fig. 4d stacked PIC/EIC through optical TSVs; Fig. 3 per-method energy and bandwidth density (pluggable column on a transceiver basis) — against the paper's own open problems: a coherence-preserving optical memory interface, low-power photonic switching, multi-hop signal regeneration. One new item ⑥ (five sub-bullets) appended to the existing Aug-3 signals block + one Sources entry.
