With Kyber cancelled/pushed out, Nvidia is weighing two replacement architectures carrying the SAME 576 compute dies — a conservative four-rack "Beer One" [ASR-garbled rack name, not resolvable from the transcript] system on traditional scale-out, versus a two-rack design (Titan) using NPO or CPO for the inter-rack scale-out link. Compute content fixed, optics content the entire difference between the candidates.** **Dates added, and they are earlier than the OCP catalyst this page had been pointing at: Rubin racks ship from the December 2026 quarter; the new Rubin Ultra rack design lands around the December quarter of 2027; downstream spec clarity expected in 1Q27 "or a little later, second quarter" — "at least before the second quarter of 2027." Fubon's own timing warning is quoted verbatim: "we are already in August, so the timeline is very, very tight to enter production for Rubin Ultra," against a design that is "not yet completed."** **Mechanism added, which is what the CPO thesis on this page has lacked from a supply-chain source: Nvidia's fixed goal of 144 GPUs per rack is blocked by heat dissipation, CABLE DESIGN and existing data-center power density, with a single rack potentially at ~1MW — copper cabling is named as one of three physical blockers, which is a stronger adoption argument than any TAM estimate here. Hon Hai has multiple two-rack/four-rack proposals with Nvidia.** ⚠️ **NAME RECONCILIATION LOGGED: the "Taycan" in the 08-03 relay item ④ and the "Titan" here are the same candidate design — the ASR renders it "Tycan"/"TaiWan" and this run's glossary resolves it to Titan. Recorded so the two entries are not read as two architectures.** ⚠️ **"Beer One" is left unresolved with an explicit ASR caveat rather than guessed.** Cross-filed [ai-datacenter-power](ai-datacenter-power.md) and [800v-dc-power](800v-dc-power.md) (~1MW/rack), [cowos-packaging](cowos-packaging.md) (576 compute dies), [outros-asia](outros-asia.md) (Hon Hai); HBM/memory and TPU legs of the same call routed to [hbm-memory](hbm-memory.md) and [custom-asic-tpu](custom-asic-tpu.md).
- **2026-08-01 — /run-inbox · [[TER]] Q2 CY26 call transcript (2026-07-29).** ⚠️ Recovery ingest: archived unrouted on 07-31, restored. New dated signals block; nothing overwritten. **Two items materially change how this theme should carry the CPO test ramp.** **① The TAM is sized AND the near year is guided down: CPO test is "a $300 million to $700 million market by 2028," but 2027 is explicitly guided to the low end — "probably aiming towards more of the low side… kind of the low-end would be in the $200 million range. I don't think there is as much upside next year as there is upside in 2028."** The path is ~$100M (2026 implied) → ~$200M (2027) → $300-700M (2028), with the 2028 outcome hinging on whether **scale-out CPO volume ramps succeed during 2027**. **② The "insertion 2 can be skipped with known-good die" thesis is rebutted on process grounds** — between insertions the electronic IC is bonded to the photonic IC wafer and **a polymer lens is applied**, and since "the lens assembly is a critical part of the optical path," loss and polarization tests cannot be run until the completed wafer exists. The narrower open question management concedes is how much post-bonding test stays at wafer level vs deferring to assembled optical engines. ⚠️ **A caution that should temper every insertion-share claim on this page (including the [[ADVANTEST]] and Chroma percentages): TER and a customer spent 15 minutes in conversation before discovering they had entirely different definitions of insertion 2 — "it's a mistake to draw broad conclusions" at this stage of maturity.** ⚠️ **Also worth reading as a signal in itself: the vendor is deliberately hedged across the physical layer** — Photon 100 (optical), MultiLane Test Products JV (copper), Quantifi Photonics — because "the timing of transitions from cable to backplane and from pluggable to XPO and CPO is in constant flux," with **NPO and XPO named as intermediate stages** and switch silicon as the layer that grows "independent of the physical layer." That is a supplier declining to underwrite a clean pluggable→CPO transition, consistent with the SemiAnalysis skepticism already carried here. Cross-filed [[TER]], [[ADVANTEST]], [semicap-wfe](semicap-wfe.md).
- **2026-07-31 (wiki-ingest) — the InP substrate layer is confirmed as the binding constraint, and Insertion-4 test tooling ships in 3Q/4Q26. New section: `## Recent signals (Jul 31)`.** **★ The cleanest evidence available that substrate AVAILABILITY (not cost, not module capacity) is the constraint: [[LITE]] signed a long-term InP supply agreement with [[AXTI]] despite having expressed confidence months earlier in a ~7-YEAR SUMITOMO AGREEMENT — with the Lumentum CEO "open recently about how they need to MULTI SOURCE AGGRESSIVELY." A 7-year incumbent agreement proved insufficient** (AXT Q2 2026 call, primary, 2026-07-30/31). **The money moving upstream prices the scarcity: $25.4M from [[COHR]], $22.3M from Casela, and a Lumentum agreement reserving minimum annual InP capacity through 2031 with two $43.5M deposits.** ⚠️ **ACCOUNTING TRAP flagged: that cash is booked as a LIABILITY and recognised only as AXT ships — it "didn't show up in the rev numbers this Q." Bridging LTA announcements to reported revenue double-counts.** **Supply ramp: AXT AHEAD OF SCHEDULE on doubling InP capacity in 2026; InP revenue opportunity to MORE THAN TRIPLE by end-2026; backlog >$100M; ~$66M realizable in Q3 without a permit; ~$130M/QUARTER exiting 2027 (~$520M annualised); expects to become "by far the largest indium phosphide producer in the world." And it is still not enough — "demand outpaces supply, no matter how fast they add capacity… AXT is shipping RECORD levels of InP wafers to LITE and COHR. And they still want more."** Demand mechanism kept precise: **800G→1.6T as OVERLAPPING (not substituting) demand; MORE LASERS PER TRANSCEIVER; higher-spec 6-inch substrates; and hyperscalers "advancing toward NPO and CPO." Also: GaAs customers are "also very interested in InP" — VCSEL and InP demand move TOGETHER.** ⚠️ **Unforecast upside logged: AXT still lacks US export permits — "if that unlocks, you are looking at a major step up again that isn't being forecasted" — with China at ~40-60% of InP revenue (doubling q/q, "not gated by permits") leaving ex-China headroom.** **★ CPO test share is now explicit: Chroma ATE claims >50% share in CPO test Insertion 3 and 100% IN INSERTION 4.0 with shipments from 3Q26, and RAISED 2027 optical-comm revenue to NT$10BN FROM NT$7BN; transceiver light-source reliability test equipment orders >3X IN 2026E (JPM · Tsai, OW PT NT$3,000 → NT$3,160). On the handler side, Hon Precision's CPO Insertion-4 optical-electrical co-test handlers remain ON SCHEDULE FOR OCTOBER — "very high ASPs with a few volumes — effectively an option value."** ⚠️ **Both Chroma and Hon shipping Insertion-4 tooling in 3Q/4Q26 is the most concrete timing evidence this theme has that CPO VOLUME PRODUCTION IS A 2027 EVENT, NOT A 2026 ONE.** **★ [[GLW]] deliberately refuses to pick between CPO and MPO — the ~$10bn Photonics opportunity inside Springboard "includes assumptions for BOTH CPO and MPO," with the swing factors being optical scale-up adoption and whether Corning's product "moves inside the box." Architecture-agnosticism is a materially different risk profile from a pure-play CPO name. Hard numbers from the same call: fully-connectorized product sales NEARLY DOUBLED y/y in Q2; US fibre production being raised MORE THAN 50% and US CONNECTIVITY CAPACITY 10X, backed by multi-billion-dollar commitments including [[NVDA]] (with a prepayment); ~$2bn capex for the year with FCF still growing.** From the ASIC side: **[[MEDIATEK]] runs co-packaged copper TODAY with CPO via TSMC COUPE beyond it, 400G SerDes ready 2H27, and explicitly "beyond 400G SerDes will need to move to optical."** Packaging timing: **ASE expects CPO to BEGIN contributing in 2027, more meaningfully from 2028.** Positioning: **COHR and LITE both named among JPM's ~$750bn hyperscaler-capex beneficiaries and both in BofA's SECOND-WAVE re-entry list of names 30-50% off highs. Events: COHR+LITE callback 08-14; JPM HW & Semis forum 08-12 with optical panels (Ranovus, Avicena) and the 650 Group.** Cross-filed [[AXTI]], [[COHR]], [[LITE]], [[GLW]], [[POET]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[TER]], [[ADVANTEST]], [[TSM]].

- **2026-07-30 (Aurelion Research / PhotonCap "A Conversation with Lumentum", 2026-07-29 — a JOINT INDEPENDENT publication, NOT a bank). One layered State-of-play bullet + one Sources entry. NOTHING SUPERSEDED; THREE CONTRADICTIONS ADDED AND LEFT OPEN.** ⚠️ **Provenance governs the weighting and is carried on the page: the management meeting and investment analysis were prepared by Aurelion Research (met Kathryn Ta, VP Investor Relations at Lumentum); the technical comments are PhotonCap's; and the authors state interview responses are paraphrased and "do not represent verbatim statements by Lumentum or its management." Management, Aurelion's analysis and PhotonCap's commentary are attributed separately throughout.** **THE TIMING CONTRADICTION (#1), which is the most valuable thing here: management dates copper's wall at "LATE 2027" — "that is when the first scale-up CPO deployments arrive, or MPO fiber connectors… driven by link lengths beyond roughly three meters connecting rack to rack" — while also saying today's CPO is "opportunistic rather than forced" and "copper has not run out of headroom yet." That is the EARLY end of this page's range and it comes from a supplier's IR, which is stronger provenance than any expert or broker mark here. It sits against three existing marks: [../LITE.md](../LITE.md)'s own Director of Silicon-Photonics R&D (07-23: CPO trial ~2028, hyperscaler deploy ~2030, GPU-GPU copper ≥1 more generation); MS/Marshall's 07-28 markdown of 2028 CPO SCALE ("not later, just smaller"); and the 650 Group / UBS / MS cluster at partial-2028 / meaningful-2029. All retained, none adopted.** **THE SIZING CONTRADICTION (#2): Aurelion's bull #4 carries Global Semi Research's 2026-06-10 channel check — NVDA guidance to Lumentum and Coherent for high-power CW lasers at roughly 40MM units in January rising to roughly 100MM by April/May, back-solving to CPO switch shipments of roughly 200,000-300,000 in 2027 and 600,000-800,000 in 2028, aligned to TSMC's COUPE plan — against SemiAnalysis's 2026-06-09 institutional note (scale-out CPO revised down through FY27, scale-up pushed to FY29, "CPO output is driven by a single ASIC that is very limited," ASIC integration difficult with challenging economics), the note behind the June selloff. With MS's "smaller in 2028" that makes THREE incompatible 2027-28 CPO sizings now on this page. ⚠️ Aurelion's own weighting is retained verbatim: "These are channel figures rather than company disclosure, so we weight them accordingly." The honest position logged: CPO unit volume is not forecastable until NVDA's rack architecture is fixed.** **THE SUPPLY CONTRADICTION (#3), and management supplied it themselves: Japanese EML capacity is up EIGHT-FOLD in two and a half years (two Japan fabs make most of the world's EML supply), Lumentum runs five fabs with a sixth ramping but "not yet producing wafers," tools have been added at every site and several building shells expanded — against an unchanged ~30% demand-over-supply imbalance, all EML capacity under LTAs through FY27, and a McKinsey study putting 800G transceiver output at ~HALF of demand in FY27 with shortfalls through 2029. Aurelion flags it directly ("cuts against a pure scarcity thesis… Lumentum is racing to relieve the bottleneck that gives it pricing power"), and PhotonCap converts it into the best argument this page carries against the SHORTAGE-PREMIUM trade as distinct from the demand thesis: "Wafer starts and qualified yield at the newest spec are different numbers. A fab-level capacity multiple does not relieve a 200G per lane shortage one to one, because the binding constraint is yield at the highest power bins, not floor space… That is why the eight fold number overstates the relief, and why the shortage premium itself is not durable while the margin that survives lives in yield and mix, not in the shortage price." Read precisely: it defends the earnings while attacking the reason the market pays for them.** **NET-NEW MECHANISMS AND FRAMINGS (additive, nothing displaced): (a) the POWER-BUDGET mechanism, better than the copper-runs-out one — "copper's limit is not a date. It is a cost curve of reach, speed, and power," and "a data center runs against a fixed power envelope, so every watt saved on networking is a watt handed to the GPUs, and Nvidia's own material puts CPO at 3.5x lower power than pluggable transceivers. Copper is not forcing this wave; the power budget is," with the systemic caveat that it "rides on one company's architecture choice" — which connects straight to the unresolved Fubon post-Kyber rack decision logged 07-28. (b) The ARCHITECTURE-SCALING framing, which lowers the bar the bull case has to clear: "the optics thesis necessitates that AI systems continue scaling, not only that copper hits a wall" — NVDA moving from 72-GPU domains "toward as many as 576 by 2027," copper "already confined on an NV72 rack," and Rubin's bi-directional signalling degrading substantially at 448G per lane over copper. (c) A WORKING MOAT DEFINITION to replace "high power": qualified usable power at operating temperature × spectral quality × reliability × high-volume yield, made concrete via RIN, COD, SMSR and FIT — "headline specs are what challengers close first. Distribution tails, noise, and lifetime statistics close last." (d) The EML/CW product map ("EML is today's cash flow and high power CW is the next mix shift") plus the delay-by-cause split: an integration-driven CPO delay "pushes CW and ELSFP revenue out while extending the EML cycle," so cannibalization "flips into a cushion" — a direct read on the June-10 complex-wide de-rate. (e) A THIRD light-source platform this page did not carry: GaAs VCSEL at consumer scale (>10bn emitters shipped) now pointed at AI, with an OFC 2026 1060nm VCSEL + photodetector 2D monolithic array co-packaged with a host ASIC for UCIe/PCIe slow-and-wide scale-up, >150°C, channel sparing — "what sits on the roadmap is not VCSEL technology. It is commercial adoption in AI chip-to-chip links." Cuts against Irrational Analysis's 05-08 dismissal of VCSELs on reliability grounds by targeting a slow-and-wide rather than high-speed-serial use case. (f) SECOND SOURCING as fact, not hypothesis: "NVIDIA's technical blog names three ELS vendors, Lumentum, Sumitomo, and Coherent" — the named trio is net-new here (Sumitomo had appeared only as substrate/capacity), and it is a direct nuance to BTG's 06-25 "essentially two, possibly three suppliers": the third name is Sumitomo, on NVDA's own list. Same-day $2B into each of LITE and COHR; COHR's OFC 2026 6-inch InP ramp + 400mW CW; AAOI's 400mW narrow-linewidth sampling (Dec 2025); "competing capacity lands outside the FY27 LTA window" — consistent with the WIN Semiconductors CW-laser project at YE27 MP logged 07-26. (g) A CHINA update: Zetta Semiconductor "no longer fits either box, not the low power dismissal and not the imminent catch-up call" — mass-producible 100G PAM4 EML at OECC 2025, 200G-per-lane device at ACP November 2025, so "device level performance deserves to be taken seriously now," with hyperscale qualification of the full spec distribution across temperature and lifetime the remaining gate. Extends the DSBJ 200G-EML-MP-2H26 watch-item (Jefferies 07-14) with a second named vendor. (h) The 4-inch-vs-6-inch competitive line: COHR's 6-inch gives "roughly 2.25 times the usable area per wafer" and "Lumentum still runs 4 inch wafers and is trying to catch up" (COHR InP "also sold out through 2027") — ⚠️ 2.25x is a 4→6-inch AREA ratio, NOT Citi's 4.36x chips/wafer off a 3-inch base (both stand), and it sits against the 07-23 confirmation that LITE bought a 6-inch III-V fab in North Carolina (current volume production 4-inch, Greensboro 6-inch; not reconciled). (i) Capacity timing: Greensboro (ex-Qorvo, GaAs→InP, fifth InP fab) contributing revenue "roughly six quarters out from the May call, which puts it in late 2027 at the earliest," plus a SEPARATE new US facility funded alongside the Nvidia investment that is "a ground-up build rather than a conversion"; EML production expanding ~40% annually against peak-priced commitments 18 months ahead of volume. (j) The inference-mix honesty check, which this page should carry: "training is the most communication intensive workload, and the way that optics have traditionally traded is around training economics. Inference is less network intensive… so a mix shift toward inference does not translate to a 1:1 demand for optics" — closing via longer context windows, heavier routing, disaggregated prefill/decode, agentic usage and geographically distributed inference driving DCI, with the AMD+Cerebras July split-inference solution as the live example and its bound kept ("what that link is made of has not been disclosed… evidence that inference moves a lot of data, not evidence of any vendor's revenue").** Cross-filed [../LITE.md](../LITE.md), [../COHR.md](../COHR.md), [../NVDA.md](../NVDA.md), [../AVGO.md](../AVGO.md), [../AMD.md](../AMD.md), [tokenmaxxing](tokenmaxxing.md).
- **2026-07-28 (/run-inbox — two sources, pulling opposite ways). One layered State-of-play bullet + two Sources entries. Nothing superseded; ONE TENSION FLAGGED AND LEFT OPEN.** **(1) Fubon (Sherman Shang / Daniel Yang, "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates," 2026-07-28)** puts the NPO/CPO question inside [[NVDA]]'s unresolved rack decision. **Kyber — 576 compute dies, 144 GPUs × 4 dies — was cancelled/delayed in March/April "due to mid-plane bandwidth issues,"** and the replacement must carry the same 576 dies. **Two candidates, neither finalised: (a) four Oberon racks per system on traditional scale-out, 144 compute dies each; (b) a two-rack design Fubon calls "Taycan" — 144 GPUs × 2 compute dies per rack, with NPO/CPO for scale-OUT between the two racks and cable for scale-UP inside.** **Option (a) carries no incremental optics; option (b) puts NPO/CPO on the critical path of NVDA's next flagship system — the two candidates ARE the bull and bear case for this page.** Fubon's posture logged verbatim and not extrapolated: **"the rack design for both solutions is not yet finalized… it is difficult to say who will benefit and who will suffer. But we think nVidia is still aggressive in the NPO/CPO solution despite a lot of market noise."** **(2) Morgan Stanley webcast · Meta Marshall (2026-07-28)** marks down 2028 CPO scale on the same day: optical bias **"a little bit more cautious, just because of concerns around CPO timing"** — but the formulation is precise and is NOT a delay call: **"we don't lean on there being delays… just the amount of scale that we think that it will have is just a little bit lower in 2028 versus kind of where expectations had been," which "created a little bit of a breather on the space in general."** **That is a distinct and more damaging claim than the delay calls already on this page: a delay pushes 2028 revenue right, a scale cut removes it.** Interest rotating to **networking, particularly Arista**, on 2027 acceleration while peers decelerate. **⚠️ THE TENSION IS THE POINT AND IS NOT SMOOTHED: Fubon reads NVDA as leaning INTO NPO/CPO at the same moment MS cuts the 2028 scale CPO will reach. They are reconcilable in principle — NVDA can be committed while 2028 volume lands below the sell-side bogey, particularly if the four-Oberon option wins the first system and CPO slips to the following generation — but they point opposite directions for anyone sizing 2028 optical content, and NVDA's rack finalisation is plausibly the event that resolves MS's scale question. Both views stay; neither is adopted.** Also folded from the same webcast: **Marshall's Corning read — enterprise +65% y/y, telco essentially flat, guide light on both revenue (~$200mn below MS's upside bogey) and EPS — which she interprets as SOLD-OUT CONDITIONS in optical rather than demand weakness**, with valuations **"contracting more towards historical valuations, but still on much more elevated EPS."** **Read logged: the optical de-rating in progress is a MULTIPLE event on a rising earnings base, not an earnings event, and should not be conflated with the 2028-scale worry above.** Cross-filed [cowos-packaging](cowos-packaging.md) (the packaging leg of the same Fubon note), [[NVDA]], [[GLW]], [[ANET]].
- **2026-07-27 (/run-inbox — UBS advanced-packaging note, 2026-07-27, slug `UBS_on_adv_packaging.html`). One State-of-play bullet + a Sources entry. Nothing superseded.** **Logged with an explicit honesty flag: this is the industry's biggest next-gen packaging note and it contains NO CPO unit forecast, revenue, port count or date — CPO appears only as a forward roadmap attribute of TSMC's CoPoS.** That absence is the finding: **on the two platforms that will carry AI accelerators in 2028-30, CPO is a differentiator on the slide, not a line in the model.** UBS's language kept verbatim — CoPoS offers "richer integration options such as embedded capacitors, 3D stacking **and eventually co-packaged optics (CPO)**"; the head-to-head Ecosystem Strength row reads CoPoS **"Deep ecosystem spanning foundry, CoWoS, SoIC and future CPO"** vs EMIB-T's "growing support from hyperscalers seeking alternative supply," verdict CoPoS; and TSMC's moat is **"a comprehensive technology portfolio of 2.5D/3D packaging and CPO."** **Net-new mechanism for this page: TSMC is using FUTURE CPO integration as a customer-retention argument against a cost-and-scale challenger TODAY, before CPO carries volume — i.e. CPO's near-term economic value to TSMC is platform lock-in, not CPO revenue.** **The one actionable CPO item is a scenario, not a forecast: "TSMC's share could further increase if Google's new TPUs (eg. v10) return to CoPoS and adopt CPO integration"** — making v10 the first named accelerator program where CPO adoption is a live commercial variable, and framing it as a reversion (v9 to Intel EMIB-T, v10 back to TSMC *because of* CPO). ⚠️ Sits against the Jefferies 06-29 mark already in Sources that **v9's 400G SerDes scale-up is "co-packaged copper," not optics** — v9 copper, v10 the CPO question; timeline now explicit rather than contradictory. **Timing implied and consistent with existing marks (nothing pulls CPO forward): CoPoS MP 2028 (Nvidia Feynman likely first, H2'28-2029), AMD and ASICs 2029-30, on 310×310mm² → 510×515mm² panels; pilot line installed late Q226; first-generation process flow, equipment specs and key suppliers fixed by MID-2027 — the one date to diarise.** Corroborates UBS's own 07-02 GPU-CPO ~2029E and MS's "no earlier than 2029." **Glass carried as a competing, SLOWER view: UBS distinguishes panel carriers (glass "the leading candidate") from glass-core substrates, and argues glass is NOT on the critical path — "both packaging technologies should be in mass production in 2028 without requiring glass-core substrates," with glass-core MP "unlikely before 2029-30E, if not later" and Ibiden's own slide at 2030+. Slower than the MS per-player ramps logged 07-26 (Innolux+TSMC+Ibiden 2H28-2029; Lens Tech+Intel 2H27); modest negative on the "glass substrate as CPO-era enabler" thesis attached to [../GLW.md](../GLW.md), and later than the 2H28 bulls.** Not resolved, both retained. Cross-filed [cowos-packaging](cowos-packaging.md), [custom-asic-tpu](custom-asic-tpu.md).
- **2026-07-26 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook, weekend batch). 2 State-of-play bullets. Nothing superseded.** **First, the best-quantified AI optical order datapoint available, and it is a clean surprise measurement rather than a retrospective: Nokia's 2Q26 AI order intake came in at €2.8bn against a €1.3-1.5bn buy-side bogey "that many investors were debating yesterday" — roughly 2x the top of the range, and "a level that far exceeded even the more bullish expectations"** (JPM/Scott Silver). It matters here because Nokia is a **systems vendor buying the optics**, so its order intake is upstream demand for exactly the transceiver/laser content [[LITE]] and [[COHR]] sell. ⚠️ **The reaction is the lesson: the stock reversed from +7% at the open to roughly flat, after already falling ~40% from its highs, with JPM writing "I struggle to identify what more the company realistically could have delivered today beyond raising guidance."** Same week's pattern at INTC, TSM, ASML, GOOGL and GEV — demand confirmed, tape not paying. Optical multiples have derated **-15% since the June 22 SOX peak**, having peaked earlier than the rest of the complex. Feedback has Nokia sustaining **20-25x → €12-15/share on 2028 numbers**; short interest **1 ADTV / 1% of free float**. **Second, and this is the supply response the moat thesis has to survive: JPM raised WIN Semiconductors (OW, PT NT$336 → NT$373) 2026 revenue growth to 30% from ~10% (Optical +50%+, Infra +40%+) on earlier-than-expected optical receiver chip (PD) mass production — "late 2Q vs 2H26" — with utilisation 65% (2Q) → 70%+ (3Q26e) and capex reiterated at NT$2-3bn, "significantly higher than the annual capex in prior years."** The demand concentration is striking: **only ONE PD project is in mass production, yet that single customer ("likely a major US design house") takes AI-related optical comm to a high-single-digit mix of WIN's total revenue in 2H26e, "more than doubled vs 2025,"** rising to double digits in 2027e. **And the competitive item: alongside short-distance VCSEL and long-distance EML laser chips already in MP since 2025, WIN has "the CW laser project with a major US optical chip IDM (mass production should take place in YE27)" — the first dated timeline on this page for third-party merchant capacity entering the CW/ultra-high-power laser bottleneck that BTG's LightCounting expert (06-25) described as effectively a two-supplier moat. Not a 2026 threat; a 2028 one.** Cross-filed [[LITE]], [[COHR]], [[AAOI]], [[AXTI]].
- 2026-07-25 — /wiki-ingest (Outlook). Added 2 State-of-play bullets; additive, no prior number superseded. Main source: **Irrational Analysis "Stranded Indium-Phosphide" (2026-07-25)** — an engineering Substack, author self-discloses **long Lumentum, AXTI and AAOI**. Net-new to the theme: (a) the **"80% of InP already goes to AI vs ~25% of DRAM, and InP has no consumer allocation to raid"** framing — the qualitative mechanism behind Citi's 07-16 InP shortage model, and the reason this squeeze should outlast the memory one; (b) an **unverified single-source claim that "Lumentum cut everyone except Nvidia off"** and that **Lumentum, not Ayar Labs, ended that relationship** — logged as a claim to confirm because it would kill the Seivers→Ayar→AMD chain being traded on FinTwit; (c) a modelled **SOA-based ELSFP** alternative for NPO/CPO with **better InP area efficiency but 17% gross margin vs 51% for the conventional 8× discrete DFB build**, buildable only by ex-LiDAR firms (**$AEVA** at **Seivers**, plus one private IDM) — i.e. the shortage has an engineering workaround that is uneconomic, so it does not get designed around cheaply. Also logged the **Zhejiang GaAs/InP substrate project approval** (TrendForce 07-23 via Susquehanna/Hosseini weekly 07-25) against the China-verticalization watch-item, and **Aixtron's Q1'26 order mix (€171m intake, +30% y/y, opto 65% of equipment orders; UBS sees power sales -28% y/y FY26)** as evidence the MOCVD cycle is now almost purely optical.- 2026-07-24 — /run-inbox SemiAnalysis batch (theme-dossier pass). **Added three State-of-play bullets + a Sources entry; all additive, no prior number superseded.** (1) **S10 "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?" (2026-07-24) — the most concrete scale-up-optics datapoint on this page, and it CLOSES the open question the Jefferies 07-16 bullet posed** ("whether AMD commits to a CPO-based scale-up solution and who supplies the optical engine"): **MI500 (2027) pivots to co-packaged copper (CPC) and near-packaged optics (NPO)** with **9 optical engines at 6.4 Tbit/s uni-di each = 57.6T of scale-up bandwidth**, **32 lanes of 200 Gbit/s per engine**, **each lane 8 lambdas (4 Tx / 4 Rx) at 50G NRZ**, and **the light source from ELSFPs also at 6.4 Tbit/s each** — an **external-laser architecture**, the key read for the laser/EML vendors (light source stays pluggable and separately sourced). Topology: flat one-tier scale-up across **576 GPUs / 8 racks** on **288 switches** using a **115.2T Tomahawk 6 SKU** (vs the 102.4T variant in Helios). Trigger mechanism recorded: today's Helios scale-up is **all copper (10,368 differential pairs per rack)** and **MI500 extends the domain to 256 GPUs across three racks "where copper will likely give way to co-packaged copper or co-packaged optics"** — both the 256-GPU/3-rack and 576-GPU/8-rack framings appear in the source and are carried as stated, not reconciled. Read: **the scale-up domain crossing a single rack is the trigger event for optics, and AMD's answer is NPO + external light source rather than full CPO.** (2) **S8 "The Future of Meta Superintelligence" (2026-07-09) — a route the keyword router missed entirely:** the **architectural mechanism** behind the SemiAnalysis scale-across sizing (~$3B 2026 → ~$14B 2028) already on this page — Prometheus as **27 datacenters across 6 campuses** (5 within 6km, the 6th 75-80km away) stitched by **AI-Backbone (AIBB)**: L3 Superspines (BAG) interconnecting up to 5 DSF or 7 NSF scale-out regions, aggregated on a single **L4 Inter-BAG hub at ~22 petabits/s bi-directional**, with **the L3↔L4 connection "a mix of LR optics and DWDM systems with ZR optics, depending on the length of the fiber."** Physics: **1-10µs intra-region but ≥500µs at 100km from light propagation alone**, forcing asynchronous training; and **"the other Titans will go even further… connecting campuses up to 2,000kms apart."** ⚠️ **Explicitly labelled a READ-THROUGH, not a company datapoint** — the implication is materially more coherent DWDM/ZR and long-reach content per GW, but **the source names no optical vendor and gives no port counts**, so no supplier attribution is drawn. (3) **S5 SMIC/Kirin teardown (2026-06-14), one line** — an existence proof for the CPO timing debate: SemiAnalysis's teardown lab has **already reverse-engineered "a major TSMC customer's COUPE CPO optical engine + EIC 3D stack,"** i.e. COUPE-based engines are shipping in enough volume for a commercial teardown to be worth funding (customer unnamed).
